If the device is lost, revoke all three: the Google logins (Google Account → Security, the access of Google Cloud SDK and Google Auth Library; `gcloud auth revoke --all` on the device itself when it is retired), GitHub → Settings → Applications (GitHub CLI) as Pulse4all-DEV, and HubSpot → Personal access key in the test account.
  - The Linux machine is not used for prod work (Martin, 16 October 2026): terminal work against prod (`p4a-cma-prod`, `cma-prod-pg`) runs from the Chromebook only.
  - No service-account keys and no secrets in local files: a secret comes from Secret Manager at run time (`gcloud secrets versions access`, or `put_secret` for a new value) and is never written to disk.
- The web app is reachable only through the load balancer and IAP; the Cloud Run address refuses direct traffic. Team access during the build through `cma@pulse4all.com`, agents through their pulse4all.com account plus an active `app_user` row
- A budget alert per project emails the billing admins (dev: €75 per month, prod: €150 per month).

### Data model: time model (migration 0002)

Designed and run on 5 October 2026 as the first half of Roadmap step 2; verified in dev (blocks A to K) and prod (A, B, C, D, F, G, I, J) the same day, records in `records/0002-dev-2026-10-05` and `records/0002-prod-2026-10-05`. Universal, no customer specifics; tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL.

**Tables**

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `work_status` | tenant | the configurable status list (available, training, meeting, break, lunch by default), each with semantic flags `is_working` (the clock runs), `is_productive`, `is_paid`, `is_billable`, one `is_default` per tenant. Seeded for every tenant by `cma.seed_default_work_statuses()`, which `create_tenant` now calls; adjustable per tenant (Pulse4all Subscriptions' own list since 7 October 2026, see Display rules) |
| `workday` | tenant | one row per user per business day in the user's zone, with the zone snapshotted at open. A header: `status`, `started_at` and `ended_at` are derived from the events by `cma.refresh_workday()`, never set by hand |
| `time_event` | tenant | the facts: `start`, `status`, `end`, append-only, with `occurred_at` and `recorded_at`. `source` is `user`, `system` (scheduler) or `correction`, and since addition 0008d `import` (an earlier system's sessions, with their batch). A correction is a new row with `reason` and `approved_by`; it adds a missing event, replaces one (`supersedes_event_id`) or cancels one (kind `void`). One correction per event; a later correction supersedes the earlier correction |

Time zones: `app_user.timezone`, then `organisation.timezone`, then `tenant.timezone` (`cma.user_timezone()`); all three validated against the IANA names Postgres knows. Newco is `Europe/Madrid` in the Pulse4all seed.

**The write path.** The application never inserts into these tables. It calls `cma.open_workday()`, `cma.set_status()`, `cma.end_workday()` and `cma.correct_time_event()` as `cma_app` with `app.tenant_id` and `app.user_id` set; the same functions serve the scheduler (source `system`, `app.actor_label` set, no user) and any later tool. Rules enforced there: a user opens and ends only their own day; today's day is returned if it exists, open or ended (an ended day stays ended); a correction needs a reason, an approver, and `workday.team` for both the acting user and the approver; the resulting day must have exactly one start, at most one end and nothing after the end. Errors carry stable SQLSTATEs `CMA01` (no acting user) to `CMA05` (tenant configuration missing) so the API maps codes, not text. `cma_app` has no `update` or `delete` on `time_event` and no `delete` on `workday` and `work_status`. Migration 0003 sharpens the correction rules (next section).

**Views.** Each computation exists once as an unfiltered core view owned by `cma_owner` and granted to nobody (`time_event_effective_all`, `time_interval_all`, `workday_summary_all`), with two faces: `cma.<name>` for the application, filtered on the current tenant and empty when none is set, and `cma_read.<name>` for readers, filtered with `reader_sees()`. `workday_summary` gives working, productive, paid and billable seconds per day plus `is_capped`, `needs_correction` and `has_correction`. An open day that was never ended keeps running until the end of its business day, is capped there and flagged; a manager closes it through a correction, later the scheduler does. Reporting views added: `work_status`, `workday`, `time_event` (including superseded rows and corrections), `time_interval`, `workday_summary`; `organisation` and `app_user` gained `timezone`.

**Identity helpers.** `cma.find_tenants_for_identity(system, external_id)`, the one `SECURITY DEFINER` function, returns tenant ids only and is what the login calls before a tenant is known; `cma.user_permissions()` and `cma.has_permission()` resolve the role ladder for the API (scopes are ignored until teams and markets exist).

**Display rules taken from the demo (6 October 2026, Martin decides):** a status's colour is derived from its flags, never stored; since 7 October 2026 (Martin) four groups: working and productive → Leaf Green, working but not productive → Deep Blue, not working and paid → Light Blue, not working and unpaid → Soft Rose, always with the label; on My day the two pauses share Light Blue, because whether a pause is paid is not agent information; the shift belongs to the business day it started on; a change to an end time afterwards is always visible to the agent and the manager; Pulse4all Subscriptions' own list is seed data in `02_seed_pulse4all.sql` (decided and run 7 October 2026, Martin): Available - Sales (the default) and Available - Operations, both working, productive, paid and billable, so availability hours report per work type from the first day; Training, Meeting and Coaching working, not productive, paid and billable; Break and Lunch not working, unpaid and not billable, with time on another project booked as Break; the universal `available` retired (inactive, history keeps its stretches); the demo's other eight statuses dropped. Invest keeps the universal ladder until its own list is decided (Roadmap step 10).

### Data model: corrections (migration 0003)

Designed and run on 6 October 2026 as increment c1 of Roadmap step 2; verified in dev and prod the same day, records in `records/0003-prod-2026-10-06` (a screenshot) and `records/corrections-dev-2026-10-06`; the dev verify output of `09` was not kept. Universal; no new tables, only functions. Tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL.

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.correct_workday(user, date, changes, reason)` | one edit of one person's day in one transaction: the changes (`start`, `status`, `end`, `void`, each with `at`, `statusKey`, `supersedes`) are written in order and the day is validated once at the end, so an edit may pass through states that are invalid on their own. On a date without a day it creates the day (Add day); the first change must then be its start. Returns the day |
| `cma.team_hours(from, to, user?)` | hours per person per day with employer, zone, working and paid seconds and the `is_capped`, `needs_correction`, `has_correction` flags; at most 92 days |
| `cma.team_day(user, date)` | every event of one day, effective or not, with reason and approver, for the day editor |
| `cma.assert_permission(key)` | the shared check: the acting user, active in the tenant, holding the permission; `CMA01` or `CMA06` otherwise |

Rules, enforced in the functions: the person correcting holds `workday.team` and is the approver (V1); nobody corrects their own day, managers included; a reason of 3 to 500 characters on every row of the edit; every time lies within the corrected business day in the day's zone, not in the future, and carries an explicit offset (`Z` or `+hh:mm`), because one local hour occurs twice at the autumn clock change; `refresh_workday` now also refuses a status or end before the start. `correct_time_event` keeps its signature for SQL tools, raises `CMA06` instead of `CMA04` for a missing permission, refuses one's own day and accepts only the acting user as approver. New SQLSTATE `CMA06`: not permitted. The team reads check `workday.team` inside the function, in the same transaction as the read; until teams exist (migration 0004) the scope is the whole tenant.

**Addition 0003a (6 October 2026, increment c2).** `cma.team_people()` lists the people whose time is kept (active, holding `workday.own`) with employer and zone, for people holding `workday.team`, checked inside the function like the team reads. Add day and the person filter need it: `team_hours` only knows people who have a day, and Add day exists for someone who never clocked in. Scripts `db/11_team_people.sql` and `db/12_verify_team_people.sql`; verified in dev (blocks A to D, 4 people) and prod (A and E, 2 people) the same day. Numbered 0003a so the planned migrations keep 0004 to 0009. The verify blocks switch role first (`cma_owner` to read, `cma_app` to act as the app), because a personal login has no direct rights on schema `cma`; Studio shows one result per statement, and the verdict is the last one.

### Data model: tenant settings and exports (migration 0003b)

Designed and run on 6 October 2026 as the database half of increment c3 of Roadmap step 2; verified in dev and prod the same day. Those verdicts were never committed, so the verify was rerun on 7 October 2026 through the Auth Proxy, normal and provoked, in both instances (`records/0003b-dev-2026-10-07`, `records/0003b-prod-2026-10-07`, PR #27): blocks A, B and C pass, and fail with `FAIL A2`, `FAIL B5` and `FAIL C1` when provoked. The closing summary rows read PASS in a provoked run because they only show the stored values; Studio and `ON_ERROR_STOP` never reach them after a failure. Universal. Numbered 0003b so 0004 to 0009 stay.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.setting` | global catalog of the settings the code may read: key, type (`text`, `boolean`, `integer`), allowed values, a universal default and a description. Changed only by migrations, like `cma.permission`; `cma_app` reads it, never writes it |
| `cma.tenant_setting` | a tenant's own value per setting; no row means the catalog default. Tenant-scoped through `setup_tenant_table` (RLS, audit trigger); a trigger validates every write against the catalog (`CMA02` unknown key, `CMA04` invalid value), whatever path writes it |
| `cma.tenant_settings()` | the effective settings of the current tenant (own value or default, with `is_default`); no tenant set returns nothing. No permission: formats are not personal data |
| `cma.set_tenant_setting(key, value)` | writes one value for the current tenant, `null` resets it to the default; needs `tenant.configure`. The configuration screens of step 5 will use it |
| `cma.export_hours(from, to, user?)` | the rows of `team_hours` plus productive and billable seconds and the employer key; needs `workday.export`; at most 92 days |
| `cma.export_status_changes(from, to, user?)` | one row per effective stretch in a status (`cma.time_interval`): person, employer, business date, zone, status key and name, its four flags, from, to (empty while open), capped, seconds and the source of the event that began it; needs `workday.export`; at most 92 days |
| `workday.export` | new permission: download hours and status changes as files. On the default `manager` role only, for existing tenants and through `seed_default_roles` for new ones |
| `cma_read.setting`, `cma_read.tenant_setting` | reporting views |

The first five settings, all for CSV exports, use readable tokens rather than literal characters: `export.csv.separator` (`comma`, `semicolon`, `tab`; default `comma`), `export.csv.decimal_mark` (`point`, `comma`; default `point`), `export.csv.date_format` (`yyyy-mm-dd`, `dd-mm-yyyy`, `dd/mm/yyyy`, `mm/dd/yyyy`; default `yyyy-mm-dd`), `export.csv.duration_format` (`decimal_hours`, `hh:mm`, `minutes`; default `decimal_hours`) and `export.csv.utf8_bom` (default `true`, so spreadsheet programs read accented names correctly). Pulse4all's values are set in `02_seed_pulse4all.sql` (semicolon, decimal comma, `dd-mm-yyyy` on both tenants), only where a tenant has no own value, so a later change on the configuration screen survives a rerun; the duration format stays the default until the Steam sample is compared. The verify proves structure and privileges, that an agent cannot change a setting and a manager can, every refusal, that another tenant does not see the value, the reset, the audit rows with their actor, and for the exports that an agent and a supervisor with only `workday.team` are refused, that the manager gets the open day and its open stretch, and the 92-day bound.

### Data model: time per status (addition 0003c)

Designed and run on 7 October 2026 as the database half of increment d of Roadmap step 2 (the Dashboard); verified in dev and prod the same day, normal and provoked, through the Auth Proxy (`records/0003c-dev-2026-10-07`, `records/0003c-prod-2026-10-07`). Universal; no table and no permission, only a function. Tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL, with four deliberate breakages each caught by the verify.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.team_status_time(from, to, user?)` | seconds per person per business date per status, summed from `cma.time_interval`: person, employer key and name, date, zone, status key, name, sort order, whether the status is still active, its four flags, seconds, stretches and whether a stretch was capped; needs `performance.team` inside the function (`CMA06`), at most 92 days (`CMA04`); runs with the caller's rights; `cma_app` may execute it, readers and `public` may not |

Because it reads the same stretches as `workday_summary`, the working and paid seconds of a person's day equal Team hours by construction. A status set inactive since stays in history; flags are returned as stored, so a flag change shows at once and every group and colour is derived from flags. `performance.team` rather than `workday.team`: supervisor, manager and analytics hold it in the default ladder since 0001, so analytics reads the Dashboard without being able to correct anything. The verify covers structure and privileges (block A) and, on a throwaway tenant with its own four statuses, exact seconds and stretches for a day with every kind of status, the agent refused, analytics allowed without `workday.team`, equality with `workday_summary` per person-day, the person filter, a flag change, an inactive status, the 92-day bound and both refusals of a broken range, today's open stretch, another tenant, and no acting user (block B).

### Data model: clock in and app links (addition 0003d)

Designed and run on 7 October 2026 as the database half of increment e1 of Roadmap step 2 (the Welcome page with an explicit Clock in); verified in dev and prod the same day, normal and provoked, through the Auth Proxy (`records/0003d-dev-2026-10-07`, `records/0003d-prod-2026-10-07`); in dev `07`, `10` and `16` were rerun after it with identical results. Universal. Tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL, with four deliberate breakages (guard disabled, the app allowed to write links, the permission filter dropped, the guard without the permission) each caught by the verify.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.time_is_kept(user)` | one definition of "this person's time is kept": active in the current tenant and holding `workday.own`. `team_people()` lists exactly these people (as since 0003a, now on this function) |
| `cma.assert_time_kept(user)` | raises `CMA06` when it is not |
| `cma.open_workday()` | creates a day only for an acting user whose time is kept; an existing day, open or ended, is still returned unchanged, also for someone who lost the clock during the day |
| `cma.correct_workday()` | Add day (a correction on a date without a day) creates a day only for a target person whose time is kept; editing an existing day is unguarded, so a leaver's last days can still be corrected after their role is gone |
| `cma.app_link` | buttons to other applications on the Welcome page, per tenant: `key`, `label`, `address` (https only, checked by constraint), optional `permission_key` (foreign key to the catalog; the link is shown only to people holding it, null means everyone with a role), `sort_order`, `status`. RLS and audit through `setup_tenant_table`; `cma_app` reads only, writes come with the configuration screens of step 5. Deep links only, never customer data; no address lives in code |
| `cma.app_links()` | the active links the acting user may see, in order; `CMA01` without an acting user; `cma_app` may execute it, readers and `public` may not |
| `cma_read.app_link` | reporting view |

The guard sits where a day is created, not where it is edited (Martin, 7 October 2026). Pulse4all's links are seed data in `02` (HubSpot and Aircall on both tenants, generic addresses until the exact portal addresses are provided, no permission), added only where a tenant has no link with that key, so a later change on the configuration screen survives a rerun. The verify proves structure and privileges (the table's RLS, policy and audit trigger, the app's read-only rights, the readers' view, the function's grants) and, on throwaway tenants, that an analyst and a person without a role cannot open a day, that an agent opens today and a second call returns that day unchanged, Add day refused and allowed, `team_people`, a leaver's existing day still correctable and no new day, the links per person in order with the inactive link for nobody, the app unable to write, another tenant, and no acting user.

### Data model: team now (addition 0003e)

Designed and run on 7 October 2026 as the database half of increment (b) of Roadmap step 2 (the Live board); verified in dev and prod the same day, normal and provoked, through the Auth Proxy (`records/0003e-dev-2026-10-07`, `records/0003e-prod-2026-10-07`). Universal. Tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.team_now()` | one row per person whose time is kept (`time_is_kept`, the same people as `team_people`), with employer key and name, zone, today in that zone, and today's day if there is one: `workday_id`, `day_status` (`open`, `ended`), `started_at`, `ended_at`, the current (or, once ended, last) status with key, name, active flag and the four flags, and the clock's inputs exactly as the own-day read computes them: `status_since`, `closed_seconds`, `running_since`. `monitoring.live` checked inside (`CMA06`), `CMA01` without an acting user; runs with the caller's rights, stable; `cma_app` may execute it, readers and `public` may not. Tenant-wide until teams exist (0004) |

Stable inputs, no computed figure (Martin, 7 October 2026): the function never returns "worked so far", so two reads of an unchanged team are equal and the screen ticks on its own. Today only: an earlier day still open belongs to the Welcome to-do line and Team hours, not to the board; and today's day can never be past its business-day end (the business day ends at the next midnight in the person's zone), so the board carries no stale flag. The open decision on shifts past midnight may change what "today" means, not this function. The verify proves structure and privileges (recorded, exists, the exact column list, grants, caller's rights, stable) and, on throwaway tenants, that an agent and a person without a role are refused, the supervisor and the analyst read exactly the people whose time is kept (the analyst not among them), a person's row after a pause (the closed seconds equal the first stretch exactly, nothing running, since the pause), a person in the default status (running since the start), two people without a day, two reads equal, an ended day (nothing since or running, closed seconds to the end), tenant two sees only its own people, no acting user refused.

### Data model: teams and skills (migration 0004)

Written in the night build of 7 to 8 October 2026 (slice 1), as the database half of the first slice of Roadmap step 5; run and verified on a local PostgreSQL 18.6 in the night (`21_teams_skills.sql`, `22_verify_teams_skills.sql`, both blocks pass and fail under provoke), in dev and prod on 8 October 2026 through the runbook (`records/0004-dev-2026-10-08`, `records/0004-prod-2026-10-08`). Universal: teams, skills, level scales and the ladder's wording are tenant rows; Pulse4all's teams and skills are seed data in `02`.

**Tables**

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `team` | tenant | membership groups with `key`, `name`, `markets` (lowercase tokens, ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 by convention), `sort_order`, `valid_from` and `valid_to`; a dissolved team keeps its row and its history |
| `team_member` | tenant | one row per person per team per stretch of membership, `valid_from` and `valid_to` as half-open `timestamptz`; a change ends the row and starts a new one, never overwrites |
| `skill` | tenant | the catalog per `dimension` (`language`, `work_type`, `channel`) with `key`, `name`, `sort_order`, `status`; channel is a dimension a tenant may leave empty |
| `skill_level` | tenant | the level scale per dimension: `level` (1 upwards) and `name`; a dimension without rows is binary (held or not). Seeded for every tenant by `cma.seed_default_skill_levels()`, which `create_tenant` calls: `language` 1 Basic, 2 Good, 3 Fluent, 4 Native; `work_type` and `channel` binary |
| `user_skill` | tenant | one row per person per skill per stretch, `level` (null for a binary dimension, validated by trigger against the scale), `valid_from` and `valid_to` as for memberships |

**The ladder.** `admin` joins the default ladder as a superset of the manager (Martin, 7 October 2026): everything the manager has plus `tenant.configure`, `users.manage_all`, connections and API keys later. `users.manage` is split into `users.manage_agents` (the manager: people whose role holds none of `users.manage_agents`, `users.manage_all` or `tenant.configure`, and only such roles may be assigned) and `users.manage_all` (the admin: everyone, every role); `tenant.configure` leaves the manager row. `seed_default_roles` carries the new ladder for new tenants; existing tenants get `admin` added and their system `manager` row changed in place, rerun-safe, and the catalog loses `users.manage`. `cma.role_is_managing(role)` is the one definition of a managing role, so the rule is derived from permissions, never from a role key. A person holds one role; nobody changes their own role or deactivates themselves (`CMA06`).

**Functions (the application only, every one checking its permission inside)**

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.directory()` | the people with employer, zone, role (the earliest grant, as the login shows it), whether the role is managing, whether the clock runs (`time_is_kept`), current teams and skills as JSON, and `may_edit`; `users.manage_all` lists everyone, `users.manage_agents` the non-managing people and the caller |
| `cma.roles()`, `cma.teams()`, `cma.skills()`, `cma.organisations()` | the ladder with `assignable` per role for the caller, the current teams with member counts, the catalog with its scale per dimension, the active employers with the zone a new person follows; the last three for any active person of the tenant |
| `cma.team_members_now()` | current memberships for the Live board's team filter; needs a watching or managing permission (`monitoring.live`, `workday.team`, `roster.manage`, `users.manage_*`) |
| `cma.add_person(email, name, employer key, role key, login system, login id, zone)` | user, login id and role in one statement (a refusal writes nothing); rerun-safe for the same person and id; a different id for an existing person, an id of another person or an inactive person is `CMA03`; the login id is stored as given, which form it has is the identity provider's business. Replaces `db/people_add_prod.template.sql` |
| `cma.set_person_role()`, `cma.set_person_active()`, `cma.set_person_teams()`, `cma.set_person_skills()` | one role per person; deactivate or reactivate; the full list of teams or skills with levels (what is not in it ends, a changed level ends the row and starts a new one); skills need `skills.manage` as well |
| `cma.upsert_team()`, `cma.dissolve_team()`, `cma.upsert_skill()`, `cma.set_skill_levels()` | the catalogs, for `tenant.configure`; the configuration screens of step 5 will call them |
| `cma_read.team`, `team_member`, `skill`, `skill_level`, `user_skill` | reporting views, so as-of questions (who was in which team on a date) can be answered |

The verify (`22`) proves structure and privileges (RLS, audit trigger and the app's rights on every table, the functions' grants, the ladder per tenant) and, on throwaway tenants, the manager's and the admin's scope, every refusal (own role, a managing role from a manager, the last admin, an unknown team, a level outside the scale), the validity chaining of memberships and skills, the directory's JSON, the catalog functions, tenant separation and the missing acting user.

### Data model: roster (migration 0005)

Written in the night build of 7 to 8 October 2026 (slice 3); run and verified locally in the night (`23_roster.sql`, `25_verify_roster.sql`, blocks A and B1 to B13), in dev and prod on 8 October 2026 through the runbook (`records/0005-dev-2026-10-08`, `records/0005-prod-2026-10-08`); the dev fixture `24_seed_dev_roster.sql` writes this week's published rosters and next week's draft through the functions as the test manager. Universal: absence types and coverage targets are tenant rows, the adherence tolerance a tenant setting.

**Tables**

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `absence_type` | tenant | the absence types with `key`, `name`, `is_paid`, `sort_order`, `status`; seeded by `cma.seed_default_absence_types()` (which `create_tenant` calls): `off` (a planned day off), `leave`, `sick`, `public_holiday`, `other`. Nothing medical beyond the word; no reasons are stored |
| `roster_week` | tenant | one roster per week per team, or for the whole tenant when `team_id` is null; `week_start` a Monday; `status` draft until first published, then published; `version` counts the publications; `published_at` and `published_by` the latest |
| `roster_publication` | tenant | every publish with its instant and person, insert-only, so what agents saw at any time is reconstructable from the versioned entries |
| `roster_entry` | tenant | one cell, versioned: `kind` shift (`start_time` and `end_time` local in the person's zone, within the business day, so 00:00 to 24:00 and never across midnight, README decision of 7 October 2026) or absence (`absence_type_id`), `note` (at most 200 characters), `recorded_at` and `recorded_by`; a change ends the row (`valid_to`, `superseded_by`) and inserts a new one, a clear ends it. At most one current entry per person per business date across every roster of the tenant (a partial unique index), so two team rosters cannot both plan the same person on a day |
| `coverage_target` | tenant | how many people holding a work type a team wants on a weekday; the planner shows planned against target |

The versioning instants (`recorded_at`, `valid_to`, `published_at`) are `clock_timestamp()`, not `now()`, so a publish and an edit in one transaction still order (the verify's B6 caught the difference).

**Setting.** `roster.adherence_tolerance_minutes` (integer, default 5): how late a clock-in or how early a clock-out may be before the Live board flags it.

**Functions (the application only, every one checking its permission inside)**

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.roster_week(week, team?)`, `roster_people()`, `roster_entries()`, `roster_coverage()`, `roster_weeks(team, from, to)`, `coverage_targets(team)` | the planner's reads (`roster.manage`): the week's header (state, version, publication, entry count, `changed_since_publish`), the people on the grid (whose time is kept and, for a team roster, who is a member during the week, with their work types), the current cells, people on shift per work type per day with the target, the weeks of a range as a calendar with state and counts |
| `cma.roster_set_entry(week, team?, person, date, kind, start, end, absence key, note)` | one cell: a new version, the old one ended; `kind` null clears. The past for that person (today in their zone is open), a date outside the week, a person not on the grid and bad times are `CMA04`; another roster's entry on that date `CMA03`; an unknown absence type `CMA02` |
| `cma.roster_publish(week, team?)` | publishes the week as it stands: version + 1, a publication row; a week never written is created and published empty |
| `cma.roster_copy_week(from, to, team?)` | copies the current cells of one week into another of the same scope: a source cell overwrites the target cell, an empty one leaves it alone, the past and people no longer on the grid are skipped |
| `cma.my_roster(from, to)` | the caller's own schedule (`roster.view`), one row per date, published entries only as of each week's latest publication, with `is_published` saying whether a published roster covers the person that day at all; at most 92 days |
| `cma.roster_today()` | today's published entry per person whose time is kept, in each person's zone, for the Live board (`monitoring.live`) |
| `cma.absence_types()`, `cma.upsert_absence_type()`, `cma.set_coverage_target()` | the catalogs: read by any active person, written with `tenant.configure` and `roster.manage` |
| `cma.roster_published_entry` | the entries as agents see them (as of each week's latest publication), an owner-only core with a tenant face and a reader face; `cma_read` views for every table |

Published means visible: agents and the Live board read the state as of the week's last publication; the planner reads the current rows and shows "changed since the last publish" until the next one. The verify (`25`) proves structure and privileges and, on throwaway tenants, the grid per scope, writes and reads of shifts and absences, every refusal, publish and the agent's view, an edit after a publish (the planner sees it, the agent does not, the header says so, the cell has two versions, a second publish shows it), the one-entry-per-date rule across rosters, today's shift for the board, coverage with a target, copying a week, the week list, the absence catalog, tenant separation and the missing acting user.

### Data model: configuration, the scheduler user and the forgotten-day close (migration 0005a)

Written in the night build of 8 to 9 October 2026 (slice 1); run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.6 (`26_configuration.sql`, `27_verify_configuration.sql`, blocks A and B1 to B8, passing and failing under provoke), in dev and prod on the morning of 9 October through the runbook (`records/0005a-dev-2026-10-09`, `records/0005a-prod-2026-10-09`). Universal: no new tables; functions for the configuration screens, two columns, a trigger, one setting, one permission and one system user per tenant. Numbered 0005a so 0006 to 0009 stay.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.upsert_work_status(key, name, is_working, is_productive, is_paid, is_billable, sort_order)`, `set_default_work_status(key)`, `retire_work_status(key)`, `work_statuses_all()` | the status list for `tenant.configure`: add, rename, reorder, reactivate a retired key, change the default, retire. A status in use is retired, never deleted (its history and usage count stay; it cannot be chosen); the default must be an active working status and there is exactly one; the last working status cannot be retired; **the four flags of a status that has any time event behind it cannot change** (`CMA03`: retire it and add a new status), so no pay or billing history is rewritten by a click. `cma_app` writes the table only through these functions, never deletes |
| `cma.upsert_app_link(key, label, address, permission_key?, sort_order)`, `retire_app_link(key)`, `app_links_all()` | the Welcome buttons for `tenant.configure`: https only, a permission from the catalog or null; retire and reactivate. `cma_app` may now insert and update `app_link` (verify `18` A5 accepts this once 0005a is recorded), never delete |
| `cma.retire_absence_type(key)`, `cma.clear_coverage_target(team, skill, weekday)`, `cma.coverage_targets_all()` | the missing halves of 0005's catalog functions; `coverage_targets_all()` lists every target across teams for the configuration grid |
| `app_user.kind` (`person`, `system`), `app_role.is_assignable` | a system user is a process the audit must name; a non-assignable role is never offered to people. One trigger `protect_system` on `app_user`, `user_role`, `team_member` and `user_skill` refuses the application (anyone but `cma_owner`) creating, editing, role-assigning or teaming a system user, or granting a non-assignable role (`CMA06`). `cma.roles()` lists assignable roles only, `cma.directory()` people only; `cma_read.app_user` gains `kind`. The README's worker kind for AI agents joins `kind` later |
| `cma.ensure_scheduler_user(tenant)` | one **Scheduler** system user per tenant (`scheduler@system.invalid`, login system and id `scheduler`, no clock) holding the system role `scheduler`, which is not assignable and holds only the new permission `workday.close_forgotten` (in no default role). Run for every existing tenant and by `create_tenant` for new ones |
| `cma.close_forgotten_workdays(p_grace_minutes?)` | ends every open workday of the current tenant whose business day ended more than the grace ago (the parameter, else the setting `workday.auto_close_grace_minutes`, default 120) with an `end` event at the business day's end (or the day's last event when that is later), `source = 'system'`, the scheduler as the acting user and `app.actor_label = 'scheduler'` (set by the function when none is), so the audit names it. Needs `workday.close_forgotten`; returns what it closed; idempotent |
| `cma.workday_summary_all` | as 0002 with one change: `needs_correction` is true while the day is open past its end **or** its effective end event is a system end, so a scheduler-closed day stays on Team hours' to-do list and Welcome's line until a manager's correction supersedes the end (confirming the time is a correction with the same instant). Same columns, so every face and function above it is untouched |
| `workday.auto_close_grace_minutes` | integer setting, default 120, shown on the Exports page |

The verify (`27`) proves structure and privileges (the columns, the permission in no assignable role, the setting, the trigger on the four tables, every function's grants, the app's write rights on `work_status` and `app_link` without delete, the summary view's columns) and, per tenant, exactly one active scheduler with its login and no clock; and on two throwaway tenants in two zones the scheduler hidden from the directory and the ladder, every refusal on it, the close of a forgotten day at its business day's end with source `system` and the audit naming the scheduler, the flag that stays until a manager's correction, a normal day untouched, a second run that finds nothing, the grace (today never closes, a negative grace refused), tenant separation (one tenant's scheduler is `CMA01` in another), every status rule (frozen flags, the default, the last working status, reactivation, a retired status not choosable), the app links, absence types and coverage targets, and the missing acting user.

### Data model: my profile (addition 0005b)

Built and verified on 9 October 2026 (slice 4(a) of the night build's handover): locally on PostgreSQL 18.6, then in dev and prod in Cloud SQL Studio before the web change was merged (`records/account-2026-10-09`). Universal: one function, no table.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.my_profile()` | exactly one row, the acting person's own: name, email, employer, time zone (`user_timezone`), the role (the first granted, as `directory()`), whether their time is kept (`time_is_kept`), current teams and current skills with level name, both exactly as `directory()` defines them so a person sees what their manager sees. Takes no user id, so nobody can ask for another person's row; needs no permission beyond being an active person (`kind = 'person'`) of the current tenant: the Scheduler and inactive people are refused with `CMA01`. Runs with the caller's rights, stable, executable by `cma_app` only. Ids in other systems are left out on purpose (Open decisions) |

The verify (`29`) proves structure and privileges (the exact column list, no argument, `cma_app` only, caller's rights, stable) and, on two throwaway tenants, the agent's own row with the employer's zone, current teams only (not an ended membership, not a dissolved team) in team order, current skills only with the level's name, the supervisor reading only their own row, a person without a role, an inactive person, the Scheduler, another tenant's context and a missing acting user refused.

### Data model: ingest and CRM records (migration 0006)

Written on 9 October 2026 as the database half of speed-to-lead slice S1a; run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.6 (`30_ingest_crm_records.sql` twice, `31_verify_ingest_crm_records.sql` passing and failing under provoke, three mutations caught, every earlier verify unchanged apart from `27`, adjusted with it); dev and prod follow through the release flow. Universal: the adapter is a data value, nothing reads a vendor payload; the ingest API maps a source's events and records to the canonical shapes below.

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `integration_connection` | tenant | one adapter instance per tenant and source account: `adapter` (data value), `external_account_id` (the portal id), an opaque `key` for the webhook address (unique across tenants, not a secret), the **names** of the signing and token secrets in Secret Manager, status |
| `connection_field` | tenant | which source property feeds which canonical field: records (deal, ticket, …) take `market` and `language`, contacts take `country`, `language` and one `ref` per other system (for example the commerce system's customer id) |
| `connection_pipeline` | tenant | source pipelines per record type with label, `is_counted` (test pipelines out of reporting; a pipeline not yet seen counts), `is_routed` (S3) and the work type skill (S2) |
| `connection_stage` | tenant | stages per pipeline with label, order and the source's own closed flag, refreshed from the source; a stage the source dropped is archived |
| `ingest_event` | tenant | one row per received event, unique on the adapter's event key: source type, canonical kind (created, changed, deleted, merged, restored, associated), object type and id, property, `occurred_at`, `recorded_at`, the event as received (ids only), status (received, processed, ignored, failed, needs_review), attempts, `next_attempt_at`, an error code |
| `crm_record` | tenant | thin mirror of deals and tickets: `source_system`, record type, source id, pipeline, stage, `owner_ref` (the source's owner id), `contact_source_id`, market, language, `is_closed` (from the stage, null while the stage is unknown), `closed_at`, source created, updated and deleted times, `synced_at`, raw (the requested properties only) |
| `crm_contact`, `crm_contact_ref` | tenant | the contacts records point to: country and language, and their ids in other systems; a contact is taken in only while a record of the same connection points to it, so the contact base is never mirrored; a deleted contact keeps its id and loses its attributes and refs |

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.ingest_connection(key)` | the ingest API's first call, before a tenant is known: tenant, connection, adapter, portal id, secret names and the tenant's Ingest user; nothing for an unknown key or an inactive connection, tenant or user. `SECURITY DEFINER` owned by `cma_owner`, pinned search path, like `find_tenants_for_identity` |
| `cma.ingest_record_events(connection, events)` | records a batch (at most 500) of canonical events; a known key is not written again; answers every key with its id, whether it was new and its status |
| `cma.ingest_claim_events(connection, ids?, limit?)` | claims due events with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, counts an attempt and moves `next_attempt_at` forward (1, 2, 4, … minutes, at most 60), so a dead worker's claim comes back |
| `cma.ingest_finish_events(connection, ids, outcome, error?)` | processed, ignored or failed; a failure at `ingest.max_attempts` (setting, default 10) parks the event as `needs_review`; a finished event never reopens |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_records(connection, records)`, `cma.ingest_upsert_contacts(connection, contacts)` | the mirror writes, guarded by the source's updated time so an older read never overwrites a newer one (stale); deletion marks a record deleted, `closed_at` is the source's close time or the first time the CMA saw the record closed |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_pipelines(connection, pipelines)` | refreshes pipelines and stages from the source, keeping counted, routed and work type; records take their stage's closed flag |
| `cma.ingest_records_without_contact(connection, max age?)` | records still without a contact, for the sweeper's second read |
| `cma.upsert_connection()`, `set_connection_status()`, `set_connection_field()`, `set_connection_pipeline()`, `connections_all()` | configuration for `tenant.configure` (the admin), `connections_all()` with events in the last 24 hours, waiting and parked; the configuration screen comes later, Studio until then |
| `cma.connection_config(connection)` | the mapping as JSON, for the Ingest user or a configuring person |
| `cma.crm_records_per_day(from, to)` | the Dashboard read for `performance.team`: records created per business date in the tenant's zone, record type, market and pipeline, with how many are closed now; counted pipelines only, deleted records left out, at most 92 days |
| `cma.ensure_ingest_user(tenant)` | one **Ingest** system user per tenant (`ingest@system.invalid`, login system and id `ingest`, no clock) holding the non-assignable system role `ingest` with the one permission `ingest.write`; run for every tenant and by `create_tenant` |

The application never deletes from these tables (the mapping rows and contact refs excepted); reporting views in `cma_read` leave out raw payloads, the connection key and the secret names. The verify (`31`) proves structure and privileges (RLS, policies, audit, no delete, the grants, the one definer function with its search path, the Ingest user per tenant, the views' columns, the setting) and, on two throwaway tenants, configuration and its refusals, the lookup by key, idempotent events, claim and backoff, parking after the maximum, pipelines and stages, records with stale, closed, deleted and unknown, a pipeline refresh closing a record, contacts with not held, stale, refs and deletion, records without a contact, the Dashboard read with counting and its bounds, tenant isolation, the Ingest user out of the application's reach and the missing acting user.

### Data model: intake core (migration 0007)

Written on 10 October 2026 as intake track I1 from `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md` §3 and §4.1 (brief B1); run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 (`32_intake_core.sql` twice, `33_verify_intake_core.sql` passing and failing under provoke, twelve mutations caught, every earlier verify unchanged, outputs in `docs/night-2026-10-10/local-records/0007/`); run in dev and prod on 10 October 2026 (`records/0007-dev-2026-10-10`, `records/0007-prod-2026-10-10`). Universal: markets, hours, forms and outcomes are tenant configuration, vendors are data values, and nothing personal is stored (no names, emails, phone numbers, addresses or free text; a phone number exists only as a keyed hash made by the ingest service).

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `market` | tenant | the countries a tenant serves: ISO 3166-1 code in upper case (`GB`, never `UK`), name, IANA zone, ISO 639-1 language, the language skill and minimum level for routing, ISO 4217 currency, status, order |
| `market_alias` | tenant | how sources write a market (`uk`, `united kingdom`, `nederland`), lower-cased, to its code; `cma.normalize_market()` reads it (alias, then a known code, else the value as written) |
| `business_hours`, `business_holiday` | tenant | office hours per market and weekday in local time, `*` as the default; a market with hours of its own uses only those; ranges of a day never overlap; holidays per market, `*` holidays apply everywhere |
| `crm_call` | tenant | call engagements held by the CRM: time, direction, status, outcome, duration, owner, the app that logged it, `counterpart_hash` (HMAC of the other party's number, never the number); stale guard on the source's updated time; a deleted call loses hash and raw |
| `connection_call_outcome` | tenant | the source's call outcomes with `is_connected` (configuration), refreshed from the source |
| `crm_association` | tenant | which call or record belongs to which contact, and which call to which record, in one stored direction; a removal keeps the row with `removed_at`, a re-add clears it, an older change than the stored one is ignored |
| `connection_form`, `form_submission` | tenant | forms with counted, lead source, market and `kept_fields` (field names whose values are not personal); submissions with ids, form, time, page host and path without query string, five UTM tags, the resolved contact id and its resolution, values only of kept fields (filtered again in SQL); insert-only apart from the resolution |
| `sync_cursor`, `sync_run` | tenant | where a poll or backfill stream stands; every backfill, reconcile, poll, subscription check or link run with range, counts and outcome, finished once |

Additive changes to 0006 objects, every 0006 call shape unchanged: `integration_connection.settings` (flat, non-secret, at most 4 kB, keys containing secret, token, password or key refused); `connection_field` with `currency`, `store`, `amount`, `source_channel`, `category` and a `slot` (above 1 for refs only); `crm_contact_ref.slot` (two commerce ids on one contact); `crm_contact.currency` and `store`; `crm_record.amount`, `currency`, `source_channel`, `category`; `connection_pipeline.is_lead`; the record and contact upserts take the new keys and normalise the market and country. Settings: `intake.start_date` (a new value type `date`), `speed_to_lead.pre_window_minutes`, `speed_to_lead.target_minutes`, `lead_to_order.max_days`, `forms.deal_window_hours`, `commerce.renewal_source_names`, `commerce.renewal_app_ids`, `alerts.max_age_minutes`.

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.upsert_market()`, `set_market_status()`, `set_market_alias()`, `remove_market_alias()`, `set_business_hours(market, weekday, '09:00-12:30,13:00-17:00')`, `set_business_holiday()`, `remove_business_holiday()` | configuration for `tenant.configure`; `UK` is refused with "use GB", as are lower-case codes and unknown zones |
| `cma.business_seconds(market, from, to)`, `cma.next_business_noon(market, at)` | office seconds between two instants in the market's zone, holidays out, each day placed in the zone separately (so the 25-hour day of a DST change counts right); 12:00 local on the next working day |
| `cma.set_connection_settings()`, `set_connection_field(…, slot)`, `set_pipeline_lead()`, `set_form()`, `set_call_outcome()` | the connection's configuration (`tenant.configure`); `connection_config()` now also gives slots, `isLead` and the settings |
| `cma.set_user_external_id(user, system, id)`, `remove_user_external_id()` | a person's CRM owner or telephony user id for attribution (`users.manage_all`); the system must read `<system>_owner` or `<system>_user`, so a sign-in id or a system user is never touched |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_calls()`, `ingest_upsert_associations()`, `ingest_upsert_call_outcomes()`, `ingest_upsert_forms()`, `ingest_upsert_form_submissions()`, `ingest_cursor_get()`, `ingest_cursor_set()`, `ingest_sync_run_start()`, `ingest_sync_run_finish()` | the ingest write path (`ingest.write`), at most 500 items per call |
| `cma.ingest_contact_forget(connection, contact)` | privacy deletion: the contact's attributes and refs cleared, the hash cleared on its calls, kept values cleared on its submissions; ids and times stay for counts |

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `market`, `market_alias`, `business_hours`, `business_holiday`, `crm_call` (no raw, no hash), `connection_call_outcome`, `crm_association`, `connection_form`, `form_submission`, `sync_run`; `crm_contact`, `crm_contact_ref` and `connection_pipeline` gain their new columns; the record's new columns sit in `crm_record_detail` (joined on id), because `31` pins the column list of `cma_read.crm_record`. Sync cursors have no view. The application never deletes from the new tables except the mapping rows (aliases, office hours, holidays), and may update a submission only in its resolution and kept values and a run only in its outcome (column privileges). The verify (`33`) proves structure and privileges and, on throwaway tenants, markets and their refusals, alias normalisation, business seconds on a weekday, across a weekend, a holiday and the DST change of 25 October 2026, outside hours, reversed and for an unknown market, the next business noon from a Friday afternoon, the date setting, connection settings refusing secret-looking keys, the field mapping with slots and its refusals, records and contacts with the new fields and two ref slots, calls inserted, updated, stale and deleted, associations added, removed, re-added and an older change ignored, the outcome and form catalogs keeping their configuration, submissions idempotent with kept values filtered, cursors, a run finishing once, the privacy deletion, the Ingest user unable to configure and a configuring person unable to ingest, people's ids and their refusals, and tenant isolation for every new table.

### Data model: telephony (migration 0007a)

Written on 10 October 2026 as intake track I3 from `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md` §4.2 (brief B2). It was run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4: `34_telephony.sql` twice, `35_verify_telephony.sql` passing and failing under provoke, 18 of 19 mutations caught, every earlier verify unchanged. The outputs are in `docs/night-2026-10-10/local-records/0007a/`. Run in dev and prod on 10 October 2026 (`records/0007a-dev-2026-10-10`, `records/0007a-prod-2026-10-10`). Universal: the telephony system is a data value (`source_system`, the connection's adapter) and nothing personal is stored. The other party's number exists only as the keyed hash the ingest service makes (DESIGN §6.3), never shown and never returned by a function; it has three purposes, each inside one tenant: the link to the CRM call that logged the call (0007a), matching commerce orders to earlier calls (0008g) and matching missed inbound calls to later callbacks (0008i). Comments, recordings and voicemail links never arrive, and a line's digits are the company's own number.

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `telephony_call` | tenant | the telephony system's calls: direction (inbound, outbound), status, missed reason, started, answered and ended, duration, `talk_seconds` (derived by the database: ended − answered, null while unanswered), the telephony user and line, archived, `counterpart_hash`; `source_version_at` (the newest event or read time) is the stale guard, so events that arrive out of order end in the right state; a deleted call keeps its id, times and tags and loses hash and raw; raw keeps flat, non-personal keys only (filtered again in SQL) |
| `telephony_tag`, `telephony_call_tag` | tenant | the tag catalog with `is_counted` (configuration); the tag history per call: a row per tagging, closed when the tag leaves the call's tag set, a new row when it comes back |
| `telephony_number` | tenant | the company's own lines: name and E.164 digits from the source, market and `is_counted` as configuration |
| `call_link` | tenant | a telephony call paired with the CRM call that logged it (`hash_time`: equal hash, same direction, at most 120 seconds apart) with the time difference; one current link per side; never rewritten, a wrong link is retired with the person's id and that pair is never linked again |

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.ingest_upsert_telephony_calls(connection, calls)` | the call write (`ingest.write`, at most 500): inserted, updated, stale, deleted or unknown; each call may carry its whole current tag set, and tags are opened and closed by difference at its `source_version_at` (a call without a tag set leaves its tags, an empty set closes them); a tag not yet in the catalog joins it |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_telephony_catalog(connection, kind, items)` | refreshes the `tag` or `number` catalog, keeping `is_counted` and the market; entries missing from the source are archived |
| `cma.set_telephony_number(connection, line, market, counted)`, `cma.set_telephony_tag(connection, tag, counted)` | configuration for `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.link_calls(since)` | pairs telephony calls started since then with CRM calls of the same tenant, nearest first, each side once (partial unique indexes decide, so two sweeps at once cannot double a side); null hashes, deleted calls and retired pairs are left out; `ingest.write` (the sweep) or `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.unlink_call(link)` | retires a wrong link (`tenant.configure`); the next `link_calls` may pair either call with another candidate |
| `cma.ingest_contact_forget(connection, contact)` | as in 0007, and the hash is also cleared on telephony calls linked, now or formerly, to the contact's CRM calls; the answer gains `telephony_calls` when one was cleared |

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `telephony_call` (no raw, no hash), `telephony_call_tag`, `telephony_tag`, `telephony_number`, `call_link`. The application never deletes from these tables. Since addition 0008f a connection stores calls on its counted lines only, and a line switched off loses its calls (Data model: uncounted lines). A tagging changes only in its end and a link only in its retirement, each once (column privileges and a trigger). The verify (`35`) proves structure and privileges and, on throwaway tenants:

- a call written created, answered, ended in order and in reverse (ended first), ending in the same state with the stale guard
- unanswered and archived calls, the raw payload filtered, deletion and restore, refusals that write nothing
- tags closed and reopened by difference, stale reads and reads without tags, the catalogs keeping their configuration
- the link on hash, direction and 120 seconds, nearest first, each side once, null hashes and older calls left out, a second candidate left unlinked, unlink and what the next run links
- the privacy deletion reaching linked and formerly linked telephony calls and leaving the rest
- the Ingest user unable to configure and a configuring person unable to ingest, tenant isolation for every new table, and no deletes

### Data model: commerce (migration 0007b)

Written on 10 October 2026 as intake track I4 from `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md` §4.3 (brief B3); run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 (`36_commerce.sql` twice, `37_verify_commerce.sql` passing and failing under provoke, nineteen mutations caught, every earlier verify unchanged, outputs in `docs/night-2026-10-10/local-records/0007b/`); run in dev and prod on 10 October 2026 (`records/0007b-dev-2026-10-10`, `records/0007b-prod-2026-10-10`). It needs 0007, not 0007a. Universal: a store is a connection with tenant configuration, the commerce vendor is a data value, and nothing personal is stored (no names, emails, phone numbers or addresses of customers; landing and referring pages as host and path only), with one exception since addition 0008g: customers and orders carry `phone_hash`, the keyed hash of the phone number, never the number (Data model: commerce phone hash; `36`'s header comment names it).

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `commerce_store` | tenant | one store per connection: `handle` (lower case, digits and hyphens, unique in the tenant; the value the CRM's store field holds), name, market (from the catalog), currency, time zone |
| `commerce_customer` | tenant | a store's customers: state, locale, lifetime order count, amount spent with its currency, source times; stale guard on the source's updated time; a deletion keeps the id and times and clears state, locale, counts, amounts and raw, and only a read newer than the deletion brings the customer back |
| `commerce_order` | tenant | a store's orders: the shop's order number, customer id, created, processed, updated and cancelled times with the reason, `is_test` (test orders kept and flagged), currency and total, subtotal, tax, discount and refunded amounts, financial and fulfillment status, `source_name` and `app_ref` (the sales channel and app), landing host and path, UTM tags, referring host, order tags (at most 30), `prior_orders_count` with `prior_count_exact`, and `order_kind`; a deletion keeps ids, times, amounts, kind and lines and clears the pages, UTM, tags and raw |
| `commerce_order_line` | tenant | an order's lines: sku, product and variant ids, quantity, unit price, currency; replaced as a set when the order arrives with lines |

`order_kind` is computed in the upsert: `renewal` when the source name (without case or spaces) is in `commerce.renewal_source_names` or the app id in `commerce.renewal_app_ids` (both settings of 0007, empty until the subscription app's orders are named); else `first` when the customer had no earlier order, `repeat` when it had, `unknown` when the count is missing. A change to either setting reclassifies the tenant's orders in the same transaction (a trigger on `tenant_setting`); `cma.reclassify_orders()` reruns it on demand. One customer id in two stores gives two rows, one per connection; the join to the CRM is `crm_contact_ref` with the connection field's ref system and any slot, and a match in two stores is left to the data-quality views of 0007d. Since addition 0008h each customer also carries the CRM contact the ingest service matched it to (Data model: commerce contact link); the views of 0007d and lead to order still join through the refs (Open decisions).

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.upsert_commerce_store(connection, handle, name, market, currency, zone)` | creates or updates the connection's store (`tenant.configure`); a handle another store holds is a conflict (`CMA03`), a market outside the catalog or an unknown connection `CMA02`, a malformed handle, currency or zone `CMA04` |
| `cma.reclassify_orders()` | recomputes `order_kind` for the tenant's orders (`tenant.configure`), answers how many changed |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_commerce_customers()`, `ingest_upsert_commerce_orders()` | the ingest write path (`ingest.write`), at most 500 items per call (and 500 lines per order); outcomes inserted, updated, stale, deleted, unknown, orders with their kind; hosts lower-cased, a path loses its query string, UTM keeps its five keys, tags trimmed, de-duplicated and kept to 30 of at most 60 characters |
| `cma.connection_config()` | as in 0007, plus the connection's store (handle, name, market, currency, zone), which the commerce adapter reads (the market is the phone hash's default region since 0008g; the CRM write-back it was first read for is unused since 0008h) |

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `commerce_store`, `commerce_customer`, `commerce_order`, `commerce_order_line`, none with the raw payload. The application never deletes stores, customers or orders; order lines are a child set replaced with their order (as contact refs are with their contact), and the audit trigger keeps every removed line. The verify (`37`) proves structure, keys and privileges and, on throwaway tenants, one store per connection and a unique handle (through the function and the keys) with the refusals and the store in `connection_config`; customers inserted, updated, stale, deleted and back only after a newer read, one customer id in two stores, the refusals; orders with lines replaced as a set, kept without the key and untouched by a stale read, `order_kind` first, repeat and unknown, renewal from each setting with reclassification on every change and on demand, test and cancelled orders kept and flagged, deletion, the normalisation of pages, UTM and tags, the refusals; the Ingest user unable to configure, a configuring person unable to ingest, and tenant isolation for every new table (another tenant's setting change included).

### Data model: outbox and CRM contact write-back (migration 0007c)

Written on 10 October 2026 as intake track I4 from `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md` §4.3a (brief B13). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4: `38_outbox.sql` twice, `39_verify_outbox.sql` passing normally and failing under provoke, a two-session claim test, 28 mutations caught and every earlier verify unchanged; the outputs are in `docs/night-2026-10-10/local-records/0007c/`. Run in dev and prod on 11 October 2026 (`records/0007c-dev-2026-10-11`, `records/0007c-prod-2026-10-11`). It needs 0007b (the store in `connection_config`), not 0007a or 0008. Universal: the fields, their target properties and modes are tenant configuration per CRM connection, and the payload holds ids, a store handle, counts, an amount and codes, never personal data (the ingest service drops the email it matched the contact with before it enqueues). The worker comes with the Shopify adapter (brief B10). **Since 13 October 2026 unused for commerce** (Decision log; addition 0008h): the HubSpot–Shopify app writes the Shopify contact fields, so the CMA writes nothing back, no runbook step configures a write-back field, nothing enqueues an action and the job `ingest-outbox` has no schedule. The tables, functions and worker stay as they are, generic, for the next action towards another system.

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `writeback_field` | tenant | per CRM connection: the canonical field (`commerce_ref`, `commerce_store`, `commerce_orders`, `commerce_spent`, `country`, `currency`, `language`), its slot (above 1 only for `commerce_ref`: a contact holds the ids of two stores), the target contact property (written by one field only per connection), the mode (`always`, `if_empty`, or `slot` for `commerce_ref` only), enabled; disabled, never removed |
| `outbox_action` | tenant | one row per action towards another system (today `crm.contact.set_properties`): connection, target type and id, the payload (canonical field → value, at most 4 kB), a dedupe hash, the reason, the state, attempts, next attempt, claimed and sent times, the system's answer (`result`, at most 4 kB), a short error, who asked (`created_by`) and who resolved a parked action (`resolved_by`, `resolved_at`) |

States: `pending`, `in_flight`, `sent`, `failed`, `needs_review` (parked for a person), `superseded` (a newer action for the same target) and `dropped` (by a person). They only move forward, enforced by a trigger: pending and failed go to in flight or superseded; in flight to sent, failed, parked or superseded (or in flight again when a dead worker's claim expired); parked to pending (retry) or dropped. Sent, superseded and dropped are final, and the target and payload never change; the application may update only the state columns and never deletes.

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.set_writeback_field(connection, field, property, mode, enabled, slot default 1)` | creates or updates one field (`tenant.configure`); an unknown field or mode, the slot mode or a slot above 1 for another field than `commerce_ref`, an empty property `CMA04`; a property another field writes `CMA03`; an unknown connection `CMA02` |
| `cma.enqueue_contact_writeback(connection, contact id, values, reason)` | the ingest side (`ingest.write`): refuses unknown fields and malformed values, keeps only enabled fields (the store and totals only with the customer id they describe), answers null when nothing is left; the same payload as the contact's latest action answers that action (the dedupe), else a new pending action supersedes the contact's older pending and failed ones, so an older value is never written after a newer one |
| `cma.outbox_claim(connection, limit default 50)` | due actions (pending, failed after the backoff, in flight with an expired claim) with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, never two of one contact at a time; counts an attempt and sets the next 1, 2, 4 … 60 minutes ahead (as `ingest_claim_events`), which is also how long the claim holds; a due action a newer one has overtaken is superseded instead, an expired claim at the maximum is parked |
| `cma.outbox_finish(ids, outcome, result, error)` | `sent` with the answer, or `failed` with a short error code; a failure at `outbox.max_attempts` (setting, default 8) parks the action; only actions in flight change |
| `cma.outbox_resolve(id, decision)` | `retry` (pending again, attempts start over) or `drop` for a parked action, recording the person (`tenant.configure`); a retry is refused when a newer action for the same target exists |
| `cma.outbox_status()` | per connection and state: counts, oldest and newest, the next attempt due (`tenant.configure` or `reports.view`) |
| `cma.connection_config()` | as in 0007b, plus the connection's write-back fields (field, slot, property, mode, enabled) for the worker, which writes no other property |

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `writeback_field` and `outbox_action` (with the payload, without the dedupe hash). The verify (`39`) proves structure, keys, the column grants, the state trigger and the claim's `SKIP LOCKED`, and, on throwaway tenants: fields set and updated in place, disabled not removed, the slot rules and the refusals, the fields in `connection_config`; enqueue with only enabled fields, unknown fields and bad values refused, null when nothing is left, the dedupe, supersede per contact, a sent action untouched, the same payload again after another one, an inactive connection refused; one claim per action, the backoff, a dead claim returning, no claim while the contact is in flight, an overtaken claim superseded, parking after failures and after an expired claim, a sent action never reopened (functions, trigger, grants); retry and drop recording the person, a newer action blocking a retry, `outbox_status` and who may read it; the Ingest user unable to configure or resolve, a configuring person unable to enqueue, and tenant isolation for both tables. Two workers in two sessions (claims held open) are in the local run: disjoint claims, no waiting, and two concurrent enqueues for one contact serialised so the second supersedes the first.

### Data model: lead metrics (migration 0007d)

Written on 10 October 2026 as intake track I5 from `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md` §4.4 (brief B4); run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 (`40_lead_metrics.sql` twice, `41_verify_lead_metrics.sql` passing and failing under provoke, twenty-five mutations caught, every earlier verify unchanged, outputs in `docs/night-2026-10-10/local-records/0007d/`). It needs 0007a and 0007b (and 0007, through them). No new tables: functions and reporting views over the intake tables. Universal: pipelines, markets, hours, targets and windows are tenant configuration; owners are matched through `app_user_external_id` with the system `<the call's or record's source system>_owner`, so no vendor name is in the code.

**Speed to lead, as computed.** The population is the deals of counted lead pipelines (`is_counted` and `is_lead`; a pipeline not yet seen counts), not deleted, created on or after `intake.start_date` in the tenant's zone. A deal's contacts are its primary contact and the contacts currently associated with it. Its calls are the CRM calls of its connection, not deleted, currently associated with the deal or one of those contacts, whose effective start (the linked telephony call's start while the link is current, else the CRM call's time) is at or after the creation minus `speed_to_lead.pre_window_minutes`. The first outbound call is the measured one; the row also gives the first call of any direction, whether an inbound call came first, and the first connected call (outcome `is_connected`, or the linked telephony call answered). Market: the deal's own mapped market first, then its primary contact's country, each through the market aliases (read again, so an alias added later applies to older deals) and only when it names a catalog market; `unknown` otherwise, and then the business measures are empty (data quality shows the values). Measures: `elapsed_seconds`, `business_seconds` (office time in the market), `within_target` (at most `speed_to_lead.target_minutes`), `before_next_noon` (before 12:00 on the next working day), `waiting_business_seconds` for open deals not called; for a deal not yet called, `within_target` and `before_next_noon` stay empty while it can still make them and turn false when it no longer can. Status `called`, `not_called_open` or `not_called_closed`. The agent is the person behind the first outbound call's owner, else behind the deal's owner.

| Function or view | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.speed_to_lead_rows(from, to)`, `cma.speed_to_lead_summary(from, to, group)` | one row per lead deal created in a business-date range of at most 92 days; the summary by `day`, `market`, `agent` or `pipeline`: deals, called, median and 80th percentile business minutes over called deals, % within target and % before the next noon over the deals whose outcome is known, open not called (`reports.view` or `performance.team`) |
| `cma.lead_to_order_rows(from, to)` | per lead deal: how many commerce ids its contacts hold (any ref slot, the ref system equal to the order's source system) and the first of their orders that is not a test, not cancelled and not deleted, created after the deal within `lead_to_order.max_days`, with its kind, store and days (same permission) |
| `cma.intake_per_day(from, to)` | per business date and market: form submissions of counted forms (the form's market), deals and tickets created and closed (counted pipelines), calls in and out on counted lines (the line's market), calls carrying a counted tag, new commerce customers, first and repeat orders and renewals (the store's market; test and cancelled orders out) (same permission) |
| `cma.data_quality()` | the total of each check for the Data page (`reports.view` or `tenant.configure`): lead deals without a contact; their primary contacts without country or language; markets or countries written by a source that are not in the catalog; CRM owners and telephony users without a person; CRM calls without a contact; telephony calls without a link after 24 hours (once the tenant has CRM calls); commerce customers whose id no contact holds, or several contacts hold; write-backs parked for review (from 0007c on; empty until the outbox exists); contacts whose first two ref slots of a system are taken; contacts whose country differs from a store their commerce id belongs to; a commerce id in two stores; pipelines no person has ever written (only the source's refresh, read from the audit log); counted forms without a market |
| `cma_read.speed_to_lead`, `cma_read.lead_to_order`, `cma_read.intake_per_day_v`, `cma_read.dq_<check>` (fifteen) | the same rows, without a date range, for every tenant the reader sees; the `dq_` views give tenant, connection, object type, object id and a code where the check is about one, never personal data |

How the rows are made once: each read is a function of an explicit tenant (`cma.*_of`) in the caller's rights. The application's functions check the permission and call it for the current tenant under row-level security. A reporting view cannot call a function that reads schema `cma` in the reader's rights, so each view reads one of four `SECURITY DEFINER` functions in `cma_read` (nine since addition 0008o added five for the follow-up views) (owned by `cma_owner`, search path pinned with `pg_temp` last, executable by `cma_readonly` only), which call the same function for each tenant `cma_read.reader_sees()` allows. `cma.business_seconds()` and `cma.next_business_noon()` of 0007 keep their signatures and now share the code of `business_seconds_of()` and `next_business_noon_of()`.

The verify (`41`) proves structure and privileges and, on throwaway tenants with a constructed week (NL and GB, office hours, a holiday), every speed-to-lead case of brief B4 (7 business minutes; Friday 16:55 to Monday 09:10 as 15 business minutes and before the next noon; not called open and closed; an inbound call first; a call on the deal only and on a second associated contact; the pre-window both ways; a linked telephony call moving the start; a removed association; non-lead and uncounted pipelines out; the market from the deal through a later alias, from the contact, unknown; the agent through the call's and the deal's owner; a holiday), the summary in each grouping, the 92-day bound, lead to order (first, repeat through ref slot 2, renewal; test, cancelled and late orders out; the window as a setting), intake per day per market, every data-quality check firing exactly once and clearing when fixed, the permissions (an agent and the Ingest user refused, the supervisor without data quality), tenant isolation for the application and for readers, and the readers' views equal to the application's rows.

### Data model: messaging core and record alerts (migration 0008)

Written on 10 October 2026 as intake track I6 from `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md` §4.5 and §8 (brief B5). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4: `42_messaging.sql` twice, and `43_verify_messaging.sql` passing normally and failing under provoke. Sixteen mutations were caught and every earlier verify was unchanged; the outputs are in `docs/night-2026-10-10/local-records/0008/`. Run in dev and prod on 10 October 2026 (`records/0008-dev-2026-10-10`, `records/0008-prod-2026-10-10`). Universal: rules, audiences and links are tenant configuration. Messages carry references only (README Messaging): an alert's title and body are built from configuration (`New deal`, then `<market> · <pipeline label> · created <local time>`), never from customer data.

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `message_rule` | tenant | which created records raise an alert for whom: name (the identity), trigger `record_created`, record type, pipeline ids (empty: every counted pipeline), audience permission (default `leads.accept`), `match_language`, `only_clocked_in`, fallback permission (default `leads.manage`, null for none), urgency, enabled, `max_age_minutes` (null: the setting `alerts.max_age_minutes`); disabled, never deleted |
| `message` | tenant | an alert or an announcement: urgency, title (≤ 120), body (≤ 500), the record it points to (`ref_system` the adapter, `ref_type`, `ref_id`, `ref_url` https only), the rule, the target as asked (for an alert, the rule's terms, the market and whether the fallback was used), sender, `created_at`, `expires_at`. Append-only; one alert per rule and record (unique index), so a retry writes nothing |
| `message_delivery` | tenant | one row per recipient, resolved when the message is written (history stays exact when teams or skills change): `delivered_at` (the recipient's first poll that returned it), `read_at`, `acknowledged_at`. Each time is set once and never moves (trigger); read implies delivered, acknowledged implies read |

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.upsert_message_rule(name, record type, pipeline ids, audience, match language, only clocked in, fallback, urgency, max age?)`, `set_message_rule_enabled()` | configuration for `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.ingest_record_alerts(connection, record type, source ids)` | the ingest service's call after a read-back of created records (`ingest.write`, at most 500 ids). Each record (not deleted, younger than the age limit) meets each enabled rule of its type whose pipelines it is in. The recipients are active people holding the audience permission, clocked in if asked, and holding the market's language skill at the market's minimum level if asked; when nobody matches, the holders of the fallback permission. The market is the record's, else its contact's country. A market outside the catalog matches nobody on language (so the fallback applies); a market without a language skill matches everyone. A message is written even when nobody receives it, so the stats show it. The link comes from the connection setting `record_url_<record type>`, a template with `{app_host}`, `{account}` and `{id}`, so no vendor's address pattern lives in the database. Answers how many messages were written |
| `cma.send_message(title, body, urgency, target)` | a manager's message (`messages.send`). The target names exactly one group: `{"everyone": true}`, `{"teams": [keys]}`, `{"language": {"skill", "minLevel"}}` or `{"users": [ids]}`, optionally with `"onlyClockedIn": true`. Resolved to active people now; a target that reaches nobody is refused |
| `cma.my_messages(since?, limit?)`, `my_unread_count()`, `mark_messages_read(ids)`, `acknowledge_message(id)` | a signed-in person's own deliveries only, newest first, expired ones left out. The first return sets `delivered_at`; acknowledging also reads |
| `cma.message_stats(from, to)` | per message in a business-date range of at most 92 days: recipients, delivered, read, acknowledged and the median seconds to read (`messages.send` or `performance.team`) |

"Clocked in" (`cma.is_clocked_in`) means an open workday that has started, pauses included; a day forgotten past its end is closed by the scheduler (0005a). Reporting views in `cma_read`: `message` (title and body hold references only, so both are included), `message_delivery` and `message_rule`. The application never deletes from these tables, never updates a message, and updates a delivery only in its three times (column privileges).

The verify (`43`) proves structure and privileges. On throwaway tenants it then proves:

- rules and their refusals;
- a rule reaching only the agents clocked in with the market's language at the minimum level;
- the market taken from the contact;
- the fallback, and a rule without one;
- a market without a language skill;
- `only_clocked_in` false;
- the age limit, from the setting and from the rule;
- idempotency;
- a disabled rule;
- pipelines that are not counted and pipelines not yet seen;
- deleted and unknown records;
- resolution at send time;
- the link and its refusals;
- the content;
- `delivered_at` set once and only for the caller, `since`, `limit` and expiry;
- read and acknowledge on own rows only, with times that never move back;
- every target of `send_message`, with its refusals and its permission;
- the stats;
- the Ingest user unable to configure, send or read, and a configuring person unable to raise alerts;
- tenant isolation and no deletes.

### Data model: assignment alerts and automation messages (addition 0008a)

Written on 11 October 2026 for the go-live with Arno (`44_assignment_alerts.sql`, `45_verify_assignment_alerts.sql`), run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.6 (`44` twice, `45` passing and failing under provoke, `43` unchanged afterwards; outputs in `docs/night-2026-10-11/local-records/0008a/`). It needs 0008. Universal: the owner vocabulary is `app_user_external_id` with the system `<adapter>_owner`, the links come from the connection's templates, nothing names a vendor.
