- **Assignment rules.** `message_rule.trigger` gains `record_assigned`: a record of the rule's type and pipelines whose owner maps to an active person raises one message for that person, `New <type> assigned to you` with the market, the pipeline label and the time, the record link, and the owner's source id as `target.assignee`; once per record and owner, so a reassignment alerts the new owner and a retry writes nothing. `cma.upsert_assignment_rule(name, record type, pipeline ids, urgency, max age?)` configures one (`tenant.configure`); the audience fields of 0008 are set to neutral values and never read for this trigger. The ingest service calls `cma.ingest_assignment_alerts(connection, record type, source ids)` after every read-back of records, created or changed (`ingest.write`; the age limit is the record's created or updated time against the rule's `max_age_minutes`, else `alerts.max_age_minutes`).
- **Uniqueness.** The one-alert-per-record index of 0008 now covers created alerts only (`where rule_id is not null and not (target ? 'assignee')`); `message_rule_ref_assignee_idx` covers assignment alerts per record and assignee; `cma.ingest_record_alerts()` is redefined with the matching conflict target, its body unchanged.
- **Messages from automations.** `cma.ingest_send_message(connection, title, body, urgency, target, ref system?, ref type?, ref id?)` (`ingest.write`) sends an announcement from the tenant's Ingest user to a target that names exactly one of `everyone`, `teams`, `language`, `users`, `owners` (`{ "system": "<crm>", "ids": [...] }`, the people behind CRM owner ids through `app_user_external_id` with the system `<crm>_owner`) or `emails` (work addresses, case-insensitive), optionally `onlyClockedIn`; the optional reference gives the link through the connection's `record_url_<type>` template. Resolved to active people now; nobody reached is CMA04, an unknown team, skill or person CMA02. The ingest service exposes it as `POST /automation/<connection key>` with `Authorization: Bearer <the connection's signing secret>` and a body `{ "messages": [ { title, body?, urgency?, target, ref? } ] }` (1 to 50): 404 for an unknown key or another adapter's connection, 401 for a wrong bearer, 400 with a code for a bad body, else 200 with `sent` (index, id, recipients) and `refused` (index, `invalid` or `not_found`), each message in its own transaction. A connection of adapter `automation` is created with `cma.upsert_connection('automation', name, <the CRM account id for the links>, <signing secret name>, null)` and the `record_url_*` settings.

### Data model: workday window and forgotten-day close (addition 0008b)

Written on 12 October 2026 (Martin's decisions of that day; `46_workday_window.sql`, `47_verify_workday_window.sql`), run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4: `46` twice, `47` passing normally and failing under provoke (one FAIL per block), nine mutations of `46` caught, and `27`, `43` and `45` unchanged afterwards. Run in dev and prod on 12 October 2026 the same way (`46`, `47` passing and failing under provoke, then `02`, `27`, `43`, `45` again; records above); before prod, the one existing day ending after 22:00 was a team member's test day, now counted to 22:00. It needs 0008a. Universal: the window, the close time and who gets the digest are tenant settings; Pulse4all's values are seed data in `02_seed_pulse4all.sql` (07:00 to 22:00, 17:00, a grace of 60 minutes, for both tenants, set only where a tenant has no value yet). All times are local times in the day's own zone (the person's zone, kept on the day; Europe/Amsterdam and Europe/Madrid for Pulse4all, which keep the same clock).

| Setting | Type, default | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `workday.window_start` | time, `00:00` | the earliest clock-in of a working day: `cma.open_workday()` refuses a new day before it with `CMA04` and the message "the workday starts at HH:MI" |
| `workday.window_end` | time, `24:00` | the end of the working day: every stretch's counted time stops here (a stretch that begins after it counts nothing), an open day past it is capped and flagged, and the forgotten-day grace counts from it. Status changes and clock-outs after it are accepted: leaving is never blocked |
| `workday.auto_close_at` | time, `24:00` | where the scheduler ends a forgotten day when the person has no published shift that day |
| `workday.auto_close_notify_permission` | text, `workday.team` | who receives the digest of closed days (a permission key from the catalog) |
| `workday.auto_close_grace_minutes` | integer, 120 (since 0005a) | now counted from the window end |

The catalog gains the value type `time` (`HH:MI`, `00:00` to `24:00`); the settings trigger refuses a window that does not start before it ends and a notification permission outside the catalog. The universal defaults keep the behaviour from before 0008b for a tenant that sets nothing: a day from midnight to midnight, closed at its business day's end.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.business_day_end(date, zone)`, `cma.business_day_start(date, zone)` | the day's bounds from the current tenant's window (`24:00` is the next midnight, as before); every function that bounds a day calls these. Executable by the application only; `cma.business_day_time(date, zone, time)` is the plain arithmetic |
| `cma.time_interval_all`, `cma.workday_summary_all` | as in 0002 and 0005a with the window end of each row's own tenant, read in the owner's rights, so the readers' faces follow without access to the settings. One change in the counting: every stretch stops at the window end, not only an open one. Same columns, so every face and function above them is unchanged |
| `cma.open_workday()` | as in 0003d, plus the window start where a day is created |
| `cma.correct_workday()`, `cma.correct_time_event()` | times only within the corrected day's window (`CMA04` otherwise) |
| `cma.close_forgotten_workdays(grace?)` | a day still open after the window end plus the grace is ended at the person's published planned shift end for that date (the latest publication's entry, when it is a shift), else at `workday.auto_close_at`; never later than the window end and never earlier than the day's last effective event. Source `system`, the scheduler as acting user, as before; the result gains `end_rule` (`shift`, `default`, `last_event`). In the same transaction: one announcement per closed day to that person ("Workday closed automatically": "Your workday of Mon 12 Oct was closed automatically at 17:00 because you did not log out. Your supervisor will review it."), and per run one announcement to every active person holding the notification permission ("Workdays closed automatically": "4 workday(s) of Mon 12 Oct were closed automatically. Review them on Team hours."), without names or a reference, sent by the scheduler. Idempotent: a second run closes and sends nothing. The day stays `needs_correction` (0005a), so Team hours and Welcome's to-do line show it as before |

The web app reads the day's end with the own day (`dayEndsAt` on `GET /api/v1/me/day` and on the team now's `day`): the running clock on Welcome and My day, and the figure on Log out, stop counting there. A refused clock-in shows "The workday starts at 07:00" under the Clock in button, with the time the database gave, and the button stays. The mock follows the same rule with 07:00 to 22:00 and 17:00.

The verify (`47`) proves structure and privileges (the value type, the four settings with their defaults, who may execute the functions, the time views' columns and who reads them). On throwaway tenants, rolled back, with every time on a date two days back so the verdict does not depend on the hour, it proves:

- a tenant without settings counts as before;
- malformed and inconsistent settings are refused, and an agent cannot set them;
- a clock-in at 06:59 is refused with "the workday starts at 07:00", and one at 07:00 is accepted;
- a status change at 22:30 and a clock-out at 22:40 are accepted, with the counted time stopped at 22:00 for the application and for a reader;
- corrections outside the window are refused in both correction paths;
- four forgotten days close at the right end: a published shift ending 16:30 at 16:30 (`shift`), no shift at 17:00 (`default`), a last status change at 19:12 at 19:12 (`last_event`), a shift ending 23:00 at 22:00;
- each person gets one notice, every holder of `workday.team` gets one digest with "4 workday(s)", and nobody else gets either;
- the closed days stay flagged;
- a second run closes and sends nothing;
- the other tenant is untouched.

The verdict lists, per tenant, the window, the close settings and the open days past the window and its grace.

### Data model: legacy time import (addition 0008d)

Written on 12 October 2026 (Martin's decisions of that day; `50_legacy_time_import.sql`, `51_verify_legacy_time_import.sql`), run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 (with a local `uuidv7()` stand-in): `50` twice, `51` passing normally and failing under provoke (one FAIL per block), eight mutations of `50` caught, and `07`, `10`, `14`, `16`, `27`, `47` and `49` unchanged afterwards, also after `02`; an end-to-end run of the tool, the COPY file and the person map statement on a local tenant. It needs 0008c. Applied in dev and prod on 12 October 2026 after 0008c (Environments). Universal: nothing in the migration names a system, a tenant, a status key or a time; the source system, the work status, the gap status and the period are the batch's values, the window and the close time the tenant's settings. Pulse4all's import status is seed data in `02`; the run is `docs/import/README.md`.

**Status: loaded in prod 12 October 2026, batch `steam-2026`** (2026-10-12T13:02:18Z, `records/0008d-import-prod-2026-10-12`): 2353 sessions in, 462 merged, 1 open session dropped, none outside the window or unmapped, no account skipped; 1506 days written (23 closed by rule, 85 clipped, 1 dropped, none skipped as existing), 3778 events; 9,504.7 working hours over 13 people, 108 days flagged for a correction review (Open decisions). Before staging, 11 duplicated session ids were reduced to the row with the latest logout and the five management accounts were left out of the map (Decision log, 12 October 2026). Team hours and Correct a day show the days and the batch key as designed.

Why: the hours before the CMA lived in Steam Connect. Pulse4all's export (2 January to 9 October 2026) holds login and logout sessions only, in local time without an offset: no statuses, no breaks. Loading them as their own event source lets Team hours, the exports and the reports start on 2 January 2026 instead of on the CMA's first day, without a second hours store.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.time_event.source` `import`, `import_batch_id` | an imported event names its batch, and only an imported event does (a check); otherwise a fact like any other: append-only, effective until a correction supersedes or voids it |
| `cma.import_batch` | one file: key (unique per tenant), source system, file name, period, the zone of its local times, `staged`, `loaded` or `voided`, the work status and the gap status it writes; at load the snapshots of the window start and end and the close time, who loaded it, when and the counts; who voided it, when and why |
| `cma.import_session` | the file's sessions: account id and name in the earlier system, session id (unique per batch), login and logout in local time (no logout: never logged out), role. Written only by COPY into `cma.import_session_staging` (the staging file's columns, the batch by its key) by a person holding `tenant.configure`, into a staged batch; never changed |
| `cma.import_person_map` | which person of the tenant an account is (inactive people too, system users never); written by the same person into a staged batch; an account without a row is skipped and counted |
| `cma.import_day` | one row per day a load wrote: the workday, its end event, the sessions and merges behind it, the seconds, and whether it was closed by the open-session rule or clipped to the window |
| `cma.stage_import_batch(key, source_system, file_name, from, to, zone, work_status, gap_status)` | a staged batch; the statuses must exist and the period must end before today in the batch's zone; called again with the same values it returns the batch |
| `cma.load_import_batch(key)` | one transaction, rules below; returns the batch with its counts |
| `cma.void_import_batch(key, reason)` | withdraws the batch, below |
| `cma.workday_summary_all` | as in 0008b, same columns, two changes: `needs_correction` also covers an imported day closed by the open-session rule or clipped, while the import's end is effective (a correction that replaces the end clears it, as for a system end); a day without an effective start, which only a withdrawn import leaves, is no day |
| `cma.team_day()` | gains `import_batch_key`; the day editor's history shows "Imported from an earlier system, batch <key>" |
| `cma_read.import_batch`, `cma_read.time_event` | the batch without names (no file name, no free-text reason); the events gain `import_batch_id`. The sessions and the map have no reader view |

All four tables are tenant tables (`setup_tenant_table`: row-level security, the application's policy, audit). The application never deletes a batch, never changes a session, a map row or an imported day, and writes them only through the functions and the COPY target; the functions run as the caller (`cma_app`) and check `tenant.configure` inside, so an agent and a manager are refused and an admin is allowed.

**The load**, per mapped account and local login date, in the batch's zone with its snapshots (`cma.business_day_time`):

- a session without a logout ends at the close time (`workday.auto_close_at`); one that starts at or after it without a logout is dropped (`sessions_open_dropped`); the day is flagged closed by rule, as a system end is;
- every session is clipped to the window (`workday.window_start` to `window_end`); a session with nothing left inside it counts as `sessions_outside_window`; the day is flagged clipped;
- overlapping and touching sessions are merged (`sessions_merged`);
- one day from the first login to the last logout: the start in the work status, at every logout a status change to the gap status and at the next login back to the work status, the end at the last logout; source `import`, `occurred_at` the local time in the batch's zone, `recorded_at` now; then `refresh_workday`. So the working seconds of a day are exactly the sessions after the rules, and the gap status holds the gaps;
- a date that already has a day with effective events is skipped (`days_skipped_existing`: the CMA's own days win); the day of a withdrawn import is used again; a day under one minute is dropped (`days_dropped`);
- the work status is seeded inactive and never offered: no screen and no write path lets anyone choose an inactive status, and the load accepts it as long as no effective event of the tenant uses it (nothing was ever booked on it; after a void the withdrawn events no longer count, so a reload works too). The load still retires it, a no-op for a status that is inactive already; the counts: `sessions_in`, `sessions_unmapped`, `accounts_skipped`, `sessions_open_dropped`, `sessions_outside_window`, `sessions_merged`, `days_written`, `events_written`, `days_closed_by_rule`, `days_clipped`, `days_dropped`, `days_skipped_existing`.

Refusals: a batch not staged (`CMA03`); an unknown status (`CMA02`); a work status that is not working, is the default, or is inactive with time behind it (an effective event of the tenant in it); a gap status that is working or paid; a map row outside the tenant; a session in the spring clock change gap of the batch's zone; a session that starts outside the period; a batch without sessions of a mapped account (all `CMA04`). A refused load writes nothing.

**The void** adds one void correction per effective event of every day the batch wrote, corrections made on those days since included, by the caller as approver with the reason "Import <key> withdrawn: <reason>". No fact is deleted and the day history keeps every row; the days then have no effective event, so the summary, Team hours, the exports and the Dashboard no longer count them, and a new batch may load the same person-days. Chosen over a status flag that hides a batch's events, because a void is the time model's own way to say an event did not count, with who and why, and it needs no change to the effective-event rule every read uses. A staged batch is simply marked voided. A reloaded day shows "Corrected afterwards", because its first import was withdrawn by a correction.

Imported days are kept in the batch's zone (Europe/Amsterdam for Pulse4all, the same clock as Newco's Europe/Madrid). The autumn change is outside Pulse4all's period; an ambiguous local time would take PostgreSQL's reading (the later, standard-time instant). The verify (`51`) proves structure and privileges, then on throwaway tenants with the dev window: agent and manager refused and the admin allowed; one batch with two overlapping sessions, an open session before and one after the close time, a session across the window end and one wholly after it, a login before the window start, three sessions with two gaps, a sub-minute day, an account without a map row, a date with a CMA day and an inactive person; the exact counts, seconds and gap seconds per day, the flags, the events and their batch, Team hours, the status-changes export (entered by import), the day history's batch key, a reader's view without the sessions; the work status retired, a second load and rows into a loaded batch refused; another tenant sees and loads nothing; the void and a reload on the same workdays; seventeen refusals, among them an inactive work status with time behind it; and an inactive work status nothing was ever booked on, as the import status is seeded, that loads.

### Data model: sources configuration (addition 0008e)

Written in the night of 12 to 13 October 2026 (`52_sources_configuration.sql`, `53_verify_sources_configuration.sql`) and amended in place on 13 October 2026 after the review of PR #110, before it ran anywhere (the reserved names and the adapter rule below). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `52` twice, and refused by its pre-flight on a copy holding an adapter with an underscore; `53` 16 PASS normally and failing A1, B1 and B8 under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; fifteen mutations caught; `04` to `51` unchanged afterwards (`docs/night-2026-10-13/local-records/0008e/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/night-2026-10-13/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008e-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008e-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `53` 16 PASS, provoked 13 PASS and 3 FAIL (A1, B1, B8); in prod the reruns of `31` and `33` PASS. It needs 0008d, and refuses to run while an existing connection's adapter is not lower-case letters and digits only (it lists tenant id, adapter and name).

Why: on 12 October Martin set the intake configuration of the live portal (pipelines, forms, call outcomes, aliases, the assignment rule, owners on persons) by SQL in Studio. The screens Configuration → Sources, Markets and Alert rules and the linked accounts on People do the same through the functions that exist since 0006 to 0008a; this addition holds what had no function, and replaces three functions for two rules of the review (the last three rows of the table). Every write of those screens goes through an existing function (`set_connection_settings`, `set_connection_status`, `upsert_connection` for the name, `set_connection_pipeline`, `set_pipeline_lead`, `set_form`, `set_call_outcome`, `set_telephony_number`, `set_telephony_tag`, `upsert_commerce_store`, `upsert_market`, `set_market_status`, `set_market_alias`, `remove_market_alias`, `set_business_hours`, `set_business_holiday`, `remove_business_holiday`, `upsert_message_rule`, `upsert_assignment_rule`, `set_message_rule_enabled`, `set_user_external_id`, `remove_user_external_id`).

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.integration_connection.kind` | `crm`, `telephony`, `commerce` or `automation`: the kind of source in the data model's terms (the tables its data lands in), never a vendor; null until set. At the migration an existing connection takes the one kind its rows imply (a connection with rows of two kinds, or none, stays unset). `cma_read.integration_connection` gains the column |
| `cma.set_connection_kind(connection, kind)` | sets it (`tenant.configure`); refuses an unknown kind (`CMA04`) and a kind that contradicts the rows the connection holds (`CMA03`): a connection with CRM records cannot become telephony |
| `cma.sources()` | every connection of the tenant with its kind, the kinds its rows imply, status, account id, non-secret settings, the names of its two secrets (names, never values), events in the last 24 hours, waiting and parked events, the last event, and the size of its catalogs (pipelines, forms, call outcomes, fields, lines, tags, the store's handle); `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.connection_catalogs(connection)` | its forms, call outcomes, lines and tags with their configuration, and the newest record id per record type for the record link preview (ids only); `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.markets_all()`, `cma.business_hours_all()`, `cma.business_holidays_all()` | every market with its aliases, inactive ones included; the office hours per market and weekday (`*` the tenant's default); the holidays; `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.message_rules()` | every rule of both triggers (`record_created`, `record_assigned`) with record type, pipelines, audience (permission, language match, clocked in only, fallback), urgency, maximum age, the switch, and its alerts in the last 30 days with the last one (the sound follows the urgency: an urgent alert chimes); `tenant.configure` (brief B14) |
| `cma.linked_account_systems()` | the ids a person can hold: `<adapter>_owner` per CRM connection, `<adapter>_user` per telephony connection (the vocabulary the reads of 0007d match on), plus every such system someone already holds an id for, so an id never disappears from the screen when its connection is switched off or changes kind; `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all` |
| `cma.linked_accounts()` | the ids held by the people the caller may see on People (as `cma.directory()`: `users.manage_all` everyone, `users.manage_agents` the non-managing people and themselves); never a sign-in id |
| `cma.set_user_external_id()`, `cma.remove_user_external_id()` | as in 0007, with the People rule instead of `users.manage_all`: a manager links the people they may edit, an admin anyone (`cma.assert_may_manage` of 0004). Checks in order: the permission, the system reads `<system>_owner` or `<system>_user` and the id has 1 to 100 characters without spaces (`CMA04`), the person exists (`CMA02`), the caller may manage them (`CMA06`), the id is not another person's (`CMA03`) |
| `cma.find_tenants_for_identity(system, external id)` | replaced: as in 0002, plus a system name ending in `_owner` or `_user` never matches, so a linked id never signs anyone in; the login's `SECURITY DEFINER` function, owned by `cma_owner`, executable by `cma_app` only (the others: `cma.ingest_connection()` of 0006, the reader functions of 0007d in `cma_read`, and those named in their own sections) |
| `cma.add_person()` | replaced: as in 0004, plus a login system ending in `_owner` or `_user` is refused (`CMA04`, "a login system cannot end in _owner or _user; those name linked accounts"), checked right after the login system's form; signature, every other check and their order unchanged |
| `cma.upsert_connection()` | replaced: as in 0006, plus an adapter name that does not match `^[a-z0-9]+$` is refused (`CMA04`, "an adapter name is lower-case letters and digits only"); signature and grants unchanged |

The functions run as the caller (`cma_app`, except the `SECURITY DEFINER` login lookup), are executable by `cma_app` only and check their permission inside, so a reader and an agent are refused. The verify (`53`) proves structure and privileges, then on throwaway tenants: the kind set, refused against held rows, unknown, or for an agent; the sources with their kinds and catalog sizes and a manager refused; the catalogs with the newest record id; markets with aliases, hours and holidays; rules of both triggers with their alert counts; linked-account systems from connections and held ids; the directory's scope; five refusals of the People rule; the other tenant seeing nothing; `add_person` refusing a login system ending in `_owner` or `_user`; `find_tenants_for_identity` finding no tenant for a linked id while it still finds the tenant's Ingest user; `upsert_connection` refusing an adapter with an underscore: 16 checks (4 in block A, 12 in block B). Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants in a rolled-back transaction next to the dev seed.

### Data model: uncounted lines (addition 0008f)

Written on 13 October 2026 (`54_uncounted_lines.sql`, `55_verify_uncounted_lines.sql`; brief B9a). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `54` twice, and on a copy at 0008e holding calls on two lines already marked not counted (6 calls, 6 taggings and 1 link removed, the counted line and the CRM call untouched, a rerun zeros); `55` 17 PASS and every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; seventeen mutations caught; two sessions at once proving the line lock; `04` to `53` unchanged afterwards (`docs/b9a-uncounted-lines/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/b9a-uncounted-lines/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008f-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008f-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `55` 17 PASS, provoked all 17 FAIL; in prod `54`'s one-off removed the calls on the 5 lines of Aircall Subscriptions not counted (12 calls, 2 taggings and 12 links), and the reruns of `31`, `33` and `35` PASS. It needs 0008e.

Why: one telephony account can serve several business lines (tenants). Pulse4all's Aircall account carries both the Subscriptions and the Invest lines, and the Subscriptions connection received the Invest calls too. A line that does not count is another business line's (or an internal or test line), so its calls are never stored in this tenant (Decision log, 13 October 2026). The ingest adapters are unchanged: every call write (webhook, sweep, reconcile, backfill) goes through the one function below.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.ingest_upsert_telephony_calls(connection, calls)` | as in 0007a, plus: a call whose `numberRef` is a line of this connection with `is_counted` false is not written, outcome `skipped_uncounted_line` (one row per call, like the other outcomes; the ingest's sync runs count it as skipped). A call on a line not yet in the catalog is stored as before. The batch's lines are locked shared (advisory, in line order) before `is_counted` is read |
| trigger `remove_uncounted_calls` on `cma.telephony_number` | after a line is added as not counted, or `is_counted` goes from true to false (`cma.set_telephony_number()` from the Sources screen, or any other update), the connection's calls on that line are deleted in the same transaction with their taggings and links, retired links included; the CRM calls stay. `set_telephony_number` itself is unchanged (returns nothing). Switching a line back on deletes nothing and stores its new calls again. Its earlier calls come back only through a read that takes them as new: the reconcile and the backfill skip a call they listed before with the same fingerprint (`sync:telephony_call:<id>:<fingerprint>`, B9), whether it was stored or skipped then (Open decisions). Its function `cma.remove_calls_of_uncounted_line()` is `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `cma_owner` with a pinned search path and executable by nobody, because the application cannot delete from the telephony tables; the catalog refresh never writes `is_counted`, so it never fires it |
| `cma.remove_uncounted_line_calls(tenant, connection, line)` | the one place that deletes telephony calls: takes the line's exclusive lock (a call write on the line in flight finishes first, and the delete then sees it), refuses a missing line (`CMA02`) or a counted one (`CMA03`), answers the calls, taggings and links removed; the owner's only |
| `cma.remove_all_uncounted_line_calls()` | the rule applied to every line already marked not counted, in every tenant: one row per connection with lines (tenant, connection, lines not counted, calls, taggings and links removed); the owner's only. `54` runs it once as its last result |
| `cma.audit_row()` | as in 0001, plus one narrow exception: a delete made by the removal on `telephony_call`, `telephony_call_tag` or `call_link` keeps the row's ids only (its id, tenant, connection, the source call id, the tag id, the linked call ids) and `"reason": "uncounted_line"`, not the row image; table, time and actor as always. The removal switches it on (`cma.audit_keys_only`, transaction-local) for its own deletes only; every other change, deletes elsewhere included, keeps its full image. The earlier insert and update rows of a removed call keep theirs (Open decisions) |

The application still never deletes from the telephony tables (`35` A3 unchanged); a removal runs with the owner's rights through the trigger, and the audit names the person (`actor_user_id`), the login and `cma_owner` as the database role. On Configuration → Sources → Lines the Counted column's hint says that calls on a line that does not count are not stored and that switching a line off removes its stored calls, and switching a line off asks for confirmation with that sentence (both languages); switching it on does not ask. The verify (`55`) proves structure and privileges and, on throwaway tenants: a counted line stored; a line that does not count skipped with its outcome (a deletion and a padded line id too; nothing of the call or its new tag written); a line not in the catalog stored; switching a line off removing its calls, taggings and links (a retired link included) while the CRM calls, the connection's other lines and the tag catalog stay; the audit of those deletes holding ids only; another connection's and another tenant's calls on a line of the same id untouched; switching back deleting nothing and a call on the line stored again; a line added as not counted removing the calls stored while it was unknown; six refusals (an agent and the Ingest user switching a line off, the application calling either removal, a counted and a missing line); the lines already off removed per connection and a rerun answering zeros; the audit exception narrow (an owner's delete outside the rule, the line's own update and a call's insert keep their images, the switch is off again); and the line lock taken shared by the write and exclusively by the removal: 17 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: commerce phone hash (addition 0008g)

Written on 13 October 2026 (`56_commerce_phone_hash.sql`, `57_verify_commerce_phone_hash.sql`; brief B10). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `56` twice; `57` 12 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; fifteen mutations caught; `04` to `55` unchanged afterwards apart from `07` and `10`, which list the new migration row (`docs/b10-shopify/local-records/db/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/b10-shopify/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008g-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008g-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `57` 12 PASS, provoked all 12 FAIL; in prod the reruns of `31`, `33` and `37` PASS. It needs 0008f.

Why (Decision log, 13 October 2026): people request information with one email address and order with another, so an order is matched to earlier calls by its phone number when indirect sales are measured (Open decisions). The calls already carry a keyed hash of the other party's number (0007, 0007a), so the smallest addition is the same hash on the order and its customer, made by the ingest service with the same per-tenant pepper (`ingest-hash-pepper-<tenant>`) and the store's market as the default region, the number dropped in memory. Nothing else personal is added.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.commerce_customer.phone_hash`, `cma.commerce_order.phone_hash` | `HMAC-SHA256(tenant pepper, E.164)` in lower-case hex (64 characters, a check refuses anything else, so a plain number can never land), nullable; a partial index on `(tenant_id, phone_hash)` where not null on each, the shape of `crm_call` and `telephony_call`'s `counterpart_hash`. The order's hash is its own phone's, else its customer's (the Shopify adapter, B10); never a billing or shipping address phone |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_commerce_customers()`, `ingest_upsert_commerce_orders()` | as in 0007b, plus the item key `phoneHash`: stored trimmed and lower-cased; refused (`CMA04`) when it is not 64 hex characters or not a text; a newer read without it clears the stored hash (the store withheld or removed the phone, and the read is the current state); a stale read changes nothing. A customer's deletion clears its hash and its orders' hashes in the same connection; an order's deletion clears the order's. Signatures, grants and every other behaviour unchanged (`37` passes unchanged) |

The reporting views of 0007b are unchanged and leave both hashes out, as they leave out `counterpart_hash`. Retention: the hash follows the tenant's retention for call data (1 year), but no retention job exists yet for call data or these columns (Open decisions); the audit log keeps the row images with the hash, as it does for calls. The verify (`57`) proves structure and privileges (the migration row, the columns with their check and partial indexes, the two tables holding exactly 0007b's columns plus `phone_hash`, the views without a hash, phone or email column, the upserts the application's only and in the caller's rights) and, on throwaway tenants: a customer's hash stored lower-cased and audited as a hash, the 0007b item shape without a hash still taken; a read without the hash clearing it, a stale read leaving it, a newer read setting it; a plain number, 63 characters, a letter beyond f and a JSON number refused for customers and orders with nothing written; orders first, repeat and renewal with their hashes and lines, a read without the hash clearing it, a deletion clearing it and keeping ids, times and kind; a customer's deletion clearing its hash and its orders' in that connection only; an order's hash meeting an earlier CRM call's and telephony call's `counterpart_hash`, the lookup by hash using each partial index; permissions and tenant isolation: 12 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: commerce contact link (addition 0008h)

Written on 13 October 2026 (`58_commerce_contact_link.sql`, `59_verify_commerce_contact_link.sql`; brief B10.1). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `58` twice; `59` 13 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; the earlier verifies unchanged apart from `57`, which now also accepts the three columns of 0008h once 0008h is recorded (`docs/b10-1-local-contact-link/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/b10-1-local-contact-link/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008h-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008h-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `59` 13 PASS, provoked all 13 FAIL, and `57` rerun 12 PASS; in prod the reruns of `31`, `33` and `37` PASS. It needs 0008g.

Why (Decision log, 13 October 2026): the HubSpot–Shopify app already writes the Shopify fields on the contact and creates a Self Serve deal per new order, and every property has one writer, so the CMA writes nothing back for a store's customers. The match itself stays, because indirect sales need each order's CRM contact; it is kept in the CMA's own database instead of in HubSpot's contact properties.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.commerce_customer.crm_connection_id`, `crm_contact_source_id`, `matched_at` | the CRM connection and the contact id the customer was matched to, and when; all three set or all three null (a check); a foreign key keeps the connection in the customer's tenant; a contact id is 1 to 100 characters without outer spaces; a partial index on `(tenant_id, crm_connection_id, crm_contact_source_id)` finds a contact's customers |
| `cma.set_commerce_customer_contact(store connection, customer id, CRM connection, contact id)` | the ingest write path (`ingest.write`): links a store's customer to a contact of an active CRM connection of the same tenant (kind `crm`, or unset with only a CRM's rows, 0008e), or clears the link with a null contact; answers `linked`, `relinked`, `unchanged` (nothing written, `matched_at` stays the time of the match), `cleared`, `unlinked` (nothing to clear) or `unknown` (a clear for a customer the store does not hold, so a deletion the CMA never saw does not fail); an unknown customer for a link or an inactive CRM connection `CMA02`, another kind of connection, the store itself or a contact id over 100 characters `CMA04` |
| `cma.forget_commerce_contact_links(CRM connection, contact id)` | the CRM's privacy deletion of a contact (`ingest.write`): clears every customer link to it in the tenant and answers how many; the ingest service calls it next to `cma.ingest_contact_forget` |

No other change: 0007b's tables, upserts and reporting views (the `cma_read.commerce_customer` view leaves the link out), 0007c's outbox and 0008g's hash stay as they are; the 0007b upserts leave the link alone, a customer's deletion included (the ingest service clears it with the deletion). A contact id is an identifier, not personal data in itself; no email, name or number is stored. The ingest service sets the link (`docs/ingest/README.md`, section Shopify): exactly one contact links; none or several clear; a linked contact the CRM deleted or merged into another is found by one batch read at the customer's next read, and the customer is matched again; a customer's deletion clears its link. The verify (`59`) proves structure and privileges (the migration row; the columns, both checks, the foreign key and the index; both functions the application's only and in the caller's rights; nothing else of 0007b, 0007c or 0008g changed) and, on throwaway tenants: a link set with its time, the same link again writing nothing, a relink to another contact and connection (a CRM connection known by its rows), a clear, clearing again and an unknown customer's clear changing nothing, seven refusals with nothing written (an unknown customer, another store's customer, an inactive CRM connection, the store itself even with its kind forced to `crm`, a telephony connection, a connection without kind or rows, an over-long id), the table refusing half a link and another tenant's connection, the upserts of 0007b and 0008g leaving the link alone (a newer read and a deletion), the privacy forget clearing that contact's links only (in every store, another tenant's untouched), permissions and tenant isolation: 13 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: inbound service and callbacks (addition 0008i)

Written on 14 October 2026 (`60_inbound_service.sql`, `61_verify_inbound_service.sql`; brief B15, backlog CMA-056 and CMA-057). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `60` twice; `61` 20 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; thirty-one mutations of `60` run against `61`, thirty caught and one equivalent (after review round 1, which made the times missed count from the latest callback); `04` to `59` unchanged afterwards apart from the listings of `04`, `07`, `10` and `14` (`docs/b15-inbound-service/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 14 October 2026 per `docs/b15-inbound-service/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008i-dev-2026-10-14`, `records/0008i-prod-2026-10-14`). In dev `61` 20 PASS, provoked all 20 FAIL, and `35`, `41`, `53` and `55` PASS; in prod `35` and `41` PASS and a read-only check of the functions on the real calls. It needs 0008f.

Why (Decision log, 14 October 2026): Kira steers on the inbound service level and on missed calls that wait for a callback (KPIs 9), and Aircall already reports per call why it was missed. The calls of 0007a hold everything needed: direction, start, answer and end, the missed reason, the line, and the caller only as the keyed hash. Matching a missed call to a later call to or from the same caller needs that hash; Yordi agreed to it as its third purpose (reported by Martin). No new personal data is stored.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.telephony_missed_reason` | one row per tenant, source system (the telephony connection's adapter, a data value) and missed-reason code: label, `counts_in_service_level`, `needs_callback`; row-level security, audit, `tenant.configure` to write (through the function below), never deleted. A code without a row counts with the defaults (the code as label, both true), so a new vendor code never disappears; an unanswered inbound call without a reason is the code `unknown`. Pulse4all's rows are seed data in `02` (source system `aircall`, every reason needing a callback; out of the service level: `out_of_opening_hours` and `short_abandoned`), to confirm with Kira |
| settings | `telephony.service_level_seconds` (20), `telephony.callback_lookback_days` (3), `telephony.waiting_max_minutes` (30: an unanswered call that has not ended is waiting for at most this long, which guards against a lost end event); Pulse4all's values set in `02`; a negative value counts as 0 |
| `cma.inbound_service(from, to, group)` | per day, market, line or hour of the business dates (each call's date and hour in its line's market zone, else the tenant's): offered (inbound calls on counted lines), answered, unanswered, waiting, answered within N seconds, the service level's denominator and the service level, the average and 80th percentile time to answer (`answered_at − started_at`, menu and ringing included, as Aircall), the unanswered calls per reason code; then a total row over every call (group key null), computed rather than summed; at most 92 days; `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.inbound_today()` | the same measures for today in the tenant's zone, plus `waiting_now` and `longest_waiting_seconds` (inbound, not answered, not ended, started within `waiting_max_minutes`) and the two settings; `monitoring.live` |
| `cma.missed_callbacks()` | one row per caller (`counterpart_hash`, read inside the function and never returned) whose latest missed inbound call with a reason that needs a callback lies within the lookback and has no later outbound call to that hash (connected or not) and no later answered inbound call from it, on any telephony connection of the tenant: the call's id and connection, line, line name, market, reason code and label, start, `missed_count` (the caller's missed calls in the lookback since their latest callback before the listed call; all of them without one), `waiting_seconds` and `waiting_business_seconds` (`cma.business_seconds_of` to now; null for a line without a market); oldest first; `monitoring.live` |
| `cma.missed_callbacks_summary()` | the callers listed, `anonymous_count` (missed calls without a hash in the lookback that need a callback: counted, never listed) and the lookback; `monitoring.live` |
| `cma.missed_reasons()` | the tenant's catalog, for the screens that label reasons; any of `reports.view`, `performance.team`, `monitoring.live`, `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.connection_missed_reasons(connection)`, `cma.set_telephony_missed_reason(connection, code, label, in service level, needs callback)` | the codes a telephony connection has seen on its unanswered inbound calls plus its adapter's catalog rows, with the label and switches in force, the calls seen and the last one, and the connection's newest call id for the record link preview; one code's label and switches, refused for a connection of another kind (by its kind, or by its rows while the kind is unset; `CMA04`); `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.inbound_calls_of()`, `inbound_service_of()`, `inbound_today_of()`, `missed_callbacks_of()`, `missed_callbacks_summary_of()` | the reads of an explicit tenant, as in 0007d, called by the functions above in the caller's rights |

Everything is `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by the application only; row-level security keeps every read to the current tenant. Definitions: inbound is direction inbound, not deleted, with a start, on a counted line (0008f stores no other; a line not yet in the catalog counts); waiting has no outcome yet; unanswered is not answered and not waiting; the service level is answered within N seconds (at most N, so a call at exactly N is within) over the answered calls and the unanswered calls whose reason counts. The reporting view `cma_read.telephony_missed_reason` shows the catalog; the measures have no reader view. The verify (`61`) proves structure and privileges and, on three throwaway tenants: the service level with and without an excluded reason and a call at exactly N seconds; the average, the 80th percentile and the total row; hours and dates across the DST change of 25 October 2026 in each line's zone; a line without a market in the tenant's zone; an unknown code with the defaults and a missing reason as unknown; a deleted call never counted; a call waiting and a lost one left out of waiting; a caller missed twice as one row with `missed_count` 2; called back by an outbound attempt on another connection and by an answered inbound call; an earlier outbound call not counting; a later missed call calling nobody back; the lookback and a longer one; a reason without a callback; anonymous calls counted and not listed; another tenant's same hash string not matching, also in the owner's rights; the hash in no answer of any function; the catalog written by a configuring person only, with nine refusals; the reads' permissions and tenant isolation; the times missed counted since the latest callback (missed, called back, missed again counts 1): 20 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: calls overview and calls per agent (addition 0008j)

Written on 14 October 2026 (`62_calls_overview.sql`, `63_verify_calls_overview.sql`; brief B16, backlog CMA-055 and CMA-044). Amended in place on 14 October 2026 after the review of PR #126, before it ran anywhere: the previous period like for like (below). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `62` twice; `63` 19 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; thirty-nine mutations of `62` run against `63`, all caught; `35`, `41`, `55` and `61` PASS afterwards (`docs/b16-calls-overview/local-records/`). `61`'s check A3 listed every `telephony.%` setting, so it failed once `62` adds a fourth; it now names its own three (`61` runs in dev only and never ran in prod, so it is amended in place, as `57` was for 0008h). Live in prod at `70354b1` (brief B16.1, 14 October 2026, from Martin; run per `docs/b16-calls-overview/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`, its records follow from his report). It needs 0008i.

Why (Decision log, 14 October 2026): Kira and the supervisors steer on calls in and out, how many became a conversation, talk time and tagging (KPIs 7 and 8), and read them today in Aircall's General Performance, Outbound and User Activity views. The calls of 0007a hold everything needed: direction, start, answer and end, the telephony user, the line and the current tags; the linked accounts of 0008e say which person a telephony user is. Nothing personal is added and nothing is written.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| setting `telephony.connected_min_seconds` | 10: an outbound call with at least this many seconds of talk time is connected; a negative value counts as 0. Pulse4all's value 10 is set in `02`, to confirm with Kira against Aircall's own "x sec" setting for a connected call. Edited on Configuration → Exports, Other settings; the telephony connection's Sources page states it in words with a link there |
| `cma.calls_overview(from, to, market, line, group)` | per day, ISO week (its Monday), market or line of the business dates (each call's date in its line's market zone, else the tenant's): calls, inbound, outbound, answered inbound, connected outbound and its share, conversations, their total and average talk time, the connected outbound calls' own talk time and average, untagged conversations; then the total over every call (`period` `current`, group key null; computed, not summed), then the same total over the previous period, like for like (`period` `previous`, below); at most 92 days; `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.calls_tag_use(from, to, market, line, group)` | per group and per current tag (any tag, counted or not, with the catalog's name and `is_counted`; tag key connection id ':' tag ref) the conversations carrying it, and per group the untagged ones (tag key null), the most used tag first; then the same over the whole period (group key null); `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.calls_per_person(from, to, market, line)` | per person (the person holding `<adapter>_user` with the call's telephony user id, 0008e), sorted by name: answered inbound with their total and average talk time, outbound calls, connected outbound with their total and average talk time, conversations, untagged conversations, and the earliest and latest time of day of a call of theirs on any day of the period (the line's market zone); then one row `not_linked` for the calls of telephony users without a person and of calls without a user, with how many such users there are, never who; then the total; `performance.team` |
| `cma.calls_per_person_tags(from, to, market, line)` | per person (and the not-linked calls) and per counted tag the conversations carrying it, then their untagged conversations; a tag that does not count is left out and still makes its conversation tagged; `performance.team` |
| `cma.calls_filters_check(market, line)` | the filters every read takes: a market is a two-letter code or `unknown`, a line a connection id ':' line ref, as the line groups; anything else `CMA04` |
| `cma.calls_of()`, `calls_figures_of()`, `calls_overview_of()`, `calls_tag_use_of()`, `calls_people_of()`, `calls_per_person_of()`, `calls_per_person_tags_of()` | the reads of an explicit tenant, as in 0007d and 0008i, called by the functions above in the caller's rights; `cma.calls_of` holds every definition once |

Definitions (one place, `cma.calls_of`): a call is a telephony call, not deleted, with a start, on a counted line (0008f stores no other; a line not yet in the catalog counts), either direction; talk time is ended − answered in whole seconds (`telephony_call.talk_seconds`), without the ringing that Aircall's duration holds, so these figures differ from Aircall's on purpose; a connected outbound call has talk time of at least the threshold (at least, so a call at exactly the threshold is connected); a conversation is an answered inbound or a connected outbound call; untagged is a conversation without any current tag, counted or not (the rule of CMA-036); a person's calls are their answered inbound and their outbound calls; the market and line filters narrow every read before anything is counted.

The previous period, like for like (Decision log, 14 October 2026): it starts as many dates before `from` as the period holds (`len` = to − from + 1). A period that ended before today, in the tenant's zone, compares with the whole previous equal period, `[from − len, from − 1]`. A period that reaches today compares with the same elapsed span, its `least(to, today) − from + 1` dates: `[from − len, least(to, today) − len]`, and on that span's last date only the calls whose time of day is at most the current time of day in their own zone (each line's market zone). A period that starts after today has no elapsed date and compares with nothing. The current period's figures are never cut. `cma.calls_overview_of` takes the instant as `p_now` (now() for the application; the verify gives fixed instants) and passes the cut-off to `cma.calls_figures_of`; Reports → Calls names what the deltas compare with: "the previous period", or "the same point in the previous period" while the period runs, its dates in the title. Since addition 0008k a previous span that starts before the tenant's telephony history shows no change at all (Data model: telephony history start).

Everything is `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by the application only; row-level security keeps every read to the current tenant; no function returns a call id, the hash or a number, and the application's reads return no telephony user id. The measures have no reader view. The verify (`63`) proves structure and privileges and, on three throwaway tenants (the third holds calls placed relative to today, for the application's read at the real time): talk time without ringing and a call at exactly the threshold (connected at 10 seconds, and with the threshold at 9 and at 11); a call on a line that does not count never counted (skipped by the write, and one written past it); the untagged rule with a counted, an uncounted and a removed tag; an uncounted tag left out of the tags per person and still making its call tagged; the market and line filters narrowing all four reads alike (six combinations); tag use by market; the previous period's totals over 19 to 25 October, across the DST change, and the days and ISO weeks of both weeks in each line's zone; the per-person rows through linked accounts, by name, an id of another adapter not linking, two users without a person as one row with their count, each user once; the permissions (`performance.team` for both per-person reads, `reports.view` enough for the overview and the tag use) with eighteen refusals; tenant isolation with equal refs, also in the owner's rights; no hash, line digits, call id or telephony user id in any answer; the overview per market with its computed total and the previous period, a deleted call never counted; like for like: a period that reaches today compared with the same elapsed dates (measured on its third day, its first day and before it starts, and through the application's read at the real time), on the previous span's last date a call later than the current time of day in its own zone left out (Amsterdam at the second, London by its own clock), and a period that ended compared with the whole previous equal period whatever the time of day: 19 checks. The previous period's checks read `cma.calls_overview_of` at fixed instants, so the outcome does not depend on the day it runs. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: telephony history start (addition 0008k)

Written on 14 October 2026 (`64_calls_history_start.sql`, `65_verify_calls_history_start.sql`; brief B16.1, a follow-up to B16). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `64` twice (`INSERT 0 1`, then `INSERT 0 0`); `65` 11 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; fourteen mutations of `64` run against `65`, thirteen caught and one equivalent; `63`, `61`, `55`, `41` and `35` unchanged, and every verify from `04` to `63` alike before and after `64` apart from the listings of `07` and `10` (`docs/b16-1-history-start/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 14 October 2026 (`docs/b16-1-history-start/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`; `records/0008k-dev-2026-10-14`, `records/0008k-prod-2026-10-14`). It needs 0008j; 0008j's functions are unchanged (`62` has run in prod).

Why (Decision log, 14 October 2026): prod's calls start on 1 October 2026, so This month (1 to 14 October) compared with 31 August to 13 September, a span without data, and the tiles read "↑ 358,900 %" and "↑ from none". The same happens for any period whose previous span starts before the first stored call.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.telephony_history_start()` | the business date of the tenant's earliest stored call, or null without one; `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.telephony_history_start_of(tenant)` | the read of an explicit tenant, as in 0008j, called by the function above in the caller's rights |

Definition: a call as `cma.calls_of` defines it (not deleted, with a start, on a counted line; a line not yet in the catalog counts), on any connection of the tenant, either direction; its business date in its line's market zone, else the tenant's, as `cma.calls_of` gives it. Tenant-wide: the Calls report's market and line filters do not narrow it. The rule (`previousBeforeHistory` and `callsChange` in `web/src/lib/reports.ts`): when the previous span, as `cma.calls_overview_of` computes it like for like, starts before the history start, every tile on Reports → Calls says "No history to compare yet" in Muted text, the history's first date in its title, with no percentage and no "from none"; a span that starts on the history's first date or later shows the change as before; a tenant without calls (null) shows the changes as before.

How it is read: `cma.calls_of` holds the definition of a call; read without bounds it scans every call of the tenant, because its optional bounds (`p is null or started_at < p`) keep the index on the tenant and the start out of use (0008j's overview reads that way: 1.6 seconds for a month over 300,000 calls locally). `cma.telephony_history_start_of` therefore reads `cma.calls_of` in windows of seven days from the earliest stored row with a start, each bound a value of its own so it reaches the read as a constant: a window without a call (only deleted calls, or calls on a line that does not count) moves on to the next stored row after it, so the first window with a call holds the earliest call by its start; the earliest business date is then among the calls that start up to two days after it (zones differ by at most 26 hours, so a call two days later cannot fall on an earlier date). Locally, over 300,000 calls: 17 to 22 ms, against 420 to 470 ms for one unbounded read of `cma.calls_of`; 51 to 61 ms with 40,000 deleted calls and calls on a line that does not count in the 140 days before (`docs/b16-1-history-start/local-records/db/scale.txt`).

Both functions are `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by the application only; row-level security keeps every read to the current tenant. They return a date, nothing personal. The verify (`65`) proves structure and privileges and, on three throwaway tenants: the permissions (`reports.view`, `performance.team`) with three refusals (an agent, the Ingest user, no acting user); null for a tenant without a call, and still null with only a deleted call, a call on a line that does not count and a call without a start; the earliest business date in its line's zone (a US line in Los Angeles puts a later call on 1 October, before the NL call of 2 October in Amsterdam), moved to 2 October when that line's market moves to NL; a deleted earliest call left out (the history started on its date before the deletion); a call on a line that does not count left out (the write skipped one, another stored past it), and counted once the line counts; tenant isolation with equal refs, also in the owner's rights; the Calls overview holding no call in the seven days before the history start and one on its date; the first call that counts after two months of calls that do not; a later call on an earlier date up to two days after the earliest call and past the end of its window (lines at UTC+14 and UTC−12): 11 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: invitations (addition 0008l)

Written in the night of 14 to 15 October 2026 (`66_invitations.sql`, `67_verify_invitations.sql`; backlog CMA-018). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `66` twice (`INSERT 0 1`, then `INSERT 0 0`); `67` 23 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; nineteen mutations of `66` run against `67`, all caught; `63` and `65` unchanged, and every verify from `04` to `65` alike before and after `66` apart from the listings of `04`, `07`, `10` and `14` (the new table, the `0008l` row, the two settings) (`docs/night-2026-10-15/local-records/db/`). Run in dev and prod on 15 October 2026 (`docs/night-2026-10-15/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`; `records/0008l-dev-2026-10-15`, `records/0008l-prod-2026-10-15`). It needs 0008k.

Why (Decision log, 15 October 2026): a person who may add people invites someone by work email, with a role, instead of asking for their account id. The invitee opens the link, signs in, accepts, and is set up with the role, employer, teams and skills the inviter chose. Matching stays on the sign-in account id: the invitation binds that id at acceptance, only when the address the sign-in provider verified equals the invited one. Add a person by account id stays as it is. The CMA sends no email in this version: the inviter sends the link from their own mail client.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.invitation` | one row per invitation, never deleted (revoked, expired and accepted rows stay): address (lower case), display name, language (a copy locale, for the mail and the invitee's page), role, employer, team ids, skill ids with levels, `token_hash` (SHA-256 hex, unique across tenants), who created it and when, `expires_at`, `renewed_at` and `renewed_by` (the inviter of the current link), `accepted_at` and `accepted_user_id`, `revoked_at` and `revoked_by`. `setup_tenant_table` (row-level security, audit); the application inserts, may read every column but the hash, may update only the hash, the expiry, renewal and revocation, never deletes; one pending (neither accepted nor revoked) invitation per address and tenant (a partial unique index) |
| `auth.invitation_valid_days`, `auth.invitation_email_domains` | settings in the catalog: how many days a link works (default 7, 1 to 30), and the email domains invitations may go to (comma-separated, stored lower case without spaces; default empty: any domain, exact domain match). `cma.check_tenant_setting()` as in 0008b plus these two checks. No tenant-specific value in the migration; Pulse4all sets `pulse4all.com` for both tenants in seed `02` (15 October 2026, Decision log) |
| `cma.create_invitation(email, name, locale, role, employer, team keys, skills)` | the token once (32 bytes, 43 url-safe characters), only its hash kept; the same permission and role ceiling as Add a person (`users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all`, `cma.assert_role_assignable`), the system role refused (`CMA06`), the employer, teams and skills checked as Add a person and Edit person do (skills need `skills.manage`, levels from the scale); refused: an active or inactive person with the address (`CMA03`), a pending invitation for it (`CMA03`: renew or revoke it), an address outside the domain setting (`CMA04`) |
| `cma.renew_invitation(id)` | a new token for an open or expired invitation (the old hash replaced, the expiry from now), the caller recorded as the inviter of the link; the role must still be within the caller's ceiling and the address allowed; accepted or revoked `CMA03` |
| `cma.revoke_invitation(id)` | revokes an open or expired invitation within the caller's ceiling; a revoked one stays revoked, an accepted one is `CMA03` |
| `cma.invitations()` | the invitations the caller may see (`users.manage_all` every one, `users.manage_agents` those for a role it may grant, as the directory hides managing people), with teams, skills and a computed state (`open`, `expired`, `accepted`, `revoked`) and who created, accepted or revoked them; never the hash |
| `cma.invitation_preview(token, system, id, email)` | what the link shows, read only (`stable`; a mail scanner may open it): `invalid` (unknown, revoked or a tenant switched off: nothing else), `already_member` (the sign-in id is linked in that tenant), `invalid` (accepted), `other_tenant` (the sign-in already works in another tenant: two tenants for one sign-in is "no access yet" until a tenant picker exists, so it is not accepted with this account), `expired` (the tenant's name), `wrong_account` (the invited address, to its link holder), else `open` with the tenant's name, the role's name and the language |
| `cma.accept_invitation(token, system, id, email)` | checked in the preview's order; then, in the invitation's tenant only and as the inviter of the current link (`app.actor_label` `invitation`), `cma.add_person()` (user, sign-in id under the given system, role), `cma.set_person_teams()` (the teams that still exist) and `cma.set_person_skills()` (the skills still in the catalog and scale), and the invitation marked accepted; the inviter's permission and ceiling, the role (never the system role: a function in the owner's rights passes `protect_system`, so it is checked here), the employer and the address are checked again, and a person with the address added meanwhile makes it `invalid` (never a match on an address). Any refusal is `invalid` and writes nothing; the caller's context is put back. Answers the result with the tenant and the person's id only |

The token is the SHA-256 of three `gen_random_uuid()` values (366 random bits from the server's strong random source): `gen_random_bytes()` needs the `pgcrypto` extension, which an IAM login acting as `cma_owner` cannot create on Cloud SQL (Decision log, 15 October 2026). The invitee's two functions are, in schema `cma`, the fourth and fifth `SECURITY DEFINER` functions after `cma.find_tenants_for_identity`, `cma.ingest_connection` (0006) and `cma.remove_calls_of_uncounted_line` (0008f), with the search path pinned to `pg_catalog, cma, pg_temp` (the temporary schema last, so nothing temporary can stand in for a name): they find the invitation by the token's hash and act on its tenant only; the system and the sign-in id are parameters (`google` behind IAP, `mock` in dev), as the login lookup and `add_person` take them. The verify (`67`) proves structure and privileges and, on two throwaway tenants: create (the token once, its hash stored and not the token, seven days, the list without either); one invitation per assignable role of the default ladder, each accepted with that role and employer; the system role refused at creation and at acceptance; the ceiling of `users.manage_agents` (agent, supervisor and analytics; manager and admin `CMA06`; managing roles' invitations hidden, not renewed, not revoked) and the refusals of an agent and of no acting user; every refusal at creation writing nothing; the domain setting at creation and acceptance; the settings' validation and the expiry following the days; renew (the old link invalid, the renewer the inviter of the link, an expired invitation open again, accepted or revoked refused); revoke (idempotent, the rows kept with who and when); acceptance with teams and skills (a dissolved team left out), the audit naming the inviter with the label `invitation`, the login lookup finding the tenant; expired; wrong account (the address compared without case); reuse (the person it made is `already_member`, anyone else `invalid`, also when that person's address changed); a sign-in id linked in the tenant, and one that works in another tenant (`other_tenant`, nothing written; free once its person there is inactive); another tenant's context (the person made in the invitation's tenant only, the context put back, the other tenant's rows hidden); the hash unreadable and unmatched by the application and absent from every audit row; the preview changing nothing; the inviter deactivated or lowered and a person with the address added meanwhile, each refusing the acceptance; malformed input: 23 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: email activity (addition 0008m)

Written on 15 October 2026 (`68_email_activity.sql`, `69_verify_email_activity.sql`; brief B17a, the first of the five increments of brief B17, email and forms reporting) and amended in place the same day, before it ran anywhere, with the database half of brief B17b (contacts' country with fallback slots and its source, contacts held for emails and threads, the market chain with the form step, the check `contact_country_fallback`), as 0008e and 0008j were amended (Decision log, 15 October 2026). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `68` twice (`INSERT 0 1`, then `INSERT 0 0`); `69` 29 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; sixty-eight mutations of `68` run against `69`, all caught; `33`, `35`, `41` and `53` unchanged, and every verify from `04` to `67` alike before and after `68` apart from the listings of `04`, `07`, `10` and `14` (the six tables, the added view, the `0008m` row, the four settings) (`docs/b17a-email-data/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 15 October 2026 (`docs/b17a-email-data/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`; `records/0008m-dev-2026-10-15`, `records/0008m-prod-2026-10-15`, from Martin's report in `Pulse4all-com/cma#138`; prod at 12:42 UTC). It needs 0008l. The email poll of brief B17b fills the email tables, every minute, through the functions below (`docs/ingest/README.md`, section Email; the job `ingest-poll-email`, for a connection whose settings hold `email_poll` true).

Why (Decision log, 15 October 2026): managers and analytics (Arno, Kira, Bas) read email, inbox and form figures in HubSpot's reports today; the CMA joins them with who was on the clock, the market and the team, inside the role checks (the reports come with B17c to B17e). This addition is the data model under those reports: metadata only, the two email sources kept apart, no actor id of anyone who is not an agent.

Why the contacts' part (brief B17b, Martin, 15 October 2026): in HubSpot's Subscriptions portal, of the 36,498 contacts created since 1 January 2026, `contact_country` (the property the CMA maps) is filled on 1, `country_dropdown` on 34,690 (labels such as "United Kingdom"), the free-text `country` adds 1 and `ip_country` 31; about 1,800 have none. Martin's rule: the market comes from the contact's country fields or the form the contact used; once `contact_country` is filled ("all set"), only `contact_country`. Out of scope: `ip_country`, `aed__country_located`, `country` (free text), `country_text_mapping`.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.connection_inbox` | per connection the inboxes of the CRM's conversations (a shared mailbox or a team inbox): name from the source; `is_counted` (default true), `market` (a catalog market, by foreign key), the work type skill and the tenant's `channel` as configuration; refreshed like forms and lines (the configuration kept, an inbox the source dropped archived); a new inbox takes the tenant's active channel with the key `email` |
| `cma.connection_inbox_channel` | per connection the source's channels (email, chat, a messenger): name and the adapter's canonical `kind`; the tenant's `channel` and `is_counted` as configuration. A new channel takes the tenant's active channel whose key equals its kind and counts only when its kind is `email`, so chat or WhatsApp threads become channels of their own later without code |
| `cma.connection_email_rule` | per connection, in `sort_order`, classification rules: `applies_to` (`sender_domain` or `subject`), `words` (1 to 20) and `exclude_words` (at most 20), lower case, trimmed, each once, and the `category` code they give (lower-case letters, digits, `_`). The first active rule whose words the value of its kind contains, and none of its exclude words, gives the category, case-insensitive: the mechanism of 0008e's rules by name pattern (`src/lib/sources.ts`), stored per rule. Never deleted: `status` active or inactive. The value matched is never stored |
| `cma.email_message` | one row per email of either source, unique on connection, `source_kind` and source id. `crm_email`: the CRM's email engagements (sent from the CRM, logged by BCC, a reply to a logged email); `inbox_message`: a message of an inbox conversation. The thread ref (required for an inbox message; for an engagement the CRM's own thread id when the adapter maps one), inbox and source channel refs, `direction` (`out`, `in`, `forwarded`), `status` (`sent`, `bounced`, `failed`, `scheduled`, `other`), `occurred_at`, `actor_kind`, `owner_ref` (an agent's CRM owner id only), `category`, the contact id, the source's created, updated and deleted times, `synced_at`, raw (filtered). The stale guard is the source's updated time (as 0006); a deletion keeps ids, times and the metadata and clears raw, and a newer read brings the email back |
| `cma.email_thread` | one row per inbox conversation: inbox and source channel refs, `status` (`open`, `closed`), `source_created_at`, `closed_at`, `owner_ref` (the current assignee's CRM owner id, null for anyone who is not an agent), the contact and ticket ids (from the thread's associations), `is_spam`, `is_archived`, `category`, the latest received and sent times, the adapter's version as the stale guard, `forgotten_at`, raw. A closed thread keeps the source's close time; a reopened one has none |
| `cma.email_thread_event` | one row per source message of the system kinds: `assigned` (`to_actor_kind` and, for an agent, `to_owner_ref`; no assignee is an unassignment), `closed`, `reopened`, `moved_inbox` (from and to inbox refs), with `occurred_at`, `actor_kind` and the acting agent's `owner_ref`; insert-only (the application cannot update a row), idempotent on the source message id |
| settings | `email.start_date` (text, default empty: `intake.start_date`; else a date), `email.reply_target_minutes` (240 business minutes, 1 to 10080), `email.no_response_days` (5, 1 to 90), `email.market_order` (`contact,form,record,inbox`: one to four of the four steps, each once, lower case, comma-separated without spaces; anything else refused, nothing rewritten); `cma.check_tenant_setting()` replaced with these four checks (0008l's branches unchanged) |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_inboxes()`, `cma.ingest_upsert_inbox_channels()` | the catalog refreshes (`ingest.write`, at most 500), answering the active count |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_emails()`, `cma.ingest_upsert_threads()`, `cma.ingest_upsert_thread_events()` | the activity writes (`ingest.write`, at most 500): inserted, updated, stale, deleted and unknown (emails); inserted, updated, stale and forgotten (threads); inserted and known (events). A later email read without a thread, inbox, channel, category or contact keeps the stored one (as 0006 keeps a record's contact) |
| `cma.ingest_thread_marks(connection, thread ids)` | per held thread (at most 500 ids) the time of its latest stored inbox message or event, their counts, the stored version and whether it was forgotten: where the poll resumes a changed thread's messages (B17b) |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_associations()` | replaced: as in 0007, and the object type `crm_email` is the activity side as `crm_call` is (an association from a contact or a record to an email is turned around); `crm_association`'s check names both |
| `sync_run.job` | gains `email_poll`, the email poll's runs (B17b) |
| `cma.set_inbox(connection, inbox, counted, market, work type key, channel key)`, `cma.set_inbox_channel(connection, channel, channel key, counted)`, `cma.set_email_rule(connection, rule or null, applies to, words, exclude words, category, order)`, `cma.set_email_rule_status(rule, status)` | configuration (`tenant.configure`): an unknown market, work type, channel or rule `CMA02`; a missing counted, a rule without words or with more than 20, a word with a comma, semicolon or line break, a malformed category, an unknown `applies_to` or status `CMA04`. An inbox or channel the catalog has not brought yet is added with its id as name, as `set_form` does |
| `cma.connection_config()` | replaced: as in 0007c, plus `inboxes`, `inboxChannels` and `emailRules` (in their order), for the adapter and the Sources screen |
| `cma.connection_held_kinds_of()` | replaced: as in 0008e, and email rows (inboxes, channels, rules, emails, threads) imply the kind `crm`. Email activity and its configuration go only to a CRM connection: kind `crm`, or unset without rows of another kind (else `CMA04`) |
| `connection_field` country slots, `cma.set_connection_field()` | the canonical field `country` on contacts takes ordered slots 1 to 9, as refs do: slot 1 the property mapped until now, a slot above 1 a fallback property. `cma.set_connection_field()` keeps both signatures and checks, for the country: slot n needs slot n − 1 and a slot goes only from the end (a gap `CMA04`), and no property feeds two country slots of a connection (`CMA03`); a slot above 1 on any other field than the country and refs stays refused (`CMA04`) |
| `cma.crm_contact.country_source` | the slot that supplied the stored country, null when there is none (a check keeps the two together). A contact stored before 0008m reads 1, the only slot there was (`68` sets it). The slot, not the property name: the property is read through `connection_field`. `cma_read.crm_contact` gains it as its last column |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_contacts()`, `cma.contact_country_pick(values)` | replaced: as in 0007, and the item takes `country` (one value, slot 1, as before) or `countries` (the values per slot, at most 9, each a string or null), not both. The database chooses: the first slot whose value `cma.normalize_market` turns into a catalog market wins; if none does, the first non-empty value as written (as 0007 stores it, so `market_not_in_catalog` still lists it); all empty is none. A contact is taken in while a deal or ticket, an email or an inbox thread of the same connection points to it (not held otherwise); nothing releases one |
| `cma.ingest_contact_forget()` | replaced: as in 0007a, and the contact is also cleared on the connection's emails and threads; the answer gains `emails` and `threads` when one was cleared and keeps its earlier shape otherwise |
| `cma.ingest_thread_forget(connection, thread)` | a conversation's privacy deletion (`ingest.write`): the thread and its inbox messages lose contact, ticket and raw and keep ids, times, status, direction and actor kinds for the counts; the thread is marked forgotten, so a later read of it changes nothing and a later message of it is stored without contact or raw; an engagement with the same thread ref is left alone; answers `{threads, messages}` |
| `cma.email_start_date_of(tenant)` | `email.start_date`, else `intake.start_date` |
