| `cma.email_market_of(tenant, connection, source kind, source id)` | the market of an email (`crm_email`, `inbox_message`) or a conversation (`inbox_thread`) by `email.market_order`, read at query time (as 0007d), with the step that gave it: `contact` (the held contact's stored country: a message's contact, else an engagement's earliest current contact association, else a message's thread's contact), `form` (the market of that contact's latest submission resolved to it, of a counted form with a market, submitted at or before the email's time or the thread's creation; none means the next step), `record` (an engagement's current associations, ticket before deal, the newest record first; a message's or a thread's ticket; deleted records left out), `inbox` (a message's inbox, else its thread's); each through the market aliases and only when it names a catalog market; `unknown` when every step is empty |
| `cma.email_category_of(tenant, connection, sender domain, subject)` | the rules' definition: the first active rule by its order, then its creation, whose words the value of its kind contains and none of its exclude words; null when none matches. The owner's only, so it is never called with a real subject: the ingest service applies the same rule in memory to the rules `connection_config` gives it (B17b) |
| `cma.data_quality_of()`, `cma.data_quality()`, `cma.data_quality_added_checks()` | the check `contact_country_fallback`: held contacts (not deleted) whose `country_source` is above 1, one row per contact with the slot as its code. `cma.data_quality_of()` is 0007d's plus this branch; `cma.data_quality_checks()` keeps 0007d's fifteen checks, because `41`, which has run in prod, pins them and their totals; `cma.data_quality()` adds the check from `cma.data_quality_added_checks()` once a contact connection maps a country slot above 1, or while it finds a contact, so it shows muted at zero on the Data page (its label is the database's description until the page has copy for it); a tenant without fallback slots keeps 0007d's fifteen rows exactly. Its reader view is `cma_read.dq_contact_country_fallback` (ids and codes only) |

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `connection_inbox`, `connection_inbox_channel`, `connection_email_rule`, `email_thread`, `email_message`, `email_thread_event`, each every column but raw (the verify pins the lists); source ids, owner ids and contact ids are opaque ids, as `crm_call` and `form_submission` show them; `crm_contact` gains `country_source` after its earlier columns, and `dq_contact_country_fallback` is new. Everything is `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by the application only (the category rule by the owner only); the application never deletes from the six tables and never updates a thread event.

**Metadata only.** Never stored: subject, body, text, rich text, attachments, email addresses, names, headers, recipients, senders. A sender's domain and a subject live in the ingest service's memory for the classification only (B17b), and only the category code is written; a rule added later does not reclassify earlier emails. A source's actor ids carry personal data for anyone who is not an agent, so only the actor's kind is stored (`agent`; `customer` for a visitor or an email address; `system`; `bot`; `ai` for an AI agent; `integration`; `unknown`), and for an agent the CRM owner id the ingest service resolves from the actor's user id; never a raw actor id. HubSpot's conversations spec (`PublicApiSpecs/Conversations/Conversations/Rollouts/54902/v3/conversations.json`, read on 15 October 2026) types the actors `AGENT`, `BOT`, `INTEGRATOR`, `SYSTEM`, `VISITOR`, `EMAIL` and `LLM`, which is why `ai` exists; it does not document the actor ids' prefixes (`A-`, `V-`, `E-`, `S-`, `B-`, `I-`); B17b confirmed `A-`, `V-` and `S-` in prod on 16 October 2026 (`A` 11, `V` 66 and `S` 1 actors in 200 threads; `records/b17b-prod-2026-10-16`), and `E-`, `B-` and `I-` are not seen yet. The emails spec (`PublicApiSpecs/CRM/Emails/Rollouts/424/2026-09/emails.json`) asks both `crm.objects.contacts.read` and `sales-email-read` for the batch read and the search, and names no email property; B17b reads the property names from the properties API in dev. The second line in the database: every ref column refuses an `@` and white space; an owner ref needs the actor kind `agent`; the raw payload keeps a flat object of at most 50 keys whose names do not name content, people or addresses and whose values are numbers, booleans or strings of at most 200 characters without an `@` and not like a phone number. The verify checks the column names of the new tables and views, and that no row or audit image holds its invented subject, body, names or addresses.

**Two sources apart.** An email engagement and an inbox message of the same email can both exist (HubSpot may log an inbox reply as an engagement too); they stay two `source_kind`s, and no report adds them into one total. B17b measures the overlap in dev (Open decisions).

**For the reports (B17c).** Inboxes and channels that do not count, and spam threads, stay stored and are left out of the reports; how an inbox or a channel the catalog has not brought yet counts is B17c's definition. The market is read at query time. Per-person figures match `owner_ref` through the linked accounts of 0008e (`<adapter>_owner`) and never show an owner id. Retention: email metadata follows the tenant's retention for call data, 1 year, inside backlog CMA-003's retention job, which does not exist yet (Decision log; Open decisions).

**Data prerequisites and effects.** A contact is held for its emails and threads as soon as B17b writes them, so its country is mirrored then too (the thin record of 0006: country, language, currency, store, refs; Yordi agreed, Decision log, 15 October 2026). No country slot is configured in this addition or in `02`: Pulse4all's slot 2 (`country_dropdown`) is configuration, set in dev and prod by B17b's runbooks (`docs/b17b-email-ingest/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` step 3, `docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 10.3), and B17b's ingest reads every slot. Effect to expect then: 0007d's deal market falls back to the primary contact's country, so speed to lead and intake show fewer unknown markets once contacts are read again (Open decisions: tell Kira). When `contact_country` is filled on every contact ("all set", a HubSpot workflow, KPIs 11), slot 2 is removed by configuration. Inbox conversations need a connected inbox in the portal and the scopes of B17b (`conversations.read`, `sales-email-read`); `02` sets no email value, so Pulse4all runs on the defaults until Arno and Kira confirm them (Open decisions).

The verify (`69`) proves structure and privileges (7 checks: the addition after 0008l; the six tables with row-level security, the tenant policy, audit, no delete, insert-only events and a reader view each; the views' exact columns without raw; no column named like subject, body, text, html, header, attachment, email address, sender or recipient; the four settings with their defaults; every function the application's only and in the caller's rights, the category rule the owner's only; the country slot rule, `country_source` with its check, `cma_read.crm_contact` with it last, 0007d's fifteen checks unchanged and the added check with its reader view) and, on three throwaway tenants (22 checks): the settings' checks and thirteen refusals (the market order with an unknown step, a step twice, spaces, upper case); inbox and channel refreshes keeping the configuration; the stale guard on emails and threads; idempotent thread events; the market by each step (contact, form, record, inbox, unknown), a later submission, an uncounted form and a form without a market left out, the order changed, a removed association, a deleted record and an alias added later; rule ordering; the contact's and a conversation's privacy deletions; tenant isolation; the Ingest user unable to configure and a configuring person unable to ingest; the raw payload filtered and refs with an `@` refused; the connection's kind; email associations; `connection_config`; the thread marks; an `email_poll` run; contacts held for an email and a thread, forgotten by the privacy deletion and left by a conversation's; the country slots (contiguous, no property twice, removed from the end, six refusals); the country chosen from its slots with its source in each case; a single country exactly as before; the fallback check listed with slot 2, firing once and clearing, and a tenant without fallback slots keeping fifteen rows; tenant isolation for contacts, slots and the check: 29 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: CRM tasks (addition 0008n)

Written in the night of 15 to 16 October 2026 (`70_crm_tasks.sql`, `71_verify_crm_tasks.sql`; night run N16-B, backlog CMA-007 and CMA-015; Decision log, 16 October 2026). The CRM's tasks reach the Workspace as rows without their text: `cma.crm_task` (tenant-aware through `cma.setup_tenant_table`: row-level security, audit, no delete for the application) holds per task the connection, the source id, the owner's id in the source (`owner_ref`), the status, type and priority as the source's references, the due and completion times, the contact, deal and ticket it points to by id, the source's created, updated and deleted times, and a filtered `raw`. **Never body or subject:** `cma.task_raw()` keeps flat scalars only and drops `hs_task_body`, `hs_task_subject`, any key naming body, subject, note, text, description or html, the property names the connection's settings `task_body_property` and `task_subject_property` give, a string holding an `@` and a string over 200 characters; a check on the column (`cma.task_raw_ok`) refuses any key of that kind on any path, so no write, not even the owner's, can store one. Two catalogs per connection fill themselves from the rows and are configured on Sources: `cma.connection_task_status` (`status_ref`, `label`, `is_done`, default false: an unknown status is open) and `cma.connection_task_type` (`type_ref`, `label`, `is_callback`, default false, `is_counted`, default true), with `cma.set_task_status()` and `cma.set_task_type()` under `tenant.configure` and `cma.connection_task_catalogs()` for the Sources page (each status's and type's task counts, the open ones, the newest task id for the link preview). `cma.ingest_upsert_tasks(connection, tasks jsonb)` (modelled on `ingest_upsert_records`: at most 500, the stale guard on `updatedAt`, outcomes `inserted`, `updated`, `stale`, `deleted`, `unknown`) takes the associations as the read gives them, so a later read without the deal clears it. A contact a task points to is held (0008m's rule, extended), and the contact's privacy deletion (`cma.ingest_contact_forget`) clears the task's contact and answers how many. Task rows make a connection's kind `crm` (`cma.connection_held_kinds_of`). Reader views `cma_read.crm_task` (without `raw`), `connection_task_status` and `connection_task_type`. The ingest service reads tasks from HubSpot (`ingest/src/adapters/hubspot/`: eight properties, the associations to contacts, deals and tickets; webhooks on creation, deletion, the eight property changes and association changes, 46 subscriptions in the project template; the nightly reconcile and the backfill know the object `task`; the store writes tasks after records); the unmapped owner is stored and shown as Not mapped. Task alert rules are not in this addition (Open decisions). The verify (`71`, dev only) proves structure and privileges (6 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (14 checks: the catalogs seeded with the defaults, update and stale, the strip and the check on raw, a person's labels and switches surviving a read, associations as given, deletion and return, a contact held for a task alone, the privacy deletion, tenant isolation, the configuration and upsert refusals, the Sources read, task rows making a connection crm, a reader scoped to one tenant); 20 PASS, provoked 20 FAIL, locally on PostgreSQL 16 (`docs/night-2026-10-16/local-records/db/`).

### Data model: follow-up (addition 0008o)

Written in the night of 15 to 16 October 2026 (`72_follow_up.sql`, `73_verify_follow_up.sql`; night run N16-B, backlog CMA-036, CMA-045, CMA-046, CMA-050, CMA-052; Decision log, 16 October 2026): what is waiting right now, for Reports → Follow-up, Welcome's Needs attention, the Live board's tiles and the person's own Waiting for you (Features 4, 5 and 9). **Everything is tenant configuration:** the settings `tickets.sla_minutes` (240: a ticket is answered within this many business minutes of its market, KPI 9), `tickets.sla_warning_pct` (75: at risk from this share), `leads.age_buckets_business_days` (`1,2,5`: the edges of the age groups of leads not called, one to five increasing whole numbers) and `tasks.callback_window_minutes` (240, for KPI 5, not yet computed), validated by `cma.check_tenant_setting()` and seeded for Pulse4all in `02`; a ticket pipeline's own response time in `connection_pipeline.sla_minutes` (null: the tenant's default) through `cma.set_pipeline_sla()`; the lead target stays 0007d's `speed_to_lead.target_minutes`, never a second one. **The reads of an explicit tenant** (`cma.*_of(tenant)`, in the caller's rights) with the application's wrappers that check the permission and read the current tenant under row-level security: `open_leads_of` (0007d's not-called open deals with the owner as a person or not mapped, market, pipeline, created, business minutes waited, the age in business days of the market bucketed by the setting, past the target or not, the last linked call in either direction; `open_leads()` for `reports.view`, `performance.team` or `monitoring.live`), `open_tickets_of` (the open tickets of counted pipelines with pipeline, stage, market, owner, business minutes open, the response time in force, the state `ok`, `at_risk`, `breached` or `no_hours` for an unknown market, counted apart, and the minutes to breach, negative once breached; sorted by the minutes to breach, no hours last; `open_tickets()`, the same permissions), `open_tasks_of` (0008n's tasks whose status is not done and whose type counts, with the state `overdue`, `today`, `later` or `none` against the tenant's business date, the deal or ticket by id, the market through the deal, else the ticket, else the contact's stored country; `open_tasks()`), `follow_up_summary_of` (counts only; `follow_up_summary()` for `monitoring.live`, `performance.team` or `workday.team`), `follow_up_by_owner_of` (per mapped person by name and one row for owners nobody maps to; `follow_up_by_owner()` for `performance.team` only: per-person figures sit under the employee-monitoring point with Yordi, like Calls per agent) and `my_follow_up_of(tenant, user)` (`my_follow_up()` for `leads.accept` or `workday.own`: the caller's own rows as JSON with the record links from the connection's templates `record_url_deal`, `record_url_ticket`, `record_url_task`; HubSpot has no task page, so its task rows have none: Open decisions). **Data quality:** `telephony_call_unlinked` skips outbound calls never connected (talk time under `telephony.connected_min_seconds`) and without a current tag when the telephony connection's setting `dq_unlinked_skip` is `outbound_unanswered_untagged`, the default for a connection of kind `telephony` (`none` switches it off; a connection without a kind is unchanged, so 0007d's `41` still passes); `ticket_no_owner` and `ticket_no_market` (open tickets per pipeline) run once the tenant has configured a response time (an own `tickets.*` setting or a pipeline's); `crm_task_owner_unlinked` for open tasks whose owner maps to no person. 0008m's `cma.data_quality_added_checks()` stays as `69` pins it; the three new checks live in `cma.data_quality_follow_up_checks()` and `cma.data_quality()` lists them when they apply. Reader views `cma_read.open_lead`, `open_ticket`, `open_task`, `follow_up_summary`, `follow_up_by_owner` (ids only, no name), each over its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function `cma_read.<view>s_rows()` / `follow_up_*_rows()` in the shape of 0007d (owned by `cma_owner`, search path pinned, executable by `cma_readonly` only, filtered by `cma_read.reader_sees()`), `connection_pipeline_sla`, `dq_ticket_no_owner`, `dq_ticket_no_market`, `dq_crm_task_owner_unlinked`. The verify (`73`, dev only) proves structure and privileges (6 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (15 checks: the open leads with order, owners, ages, buckets, the target and the last call; the buckets at their edges and the setting moving them; the settings' refusals; tickets at 74 %, 75 %, 100 % and 125 % of the default, no business hours apart and last, closed, not counted and deleted absent; the ticket checks silent before an own response time; the pipeline override beating the default and its refusals; the ticket checks once configured and their reader views; the tasks' due states, records and markets; overdue against the tenant's own midnight in Kiritimati; the summary; per person with the not-mapped row; the person's own rows apart between people and tenants with their links; the skip rule on, off, inbound, tagged and connected; the permissions refused; a reader scoped to one tenant); 21 PASS, provoked 21 FAIL, locally on PostgreSQL 16, then `41`, `69` and `71` unchanged (`docs/night-2026-10-16/local-records/db/`). Since addition 0008s (18 October 2026) the application's wrappers read a snapshot computed once a minute per tenant; the reads of an explicit tenant compute it and stay for the reader views and the verifies (see Data model: follow-up snapshot).

### Data model: forms reporting (addition 0008p)

Written on 16 October 2026 (`74_forms_reporting.sql`, `75_verify_forms_reporting.sql`; brief B17d with its amendment of the same day, the forms part of brief B17). Numbered after `Pulse4all-com/cma#142` (N16-B, CRM tasks: 0008n and 0008o, scripts `70` to `73`), which merged first (`c7a3cab`; 0008n and 0008o have run in dev and prod): `74` stops with `addition 0008o (72_follow_up.sql) must run before 0008p` and applies nothing without it.

Why (Martin, 16 October 2026): prod's catalog holds 50 active forms in `pulse4all-subscriptions`: 28 counted lead forms (Contact, Info and Quote per market) and 22 support forms (Cancellation and Request a Replacement Device per market plus a general one each, the General support form, DE change IBAN). `is_counted` meant both "poll this form" and "count it in Intake", so the support forms were never read. The team wants every form's submissions per form and per market in a report of their own, without a support form becoming a lead in Intake (KPI 1); and the answers of the forms' choice questions (a cancellation's reason, a contact's "already a customer"), never free text.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `connection_form.is_polled` | whether the forms poll and the backfill read the form, counted in Intake or not. Existing forms are polled exactly when they count; a check keeps a counted form polled. `is_counted` keeps its meaning: every intake, speed-to-lead and lead-metrics figure reads it, and only it |
| `cma.form_category`, `connection_form.category` | the tenant's form categories (code: lower-case letters, digits and `_`, starting with a letter, not `none`, which the report's filter uses for forms without one; name; order; `active` or `inactive`, never deleted) and a form's category, a code of that catalog by foreign key. No category is named in code or in the migration: Pulse4all's are configuration, set by the runbook (`docs/b17d-forms/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`) |
| `cma.connection_form_field` | per form its fields as the source describes them: name, a canonical `kind` (`dropdown`, `radio`, `multiple_checkboxes`, `single_checkbox`, `other`) and a choice field's option values (names and option values only: at most 200 fields per form, each option 1 to 200 characters and once; the ingest reads at most 500 options per field). Refreshed with the form catalog (`cma.ingest_upsert_forms()` when an item carries fields; `connection_form.fields_refreshed_at`); an option the source drops is kept among the field's retired options and a field it drops is archived, so a stored answer stays explainable |
| kept fields | `connection_form.kept_fields` (0007) names the fields whose answers are stored. Of a form the source has described (`fields_refreshed_at` set), only choice fields (CMA04); of a form not described yet, 0007's rule by name holds (`33`, which has run in prod, configures such forms). The ingest keeps an answer only when the field is a choice field and every value is one of its current options (multiple checkboxes: each ticked option, stored as a list), else drops it and counts it as `dropped` in the run's counts (`cma.ingest_sync_run_finish()` takes the key). Never free text, an address, a serial number or a bank field |
| `connection_form.market_field` | a kept dropdown or radio field (such as `country_dropdown` or `ticket_country`) whose answer gives a submission's market when the form has none, read through the market aliases; `cma.set_form_market_field()` |
| `connection_form.lead_condition` | the lead rule: a kept choice field and the option values that make a submission a lead (`{"field", "values"}`, 1 to 50 values); `cma.set_form_lead_condition()`. Intake's form count (`cma.intake_per_day_of()`, KPI 1) takes a counted form's submission only when its form has no condition or the submission's answer is one of the values; a missing answer is not a lead. The submission stays stored and reported in Forms |
| `form_submission.kept_cleared_at` | set by `cma.ingest_contact_forget()` when a privacy deletion clears a submission's answers, so the backfill never fills them again |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_form_submissions()` | replaced: as in 0007, and a stored submission whose answers are empty gets the answers of a later read once (outcome `filled`; the backfill, after kept fields were set), never overwritten (`known`), never after a privacy deletion; the only update besides the contact resolution |
| `cma.set_form()`, `cma.set_form_reporting(connection, form, polled, category, market)` | configuration (`tenant.configure`). `set_form` keeps 0007's signature: a counted form is polled, a form set not counted keeps whether it is polled. `set_form_reporting` sets polled, category and market: a counted form not polled `CMA03`, an unknown category or market `CMA02`, a switched-off category only on a form that has it already (`CMA04`) |
| `cma.connection_catalogs()` | replaced: the forms with polled, category, market field, lead condition and their fields, for Configuration → Sources |
| `cma.forms_report(from, to, group, market, category, form)` | per form (connection id, form id, name, category) and market and day or ISO week the submissions and their contact resolution (resolved, not found, ambiguous, no email, pending), the period's totals per form and market, and the previous period's, like for like (as 0008j); every polled form's submissions, counted or not. Filters: a market code or `unknown`, a category code or `none`, a form (connection id `:` form id); group `day` or `week`; at most 92 days (`CMA04`). `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.forms_answers(from, to, market, category, form, field, value)` | per form, market, kept field and option value the submissions, a multiple-checkboxes answer under each of its values, with the previous period; narrowed by a field and a value. Option values only |
| `cma.forms_history_start()` | the business date of the tenant's earliest stored submission, in its market's zone, or null (as 0008k), so the report shows no change against a previous span before it |
| `cma.data_quality_forms_checks()`, `cma.data_quality_of()`, `cma.data_quality()` | the check `form_answer_not_in_options`: kept answers no option of their field accounts for, 0 by construction, listed in its own list (`41` pins 0007d's fifteen checks, `69` 0008m's list and `71` and `73` 0008o's, so none of them changes); its reader view `cma_read.dq_form_answer_not_in_options` |

**The market of a submission**, read at query time: the form's market; else the answer of the form's `market_field` through the market aliases; else the resolved contact's country (its slots of 0008m); else `unknown`. Its business date is in that market's zone, else the tenant's (as intake). **Nothing personal:** ids, form and field names, option values and counts; the reads return no submission id and no contact id, and `deployment_outcome` (the general replacement form's question about a device's use, health data of a third person) is never kept (Decision log, 16 October 2026).

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `form_category`, `connection_form_field`; `connection_form` and `form_submission` gain their new columns at the end, and `dq_form_answer_not_in_options`.

The verify (`75`) proves structure and privileges (5 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (19 checks, rolled back), among them that a form polled and not counted changes no output of `cma.intake_per_day_of()`, `cma.speed_to_lead_of()`, `cma.lead_to_order_of()` or `cma.data_quality()`. Verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 (`75` 24 PASS, provoked all 24 FAIL; `33`, `35`, `41` 2 PASS rows each, `53` 17, `69` 30, `71` 21 and `73` 22 PASS rows after it; `docs/b17d-forms/local-records/`); run in dev and prod on 17 October 2026 (`records/b17d-dev-2026-10-17`, `records/b17d-prod-2026-10-17`, written down from Martin's report), in prod at 09:58 UTC, before the merge (`964a347`).

| 0008o Follow-up | the four follow-up settings, a ticket pipeline's own response time, the open leads not called with their age bucket and last call, the open tickets against their response time, the open tasks with their due state, the summary, per person and the person's own rows, the data-quality skip rule for unlinked outbound calls and the checks for tickets without owner or market and tasks with an unlinked owner, the reporting views | backlog CMA-036, CMA-045, CMA-046, CMA-050 and CMA-052 (Decision log, 16 October 2026); written in the night of 15 to 16 October 2026 (see Data model: follow-up), verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 (`73` 21 PASS, provoked all 21 FAIL; `41`, `69` and `71` unchanged); run in dev and prod on 16 October 2026 (`records/0008o-dev-2026-10-16`, `records/0008o-prod-2026-10-16`, written down from Martin's report; `02` rerun after `72` in each; dev `73` 21 PASS, provoked all 21 FAIL, `41`, `69` and `71` unchanged, `53` 15 PASS with A4 failing on two connections that earlier runs of `verify:email-flow` left in dev, not on 0008o: the dev record says how to clear them); the data map from dev at `cfa6cd6`; N16-B merged as `c7a3cab` |

### Data model: System health (addition 0008r)

Written on 17 October 2026 (`78_system_health.sql`, `79_verify_system_health.sql`; the full audit's brief, 7.5) as the database half of Configuration → System health; the page itself ships separately (web). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 on a database built from `00` in README order: `78` applied and rerun unchanged, `79` 13 PASS and, provoked, 13 FAIL; `33` and `73` rerun unchanged.

Why (the audit's brief): a bad deploy or a silent ingest must be visible to the admin without the Console. The page reads what the jobs leave behind, never a vendor; the two thresholds that turn an age into fresh, late or silent are tenant settings, not code (principle 1); the scheduler's close left no trace a page could read, so it writes a run row like every other job.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| settings `health.heartbeat_fresh_seconds`, `health.heartbeat_late_seconds` | the heartbeat (`link_calls`, the sweep's run each minute) counts as fresh under the first, silent from the second, late in between; 120 and 900 by default; `check_tenant_setting()` keeps fresh below late whichever is written |
| settings `health.link_gemini_doc`, `health.link_datamap` | the Documents card's two external links (the Gemini findings document, the published data map): an https address or empty (not set) |
| `sync_run.job` `close_forgotten_workdays`, `connection_id` nullable | the scheduler's run row; `sync_run_connection_check` lets only that job go without a connection; `sync_run_tenant_started_idx` serves the page's 30-day read |
| `cma.scheduler_run_start()`, `cma.scheduler_run_finish(run, status, closed, error)` | `workday.close_forgotten` (the Scheduler user): one row per execution with `clock_timestamp()`, finished once with its count of closed days or its error code; a second finish is CMA03, an unknown run CMA02, a bad status CMA04 |
| `cma.system_health_runs()` | `tenant.configure`: per job and connection the last run (started, finished, status, counts), the last success, the last failure with its error and when, and the last 24 hours' run and failure counts, over 30 days; the ingest's rows next to the scheduler's |

Verified (`79`): structure and privileges (the addition row, the settings with their defaults and type, the three functions the application's only and in the caller's rights, the job list and the index); behaviour on two throwaway tenants (above). The reader schema `cma_read` is unchanged: `sync_run`'s reader view keeps its columns, the new rows carry no connection.

### Data model: follow-up snapshot (addition 0008s)

Written on 18 October 2026 (`80_follow_up_snapshot.sql`, `81_verify_follow_up_snapshot.sql`; the IT audit of 17 October 2026, 5.6 continued; Decision log, 18 October 2026). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 on the dev seed: `80` applied and rerun unchanged, `81` 19 PASS and, provoked, 19 FAIL; `73` 21 PASS with its two refreshes (below) and `79` 13 PASS rerun.

Why: measured by Martin in prod on `1ead2c3` (18 October 2026), Reports → Follow-up refreshed every 10 seconds at 5.2 to 6.6 s, with 7.8 to 9.7 s of database time summed over its parallel reads (7 transactions, 29 statements) and an RSC payload of about 1,044 kB. The page read `cma.open_leads()`, `open_tickets()` and `open_tasks()` in full plus `cma.follow_up_by_owner()`, which built the three sets again, and the Live board's card (`follow_up_summary`) and Welcome's Waiting for you (`my_follow_up`) built them once more on every request. Now one refresh per tenant per minute evaluates each set once and keeps the result.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| setting `follow_up.list_rows` | the rows each Follow-up list shows after its filters: 100 by default, a whole number from 1 to 500 (`check_tenant_setting()`, CMA04); on Configuration → Exports with the other follow-up settings |
| permission `follow_up.refresh` | the Scheduler system user's only (no default role): granted to every tenant's scheduler role by the migration and by `cma.ensure_scheduler_user()` from now on |
| `sync_run.job` `refresh_follow_up` | the refresh's run row, without a connection like `close_forgotten_workdays` (`sync_run_connection_check` names both); `cma.job_run_start(job)` and `cma.job_run_finish(run, status, counts, error)` write a scheduler job's row under that job's own permission (counts an object of numbers or booleans; a second finish CMA03, an unknown run CMA02, a bad status or counts CMA04) |
| `cma.follow_up_snapshot` | one row per tenant: `computed_at`, `duration_ms`, `summary` (the eight counts) and `by_owner` (per person: ids and counts; names are joined when read) |
| `cma.follow_up_item` | one row per open lead, ticket and task of the latest refresh: `kind` (`lead`, `ticket`, `task`), `sort_key` (the live function's order), `item_id`, the filter columns `market` (`unknown` when none), `owner_user_id` and `state`, and `row` (the row type's fields as JSON); indexes on (tenant, kind, sort_key) and per filter |
| `cma.refresh_follow_up_of(tenant, wait)` | evaluates `open_leads_of`, `open_tickets_of` and `open_tasks_of` once each and replaces the tenant's items and snapshot in the caller's transaction, with `duration_ms`; a transaction-level advisory lock per tenant, so a second refresh at the same time skips (`refreshed` false, `reason` `running`); `wait` waits for it instead (the fallback); the current tenant only (CMA06) |
| `cma.refresh_follow_up()` | the job's entry (`follow_up.refresh`) |
| `cma.follow_up_page(market, person, show, rows)` | the page in one call, for `reports.view`, `performance.team` or `monitoring.live`: the summary, the figures per person only for `performance.team` (null otherwise), the filter choices (markets with `unknown` last, the people, whether a row has nobody), the counts before the filters, per list the first `rows` after the filters (a market code, a person's id or `none`, `attention`; anything else CMA04) with the filtered total, each row with the owner's name and the record link, the target and warning share the hints name, and `computed_at`; `rows` defaults to the setting and is held within 1 to 500 |
| `cma.follow_up_summary()`, `cma.my_follow_up()` | read the snapshot: the same shapes plus `computed_at` (the summary's return type gained the column, so it is dropped and created) |
| `cma.open_leads()`, `open_tickets()`, `open_tasks()`, `follow_up_by_owner()` | read the snapshot: the same rows, every row, no cap (the API's lists) |
| `cma.follow_up_computed_at()` | when the tenant's snapshot was computed, for the API's `computedAt` |
| the fallback (`cma.follow_up_snapshot_of`) | a read that finds no snapshot for its tenant computes it once on demand with the same function, waiting for a refresh in flight, so a new tenant or a missed job never shows an empty page |

0008o's reads of an explicit tenant (`cma.open_leads_of` and the rest) stay as they are: the refresh uses them, the reader views in `cma_read` (`open_lead`, `open_ticket`, `open_task`, `follow_up_summary`, `follow_up_by_owner`) keep reading them live, and the verifies compare against them. **No audit trigger on the two tables** (derived in 0008s: Decision log, 18 October 2026): they are rewritten every minute, and `cma.audit_log` would gain a row image per item per minute, about 6,000 rows a minute for a tenant with 3,000 open items; `cma.setup_tenant_table()` still gives them row-level security, the tenant policy and the grants, and every refresh leaves its audited `sync_run` row and the snapshot's `computed_at` and `duration_ms`. No lookback bound on overdue tasks: it would change the KPI's meaning (an option for Kira, Open decisions). Since 0008s `73` refreshes the snapshot after its two changes (the bucket setting, a pipeline's response time), because the wrappers it checks read the snapshot; its checks are the same.

Verified (`81`, dev only): structure and privileges (6 checks: the addition row, the setting, the permission on every scheduler role and no other, the two tables with row-level security, the tenant policy, the application's grants, the indexes and no audit trigger, every function the application's only and in the caller's rights, `sync_run`'s constraints) and behaviour on throwaway tenants, rolled back (13 checks: the fallback filling an empty tenant; the snapshot's counts equal to the live functions', in their order; every row's fields both ways and the figures per person equal; the filtered totals equal to filtering the live sets; `rows` by default, honoured, 0 to 1 and 1000 to 500, and the setting's refusals; per person absent without `performance.team`; no other tenant's row; the lock held by a refresh in flight; the job's run rows and their refusals; the person's own rows; the wrappers equal to the live reads; a new task seen only after a refresh; the filter choices, counts, links and settings); 19 PASS, provoked all 19 FAIL. A second session skipping while the first holds the lock needs two connections, so `web/verify/refresh-follow-up.mjs` proves it (API, The follow-up refresh).

### Data model: email reports (addition 0008t)

Written in the night of 18 to 19 October 2026 (`82_email_reports.sql`, `83_verify_email_reports.sql`; night run N19-A, part 1; brief B17c; backlog CMA-008 and CMA-051; Decision log, 18 October 2026). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 on a database built from `00` in README order (`docs/night-2026-10-19/local-records/db/`): `82` applied and rerun with nothing new, `83` 26 PASS and, provoked, 26 FAIL; `69` 29, `73` 21, `75` 24 and `81` 19 PASS rerun after it. `69` was amended in place (it runs in dev only): its view checks accept the columns 0008t adds at the end of `cma_read.email_thread` and `email_message`, and 0008u's at the end of `cma_read.crm_contact`; its other checks are unchanged. Since addition 0008x (19 October 2026) the reads below run set-based, and the Email page and Email per agent read in one pass each, with the same figures: see Data model: report speed.

Why: managers and analytics read emails received per mailbox and sent per agent in HubSpot today (Arno's reports 15a and 15b, the first-reply half of 17, the library's Conversations reports, "Contacts with deals who aren't responding to emails"). Addition 0008m holds the data, filled by the email poll of brief B17b since 16 October 2026; this addition adds the mailboxes, the origin of an engagement, the per-person setting and the reads. Metadata only: ids, counts, times and codes, never a subject, a body, an address, a name, a phone number or a header.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| setting `reports.per_person` | the per-person report pages a tenant switches on: comma-separated keys from the fixed list `email`, `orders`, each once, lower case, no spaces; empty (the default) for none; `check_tenant_setting()` refuses anything else (`CMA04`). `cma.per_person_on(tenant, key)`, `cma.assert_per_person(key)` (`CMA06`). Calls per agent is not under it |
| `cma.connection_inbox_account` | the mailboxes: per connection the inboxes' channel accounts (a shared address connected to an inbox) with the source's id, the source's name (refreshed; a name that holds an `@` gives way to the id, so no address is stored), a person's label (1 to 80 characters without an `@`, kept over every refresh), the inbox and channel refs, counted (default true), market (catalog) and status (`active`, `archived`; never deleted). `cma.set_inbox_account()` (`tenant.configure`), `cma.ingest_upsert_inbox_accounts()` (`ingest.write`, at most 500; an account the source drops is archived) |
| `email_message.account_ref`, `email_thread.account_ref` | the channel account a message went through and the one a conversation started on; a later read without one keeps the stored one |
| `email_message.origin` | an engagement's origin code (the connection's setting `email_origin_property`, `hs_engagement_source` for HubSpot), filled for the stored engagements from their raw payload. The connection's `email_inbox_copy_origins` names the origins whose engagement copies an inbox message (left out of every report; `CONVERSATIONS` for Pulse4all) and `email_automated_origins` (B17b) the automations' |
| `cma.email_market_of()` | the inbox step reads the mailbox's market first, then the inbox's; everything else as 0008m |
| `cma.email_of`, `email_openings_of`, `email_closes_of` | the definitions (below): every counted email of a period with its flags, market, zone, business date and hour; every opening of an inbox conversation with its first incoming message, first agent reply and close; every close |
| `cma.email_report()` | the report page's one read (`reports.view` or `performance.team`): per day or ISO week received, logged replies, agent sends, automated, bounced and failed, conversations opened and closed, first replies (medians elapsed and in business time, within `email.reply_target_minutes`, without business hours) and time to close; the same per inbox, mailbox, category and market; received per date and inbox or mailbox; the previous period's totals like for like; filters market, inbox and mailbox; at most 92 days (`CMA04`). Also `email_overview()`, `email_by_dimension()`, `email_received_series()` |
| `cma.email_per_person()` | per person by name, never ranked, through the linked accounts: sends, first replies, closes, first-reply medians and within target, and sends per hour of day; the not-linked actions as one row with how many owner ids that is; the total. `performance.team` and `reports.per_person` holding `email` (`CMA06` otherwise) |
| `cma.email_waiting()`, `cma.email_not_responding()` | conversations waiting now (the latest message the customer's: waiting since, business and elapsed time) and open lead deals whose contact got an agent email at least `email.no_response_days` ago with nothing back since (no email, inbox message or connected call); each capped by `follow_up.list_rows` with its total |
| `cma.email_history_start()` | the business date of the tenant's earliest stored email, so no change shows against a previous span before it (as 0008k) |
| `cma.crm_call_connected_of()` | whether a CRM call is a conversation as 0008j defines one, through the call link, and its effective start; shared with 0008u |

**Definitions** (one place each): a counted email is not deleted; an inbox message counts when its inbox, mailbox and channel count (one the catalog has not brought yet counts, as a line does in 0008j) and its conversation is not spam; an engagement counts when its origin is none of the inbox-copy origins. Received: an inbox message in or forwarded; an engagement in is a logged reply, a column of its own, never added to the inbox total. Sent: direction out, status sent; automated by origin or when an inbox message's actor is a bot, an AI, the system or an integration. An opening is a counted, non-spam conversation's creation or a reopen after a close (a reopen at a thread's creation is no opening: Open decisions). The first reply is the first agent message, not failed, after the opening's first incoming message, in business seconds of the conversation's market (`cma.business_seconds_of`; none for an unknown market). The credited person is the replying, closing or sending agent through `<adapter>_owner`; never by id. Dates, weeks and hours are in the market's zone, else the tenant's.

Reporting views in `cma_read`: `connection_inbox_account`; `email_thread` and `email_message` with the new columns at the end; `email_waiting` and `email_not_responding` (ids and ages), each over its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function in `cma_read` as 0008o's. The verify (`83`, dev only) proves structure and privileges and behaviour on three throwaway tenants (the mailbox catalog and an address never stored, account refs and origins, inbox copies left out, received and logged replies apart, openings and the reopen at creation, the first reply in and out of business hours, the market through the mailbox, per person with the not-linked row and refused while the setting does not hold `email`, the previous period, the filters, waiting now, not responding, isolation, a reader scoped to one tenant). The ingest reads the channel accounts with the hourly catalog (`conversations.mjs`, `accountItems`), a message's `channelAccountId` and a thread's `originalChannelAccountId`, and an engagement's origin property (`email.mjs`); nothing is written to HubSpot.

### Data model: first orders (addition 0008u)

Written in the night of 18 to 19 October 2026 (`84_first_orders.sql`, `85_verify_first_orders.sql`; night run N19-A, part 2; KPIs, 2 Indirect sales). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 (`docs/night-2026-10-19/local-records/db/`): `84` applied and rerun with nothing new, `85` 26 PASS and, provoked, 26 FAIL; `41`, `69` and `83` unchanged. Since addition 0008x (19 October 2026) `cma.first_orders_of` reads the touches once per first order, and the First orders page reads the report, the amounts and the list in one pass, with the same figures: see Data model: report speed.

Why (Martin, 18 October 2026): of the new customers' first orders, which came in by self-service and which through the call center. A first order is a won deal in an order pipeline (the deal HubSpot's commerce integration creates per new order, won on creation), matched against the same contact's lead deals, the agents' calls and emails with it, a call from the order's phone number (the keyed hashes of 0007a and 0008g) and whether the contact was known before. The same CRM contact means the same email address: HubSpot keeps one contact per address, and the CMA never stores one. The rules are proposed defaults held as tenant settings; Kira confirms them (Open decisions, Indirect sales). Nothing is written to the CRM.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `connection_pipeline.is_order` | a deal pipeline whose won deals are orders (default false; a check keeps it to deals). `cma.set_pipeline_order()` (`tenant.configure`); on Configuration → Sources as the Order pipeline column |
| `connection_stage.outcome`, `outcome_source` | a deal stage's outcome: `won`, `lost` or none, and whether the source or a person set it. The adapter gives the source's with every pipelines read (a HubSpot deal stage at probability 1.0 is won, a closed one at 0.0 lost; a ticket stage none); a person's choice (`cma.set_stage_outcome()`, `tenant.configure`, deal stages) survives every later read. Pipelines and stages are found by these flags, never by label |
| `crm_contact.source_created_at` | when the contact was created in the source: the earliest non-empty value of the canonical field `created_at`, slot 1 the object's own creation time (no property, no extra read), slots 2 to 9 optional properties (a created date kept from an earlier CRM); 0008m's slot rule (contiguous from 2, removed from the end, no property twice) |
| settings | `attribution.window_days` (90, 1 to 365), `attribution.level_order` (`lead_deal,conversation,email,phone_match,attempt,known_contact`: one to six of them, each once), `attribution.assisted_levels` (`lead_deal,conversation,email,phone_match`: none to six of them), `attribution.known_contact_minutes` (60, 0 to 10080), `attribution.order_match_minutes` (1440, 1 to 10080); `check_tenant_setting()` refuses anything else |
| `cma.first_orders_of(tenant, from, to)` | the definition: every order deal created in the period with its kind, linked store order, currency and amount, the six evidence flags, level, attribution, first touch and credited person |
| `cma.first_orders_report()` | per day, ISO week, market or level, per market and per level, and the previous period's total like for like: first orders, via the call center, self-service, repeat, renewals, not first, without a contact, with a touch, the median days from the first touch. Filters market and level; at most 92 days. `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.first_orders_amounts()`, `first_orders_list()` | the amounts per currency (never summed across currencies), in total and per market; the first orders newest first with their flags and the deal's link, capped by `follow_up.list_rows` with the total |
| `cma.first_orders_per_person()` | the first orders via the call center per credited person by name, never ranked, with the levels and the median days; the owners without a person as one row. `performance.team` and `reports.per_person` holding `orders` |
| `cma.first_orders_history_start()` | the history start (the later of `intake.start_date` + `attribution.window_days` and the earliest stored order deal: before it a window reaches back past the data), the phone tier's own start (the earliest stored store order with a phone hash), and whether an order pipeline exists (connected) |

**Definitions:** an order deal is a deal, not deleted, in a counted pipeline marked `is_order` whose stage's outcome is won; T is its creation at the source; its contact the deal's primary contact, else its earliest current association. The linked order is a store order (not a test, not cancelled, not deleted) of a customer matched to that contact (0008h), within `attribution.order_match_minutes` of T, the nearest; its amount and currency, else the deal's own. Kind: `no_contact`, `renewal` (the linked order is one), `repeat` (an earlier won order deal of the contact), `not_first` (the store counts earlier orders for the customer), else `first`; only first orders are attributed. Evidence, each in [T − window, T): `lead_deal` (a deal of the contact in a counted lead pipeline that is no order pipeline), `conversation` (an associated CRM call that is a conversation, 0008j), `email` (an agent's email to the contact: an inbox message not of a bot, an AI, the system or an integration, or an engagement neither an inbox copy nor an automation, 0008t), `phone_match` (any call whose other party's hash is the linked order's or its customer's phone hash), `attempt` (an associated call that is no conversation); `known_contact` (the contact created at least `attribution.known_contact_minutes` before T). The level is the first evidence in `attribution.level_order` that holds, else `self_service`; the attribution is `call_center` when the level is in `attribution.assisted_levels`. The credited person owns the latest assisted touch before T, through `<adapter>_owner` and `<adapter>_user` (0008e). The date and week are T in the deal's market zone (0007d's market).

Reporting views: `cma_read.connection_pipeline` and `connection_stage` with the new columns last, `crm_contact` with `source_created_at` last, and `cma_read.first_orders` (ids, codes, flags and times, over its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function). The verify (`85`, dev only) proves structure and privileges (6 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (20 checks: the settings and their refusals, the stage outcomes and a person's choice, the created-at slots, the kinds, the linked order, every flag at the window's edges, an inbox copy, an automation and a bot no touch, the levels with the order and the assisted set changed, the credited person, days, the report, amounts per currency, the previous period, the history start, the list, per person, the filters, the permissions, isolation, a reader scoped to one tenant). The ingest maps a contact's `createdAt` to slot 1 and a deal stage's outcome from its probability (`map.mjs`, `read.mjs`).

### Data model: journeys (addition 0008v)

Written in the night of 18 to 19 October 2026 (`86_journeys.sql`, `87_verify_journeys.sql`; night run N19-A, part 3). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 (`docs/night-2026-10-19/local-records/db/`): `86` applied and rerun with nothing new, `02` rerun after it twice (three journeys, then nothing new), `87` 16 PASS and, provoked, 16 FAIL.

Why (Martin, 18 October 2026): a place for campaign and compliance monitoring that becomes the journey engine of the FCA compliance monitor concept (UK consumers; journeys as configuration: a population, ordered steps with proof events, due rules, evidence and escalation). Tonight placeholders only, named from configuration. The model word is journey; campaign stays reserved for the queue engine (0011); "Campaign monitoring" is a screen's copy.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.journey` | per tenant a code (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,40}$`, unique), name (1 to 80), summary (at most 500), market (catalog, or none), status (`placeholder` by default, `building`, `live`, `archived`; never deleted: archived hides it), design link (https, or none), prerequisites (at most 20 lines of 1 to 200 characters, in their order) and sort order; `cma.setup_tenant_table()` (row-level security, the tenant policy, the grants, audit), no delete for the application |
| `cma.journeys()` | every journey of the tenant but the archived ones, by sort order, name and code; `reports.view` or `performance.team` |
| `cma.set_journey(code, name, summary, market, design_url, prerequisites, sort_order)`, `cma.set_journey_status(code, status)` | add or change a journey (a new one a placeholder; its status kept), and its status; `tenant.configure`; `CMA04` for a bad value, `CMA02` for an unknown market or journey |
| `cma_read.journey` | the reader view, archived journeys included |

No step, population or per-customer table yet. Pulse4all's three journeys are seed data in `02` (Pulse4all Subscriptions only, added when missing so a change on Configuration survives a rerun): `fca-uk-consumers` "FCA · UK consumers" (market GB where the catalog holds GB) with its seven data prerequisites, and `opc` "OPC" and `exchange` "Exchange", whose steps and population Kira defines; no design link until Martin sets one. The verify (`87`, dev only) proves structure and privileges (6 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (10 checks: a new journey's defaults, a change keeping status and order, every refusal, the statuses, archived hidden and kept, no delete, the audit naming the person, the permissions, isolation, a reader scoped to one tenant).

### Data model: callbacks kept (addition 0008w)

Written in the night of 18 to 19 October 2026 (`88_callbacks_kept.sql`, `89_verify_callbacks_kept.sql`; night run N19-A, part 4; KPI 5; backlog CMA-066). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 (`docs/night-2026-10-19/local-records/db/`): `88` applied and rerun with nothing new, `89` 15 PASS and, provoked, 15 FAIL; `73` and `81` unchanged. 3,040 callback tasks over eight weeks read in about 50 ms locally (the report with its previous period; per person 43 ms): the call is looked up in the callback's own row, because a grouped join back to the tasks grew with their square (minutes at that volume).

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.callbacks_kept_of(tenant, from, to, now)` | the definition: every task of a counted callback type (0008n's `is_callback` and `is_counted`), not deleted, open or done, due in the period (one without a due time placed by its creation), with its owner and person, due date, ISO week and local time in the tenant's zone, its state and the call that kept it with its minutes from the due time |
| `cma.callbacks_kept_report()` | per ISO week and in total: callbacks (with a contact and a due time), kept, not kept, still open, and apart without a contact or a due time; the previous period's total like for like (0008p's rule). `reports.view` or `performance.team`; at most 92 days |
| `cma.callbacks_kept_per_owner()` | per task owner by name, never ranked, the owners without a person as one row with how many, the total. `performance.team`, as Follow-up's per person (Decision log, 16 October 2026) |
| `cma.callbacks_kept_connected()` | whether the tenant counts a callback type at all, so the screens say "No callback type set" instead of 0 |
| `cma_read.callbacks_kept` | every callback since `intake.start_date` with its state, the owner's person by id, the call's time and minutes (over its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function) |

**Definition** (README Open decisions, Callbacks kept, as proposed there; Kira confirms): a callback is kept when a call with its contact, in either direction (a CRM call associated with the contact, not deleted), starts within `tasks.callback_window_minutes` (240) before or after the due time, both edges included, the start being the linked telephony call's, else the CRM's (0008j's effective time); not kept once that window has closed without one, also when the task was completed without a call; still open (pending) while the window is open. The kept share is kept over kept and not kept. The verify (`89`, dev only) proves structure and privileges (3 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (12 checks: the states and minutes, the window's edges and the earliest call, an inbound call, a removed association, the setting moving the window, pending, the tasks apart and absent, the figures per week, the previous period in full and while the period runs, per person, a callback type or none, the range and the permissions, isolation, a reader scoped to one tenant).

### Data model: report speed (addition 0008x)

Written 19 October 2026 (`90_report_speed.sql`, `91_verify_report_speed.sql`; the report speed brief; Decision log, 19 October 2026). Measured first: `docs/perf-2026-10-19/diagnose.sql` ran once in prod as the owner (row counts and shape, indexes, timings, and the plans of the bodies of `cma.first_orders_of` and `cma.email_report_of` as the owner and as `cma_app`); its output is `docs/perf-2026-10-19/prod-output.txt`. Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 (`docs/perf-2026-10-19/local-records/db/`): `90` applied and rerun with nothing new, `91` 13 PASS and, provoked, 13 FAIL; `83`, `85`, `87`, `89`, `41`, `69`, `73`, `75` and `81` unchanged.

Why: in prod as `cma_app` in October, `first_orders_report` took 26.8 s, `first_orders_amounts` 20.1 s and `first_orders_list` 21.1 s, each above the web's 15 s statement timeout, so Reports → First orders always failed; `email_report` took 7.0 s (14.6 s with a filter, which read it twice) and Email per agent 18 to 20 s. The plans showed no missing index, and these causes:
- first orders: the touches were planned as a nested loop that ran the whole touch lateral once per first order per first order (166 × 166 = 27,556 runs, 20.7 of 20.7 s), with `cma.crm_call_connected_of` called per call inside it (26,228 calls, about 2.6 s);
- email under row-level security: the market chain's contact key, a `coalesce` holding the association subquery, kept the unique index out, so `crm_contact` was scanned whole per email (4,870 × 1.9 ms) and `form_submission` 2,033 times; as the owner the same read took 1.1 s;
- `cma.market`, `market_alias` and `app_user_external_id` had never been analyzed.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.first_orders_of` | the same rows: the touches read once per first order and joined back by id; the CRM calls a first order can count read once each, with 0008j's effective time and conversation flag as `cma.crm_call_connected_of` gives them; `cma.market_code_of` once per distinct value |
| `cma.first_order`, `cma.first_orders_rows_of()` | the row type of `cma.first_orders_of` and a span's order deals in one pass; `first_orders_figures_from()`, `first_orders_report_from()`, `first_orders_amounts_from()` and `first_orders_list_from()` compute from those rows. `first_orders_report_of` reads the period and its previous span in one pass |
| `cma.first_orders_page()` | the First orders page in one read and one pass (`reports.view` or `performance.team`): the report, the amounts and the list as the three reads give them, each row tagged with its part |
| `cma.email_of`, `email_threads_counted_of`, `email_openings_of` | the same rows: the contact first, as a plain column, then the market chain of `cma.email_market_of` by joins (`cma.market_code_of` once per distinct value; the form and record steps only where the steps before found nothing, as before), the zone and the connections' origin lists once; an opening's business seconds of the reply and the close computed in the query as `cma.business_seconds_of` defines them |
| `cma.email_fact`, `cma.email_facts_of()` | a span's emails, openings and closes in one pass; `email_figures_from()`, `email_report_from()`, `email_by_dimension_from()` and `email_per_person_from()` compute from them. `email_report_of` and `email_overview_of` read the period and its previous span in one pass |
| `cma.email_report_page()` | the Email page in one read and one pass (`reports.view` or `performance.team`): the rows of `cma.email_report` and, with a filter set, the figures per inbox, mailbox, category and market without the filters (period `unfiltered`) for the filter lists |
| `cma.email_per_person_page()` | Email per agent in one read and one pass (as `cma.email_per_person`): per person (part `person`) and the figures without the filters for the filter lists (part `dimension`) |

The earlier reads keep their names, arguments, columns and checks (the First orders file still reads `first_orders_list`); the web reads the three page reads. Everything stays `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by the application only. No table, view or column changes, so `docs/datamap/catalog.json` and `schema.json` stay as they are.

The verify (`91`, dev only) builds a throwaway tenant at prod's counts of 19 October 2026 (sections 2 and 3 of the prod output: 5,257 contacts, 1,486 order deals won in October's read window, 4,156 CRM and 4,096 telephony calls, 5,263 emails, 1,362 conversations, 2,806 orders and 11,313 store customers, with the calls per number and the emails per contact of section 3). It copies the definitions of 0008t and 0008u into `pg_temp` and proves three things. Every rewritten read and each page read as the application gives the old rows (0 apart, both ways). Every report read of October as `cma_app` answers under 1 s (best of three; locally the slowest took 229 ms). The first orders pass, the email report and per person are at least five times faster than before (locally 66 ms against 39.4 s, 250 ms against 2.4 s and 159 ms against 2.1 s). Prod decides: the morning runbook times the reads there as `cma_app`.

### Data model: attempts per deal (addition 0008y)

Written 19 October 2026 (`92_attempts_per_deal.sql`, `93_verify_attempts_per_deal.sql`; Martin's brief of 19 October 2026; KPIs 1; backlog CMA-046, the rest, and CMA-020; Decision log, 19 October 2026). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 16 on a fresh database built in README order (`docs/attempts-2026-10/local-records/db/`): `92` applied and rerun with nothing new (the same function and view definitions, settings and migrations), `93` 25 PASS and, provoked, 25 FAIL; `41`, `61`, `69`, `73`, `75`, `77`, `81`, `83`, `85`, `89` and `91` unchanged; `03` again sets `email,orders,attempts`, `02` again keeps `email`. It answers how many times the call center tries a deal before it reaches the customer, how often the first attempt reaches them, whether more attempts win more deals, and how long the open deals have waited since their last attempt (Features 5, Reports → Attempts per deal). The attempts are the CRM's logged calls until the queue engine records its own (0011).

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| settings `attempts.max_bucket`, `attempts.since_last_buckets_business_days` | the distribution's top bucket (`5`, 2 to 20, shown as `n+`) and the buckets of the business days since the last attempt (`1,2,5`: one to six increasing whole numbers, as `leads.age_buckets_business_days`; spaces dropped); `reports.per_person` takes `attempts` as a third key (one to three of `email`, `orders`, `attempts`, each once) |
| `cma.counted_deals_of(tenant, from, to)` | the deals of every counted deal pipeline with 0007d's rules and columns, plus whether the pipeline is a lead or an order pipeline, the stage and the close; the market as `cma.record_market_of` gives it, with `cma.market_code_of` once per distinct value (per deal it took about 0.25 ms). `cma.lead_deals_of` keeps its signature, bounds and rows and reads it for the lead pipelines, so the population is written once (`93` compares it with 0008q's body), and Speed to lead, Follow-up, Forms and Email get the faster market |
| `cma.attempts_per_deal_of(tenant, from, to, scope, now)` | the definition, one row per deal (the row type `cma.attempt_deal`): connection, deal id and link, pipeline, whether lead, stage, market, owner and person, created, business date and week, closed and the outcome, the attempts, how many connected, the first connected attempt, the first and last attempt and, for an open deal, the business days since the last attempt with its bucket |
| `cma.attempts_per_deal_page(from, to, group, market, pipeline, scope, rows)` | Attempts per deal in one read and one pass (`reports.view` or `performance.team`; at most 92 days): the figures and the previous period like for like (0008p's rule), the buckets with the first connected attempts, won and lost, the groups per day, ISO week, market or pipeline, the open deals by time since the last attempt, the list (open first, the longest waiting first, then closed by the newest close, capped by `follow_up.list_rows` with the total), the filter lists, the history start (the later of `intake.start_date` and the first stored CRM call) and the settings |
| `cma.attempts_per_person_page(from, to, market, pipeline, scope)` | per deal owner by name, never ranked, the owners without a person (or no owner) as one row with how many owner ids, the total (`performance.team`, and `reports.per_person` holding `attempts`; `CMA06` otherwise) |
| `cma.telephony_number_stored_calls(connection, line)` | the stored calls of one line and the first one's start, which switching it off removes (`tenant.configure`; an unknown line `CMA02`; backlog CMA-020). A line has no id of its own, so it is named by its connection and line ref, as the lines route does |
| `cma_read.attempts_per_deal` | every deal of the counted scope since `intake.start_date` with whether it is a lead, the market, the owner's person by id, created, closed and the outcome, the attempts, connected, the first connected attempt, the first and last attempt and the days since the last attempt with its bucket (over its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function, for every tenant `cma_read.reader_sees()` allows) |

**Definitions** (0007d's reused, never copied):
- deal: scope `lead` (the default) a deal of a counted lead pipeline as `cma.lead_deals_of` has it; scope `counted` a deal of any counted deal pipeline, with the same rules (not deleted, created on or after `intake.start_date`). An order pipeline (0008u) is in neither. The business date is the creation in the tenant's zone.
- attempt: an outbound CRM call of `cma.lead_calls_of` (not deleted, associated with the deal or its primary or currently associated contacts, its effective start at or after the creation minus `speed_to_lead.pre_window_minutes`), at or before the deal's close (now while it is open). A call to a contact with two open deals counts for both; the page says so (Open decisions).
- connected: the call's outcome counts as connected, or the linked telephony call was answered (0007d). The first connected attempt is the n-th in the order of the effective start, or none.
- outcome: won or lost from the stage (0008u); else open while the deal is open, unknown when it closed in a stage without an outcome.
- market and owner: 0007d's market; the owner's person through `app_user_external_id` (`<source>_owner`), else none, never by id.
- since the last attempt: for an open deal, the business days in its market (`cma.business_days_of`, 0008o) from its last attempt, or from its creation when never attempted, to now, bucketed by `cma.age_bucket_of`; none for an unknown market.

Everything is `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by the application only; the reader function is `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `cma_owner`, its search path pinned with `pg_temp` last, executable by readers only. No table and no index: the reads use 0007d's. Speed: each deal's calls are read once, `business_days_of` runs once per distinct market and day and `age_bucket_of` once per distinct count; measured locally as `cma_app` on a throwaway tenant at about twice prod's lead volume of 19 October 2026 (3,000 lead deals over September and October, 9,598 CRM calls; `docs/attempts-2026-10/local-records/db/volume-timing.sql`): October's deal page in 286 ms (by pipeline with every counted deal 325 ms), per agent 146 ms, under 0008x's bar of 500 ms. The runbook measures both in prod as `cma_app` before the merge. The verify (`93`, dev only) proves structure and privileges (5 checks) and behaviour on two throwaway tenants (20 checks): 0, 1, 3 and 7 attempts, 7 in `5+` and, with the top at 8, in 7; an inbound call out; a second contact's call in and a removed association out; the pre-window both ways; a call after the close out; connected by the outcome and by an answered telephony call, the first connected attempt 3 and 2; the outcomes won, lost, open and unknown; both scopes, with the order pipeline, an uncounted pipeline and a deleted deal in neither; the business days across a weekend and a holiday, never attempted, an unknown market none; the figures, groups and the previous period like for like; the list's order, cap and link; the filters and the history start; the 92-day bound and the parameters; per person with the not-linked row and its gates; the setting refusals; a line's stored calls; tenant isolation; the reader view equal to the application's rows; `cma.lead_deals_of` against 0008q's body, with every market as `cma.record_market_of` gives it. `web/src/lib/attempts.ts` mirrors the definitions for the mock, and `verify:reports` checks it on `93`'s own scenario.

Data prerequisites (KPIs 11): the CRM's calls logged with their outcome and associated with the contact or the deal (the Aircall–HubSpot integration does this), the connected outcomes marked on Configuration → Sources, the deal owners linked to people on People, the stage outcomes (0008u) for the won rate, and the markets' business hours for the time since the last attempt.

### API: the web app's data layer (since 5 October 2026)

The web app reads and writes Postgres **in-process**: screens and route handlers call `data()` from `src/lib/data`, which returns the Postgres implementation when `CMA_DATA_MODE=api` and the in-memory mock otherwise. There is no HTTP hop between the screens and the database. The ingest API for Make and n8n (Roadmap step 3) is a separate Cloud Run service with its own service account and per-tenant keys; it can lift `src/lib/db/client.ts`.

**Rules in every call.** One transaction per call with `app.tenant_id`, `app.user_id` and `app.actor_label` set by `set_config(…, true)`, so a pooled connection can never carry one request's tenant into the next. Own data only through the database: every read filters on `cma.current_user_id()`, the transaction's acting user, never on an id passed in. The database clock decides: `open_workday()`, `set_status()` and `end_workday()` are called without a time. Today is the business date in the user's zone, computed in SQL. Minutes on screen are working seconds rounded down per day (which hours the agent sees stays open, see Open decisions). Errors keep their SQLSTATE; SQL text never reaches a screen. `GET /api/health` is the only route outside `/api/v1`: liveness for Cloud Run and the deploy's smoke test, `{ status, version, auth, data }`, no user data, not gated by `proxy.ts` (IAP in front of the load balancer still gates it).

**Login (`findPrincipal`).** `cma.find_tenants_for_identity(provider, subject)` without a tenant; exactly one tenant continues, none or several (no tenant picker yet) is "no access yet". Inside that tenant the `app_user` row, its employer, its zone (`cma.user_timezone`), its role and, since 6 October 2026, its permission keys (`cma.user_permissions`) for showing or hiding screens: screens check a permission, never a role key, and the database checks again on every call. A user without a role is "no access yet". With several roles the earliest grant is shown, without ranking role names. A database that cannot be reached is an error, never "no access".

**JSON API**, versioned, behind the same gate and identity as the screens, no tenant parameter anywhere and no user parameter on the `/me` routes, `cache-control: no-store`:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/me` | who the caller is: name, tenant, employer, role, zone | `findPrincipal` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/day?date=YYYY-MM-DD` | the caller's workday, default today in their zone; reading never clocks in; `data` is null when there is no day. Since addition 0008b the day carries `dayEndsAt`, the window end of its date, where the clock stops counting | `getWorkday` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/day/start` | Clock in (since 7 October 2026, increment e1): opens the caller's day today, the same path as the Clock in button. Answers the day in every case: opened now, already open, or ended and unchanged, so a client compares `startedAt` rather than status codes. Refused by the database for someone whose time is not kept (`CMA06` → 403), and since addition 0008b before the workday window starts (`CMA04` → 400, message "the workday starts at HH:MI", the one database text the route passes on, so the card can say when the day starts) | `startWorkday` → `cma.open_workday()` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/day/end` | ends the caller's day today, same path as the button; an ended day is returned unchanged | `endWorkday` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/hours?from=&to=` | the caller's hours per day, at most 92 days per request | `getHours` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/statuses` | the work statuses the caller can choose: the tenant's active `work_status` list in its order, with `key`, `name`, `isWorking`, `isProductive` (since 7 October 2026, for the status colour) and `isDefault`; pay and billing flags are not exposed | `listStatuses` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/status` | sets the caller's status on today's day: body `{ "key": "…" }` with a key from `/statuses`; answers the day. Never opens a day: no day today or an unknown key is 404, an ended day 409 (changing it is a correction) | `setStatus` → `cma.set_status()` |
| `GET /api/v1/settings` | the tenant's effective settings: every catalog key with its value and `isDefault`; any person of the tenant (since 7 October 2026) | `listSettings` → `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/app-links` | the app links the caller may see (since 7 October 2026, increment e1): the tenant's buttons to other applications, filtered by the database on the caller's permissions, in the tenant's order; `key`, `label`, `address` | `listAppLinks` → `cma.app_links()` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/follow-up` | the caller's own follow-up (addition 0008o, 16 October 2026): the leads not called, the tickets and the tasks whose owner is linked to them on People, each with its record link; always the caller's own, whatever role an admin previews as; since addition 0008s from the follow-up snapshot, all of the caller's rows, with `computedAt` (when the snapshot was computed); `leads.accept` or `workday.own` | `getMyFollowUp` → `cma.my_follow_up()` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/preview` with `{ roleKey: string \| null }` | starts or ends the caller's preview as a role (16 October 2026, Users and roles): 204 with the cookie `cma-preview-role` set for 12 hours (httpOnly, secure, samesite=lax, path `/`), or cleared for `null`; a caller whose real permissions lack `users.manage_all`, or an unknown role key, is a 403 that sets nothing; an ill-formed key a 400; the same write guards as every POST. The real principal decides (`forPrincipal(…, { real: true })`), so a previewing admin can exit; every other route gets the narrowed principal and answers the role's 403 where the role may not call | `listRoles` → `cma.roles()` |

**Team routes (since 6 October 2026)**, for people holding `workday.team` (the exports `workday.export`, time per status `performance.team`, the team now `monitoring.live`). They take a user id; whether the caller may use it is answered by the database function in the same transaction (`CMA06` → 403), never by the route alone:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/team/hours?from=&to=&userId=` | hours per person per day (all people of the tenant, or one), with employer, zone, working and paid minutes and the capped, needs-correction and has-correction flags; at most 92 days | `getTeamHours` → `cma.team_hours()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/people` | the people whose time is kept (active, holding `workday.own`) with employer and zone, for Add day and the person filter | `listTeamPeople` → `cma.team_people()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/days/{userId}/{date}` | one person's day and every event of it, effective or not, with reason and approver: `{ day, events }`, `day` null when there is none | `getTeamDay` → `cma.team_hours()`, `cma.team_day()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/days/{userId}/{date}/corrections` | body `{ "reason": "…", "changes": [ { kind, at, statusKey, supersedes } ] }`; on a date without a day this is Add day. The caller is the approver. Answers `{ day, events }` | `correctWorkday` → `cma.correct_workday()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/exports/hours?from=&to=&userId=` | hours per person per day as a CSV download (since 7 October 2026), for people holding `workday.export`; at most 92 days | `exportHours` → `cma.export_hours()`, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/status-time?from=&to=&userId=` | time per status (since 7 October 2026, increment d1): one row per person per business date per status with key, name, sort order, active, the four flags, seconds, stretches and capped, for people holding `performance.team`; at most 92 days. Named after the data, not the screen | `getTeamStatusTime` → `cma.team_status_time()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/exports/status-changes?from=&to=&userId=` | one row per stretch in a status as a CSV download, same permission and bound | `exportStatusChanges` → `cma.export_status_changes()`, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/now` | the team now (since 7 October 2026, increment b): one entry per person whose time is kept with employer, zone, today in that zone, `day` (today's day shaped exactly as `GET /api/v1/me/day`, null without a day) and `status` (the current status with name, active and the four flags; null without a day and once ended), plus `statusFlags`, the tenant's active statuses' working, productive and paid flags for the board's tiles (keys and names left out). No parameters, today only, the whole tenant until teams exist; for people holding `monitoring.live`. Named after the data, not the screen | `getTeamNow` → `cma.team_now()`, then the active `work_status` flags in the same transaction |
| `GET /api/v1/telephony/inbound-now` | the Live board's inbound part (since addition 0008i): `today` (cma.inbound_today: today's figures in the tenant's zone, `waitingNow`, `longestWaitingSeconds`, the two settings), `callbacks` (cma.missed_callbacks: one row per caller, oldest first, with line, market, reason, start, `missedCount`, waiting in office time and `recordUrl` from the connection's `record_url_call` template, null without one), `anonymousCount` and `lookbackDays`; `telephony` false with no figures when no telephony is connected. Never a number, a name or the caller's hash; for people holding `monitoring.live`. Named after the data | `getInboundNow` → `cma.inbound_today()`, `cma.missed_callbacks()`, `cma.record_url()`, `cma.missed_callbacks_summary()` |


**Team screen routes (night build, 8 October 2026, migration 0004)**, for people holding `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all`; the database decides who is listed and who may be edited (`CMA06` → 403). `GET /api/v1/team/people` stays the time-kept list for Add day, so the directory has its own path:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/team/directory` | the people with employer, zone, role, `isManaging`, `timeKept`, teams, skills with levels and `mayEdit` | `listDirectory` → `cma.directory()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/directory` | add a person: `{ email, displayName, organisationKey, roleKey, loginSystem, loginId, timeZone? }`; answers `{ userId }` with 201, the same id for the same person again, 409 for a different id or an inactive person, 403 for a managing role without `users.manage_all` | `addPerson` → `cma.add_person()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/role` | `{ roleKey }`; own role or a managing role from a manager 403 | `setPersonRole` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/active` | `{ active }`; oneself 403 | `setPersonActive` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/teams` | `{ teamKeys }`, the full list; unknown team 404 | `setPersonTeams` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/skills` | `{ skills: [ { key, level? } ] }`, the full list; needs `skills.manage` too; a level outside the scale 400 | `setPersonSkills` |
