| `GET /api/v1/team/roles`, `/teams`, `/skills`, `/organisations` | the ladder with `assignable`, the current teams with member counts, the catalog with its scale per dimension, the active employers with their zone | `listRoles`, `listTeams`, `listSkills`, `listOrganisations` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/directory/linked-accounts` | the ids a person can hold (`systems`: `<adapter>_owner` per customer-records connection, `<adapter>_user` per telephony connection, with the connections' names and whether one is active) and the ids held by the people the caller may see (`accounts`: user id, system, id); since addition 0008e | `getLinkedAccounts` → `cma.linked_account_systems()`, `cma.linked_accounts()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/linked-accounts` | `{ system, externalId }`, `externalId` null or empty unlinks; a person the caller may not edit 403 (also oneself), an id another person holds 409, a system that is not `<name>_owner` or `<name>_user` or an id with spaces 400; answers the caller's linked accounts; since addition 0008e | `setLinkedAccount` → `cma.set_user_external_id()`, `cma.remove_user_external_id()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/invitations` | the invitations the caller may see (since addition 0008l): address, name, language, role, employer, teams, skills, `state` (`open`, `expired`, `accepted`, `revoked`), who sent, accepted or revoked them and when; never a token; the route answers 403 itself without `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all` and the database checks again | `listInvitations` → `cma.invitations()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/invitations` | `{ email, displayName, locale, roleKey, organisationKey, teamKeys?, skills? }`; answers `{ invitationId, token, expiresAt }` with 201, the token only here and in renew (the screen makes it the link); a role above the caller's ceiling or the system role 403, an unknown employer, role, team or skill 404, a person or a pending invitation with the address 409, an address outside the domain setting or a level off the scale 400 | `createInvitation` → `cma.create_invitation()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/invitations/{id}/renew` | a new link: `{ invitationId, token, expiresAt }`; the old link stops working, the caller becomes the inviter of the link; accepted or revoked 409, above the ceiling 403 | `renewInvitation` → `cma.renew_invitation()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/invitations/{id}/revoke` | revokes an open or expired invitation (again: no change), answers the list; accepted 409, above the ceiling 403 | `revokeInvitation` → `cma.revoke_invitation()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/memberships` | current team memberships of everyone, for the Live board's team filter | `listTeamMembersNow` → `cma.team_members_now()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/shifts-today` | today's published roster entry per person whose time is kept (migration 0005), for the board's shift line; `monitoring.live` | `getRosterToday` → `cma.roster_today()` |

**Roster routes (night build, 8 October 2026, migration 0005)**, for people holding `roster.manage` except where stated; a week is named by its Monday:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/roster/weeks?team=&from=&to=` | the weeks of a team (or the tenant without `team`) in the range as a calendar, one row per week with state, version and counts (an unwritten week is a draft at version 0); the planner's browser shows the written ones; at most two years | `listRosterWeeks` → `cma.roster_weeks()` |
| `GET /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}?team=` | the planner's week: `{ header, people, entries, coverage }` | `getRosterWeek` → `roster_week()`, `roster_people()`, `roster_entries()`, `roster_coverage()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}/cells` | `{ team?, userId, date, cell }` with `cell` as `{ kind: "shift", start, end, note? }`, `{ kind: "absence", absenceKey, note? }` or `{ kind: "clear" }`; answers the current cell, null after a clear; the past, a date outside the week, a person off the grid, bad times 400; another roster that day 409; unknown absence 404 | `setRosterEntry` → `cma.roster_set_entry()` |
| `POST /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}/publish` | `{ team? }`; answers the header with its new version | `publishRoster` → `cma.roster_publish()` |
| `POST /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}/copy` | `{ team?, from }`; answers `{ copied }` | `copyRosterWeek` → `cma.roster_copy_week()` |
| `GET /api/v1/roster/absence-types` | the active absence types in order; any person | `listAbsenceTypes` → `cma.absence_types()` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/roster?from=&to=` | the caller's own schedule, published entries only, one row per date with `isPublished`; `roster.view`; at most 92 days | `getMyRoster` → `cma.my_roster()` |

**Configuration routes (night build, 9 October 2026, migration 0005a)**, for people holding `tenant.configure` (the admin in the default ladder), named after the data; every write answers the full list as the database now holds it, so a screen never shows a guess:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/statuses`, `PUT` | every status with its four flags, the default, active or retired and its usage; `PUT { key, name, isWorking, isProductive, isPaid, isBillable, sortOrder }` adds, changes or reactivates; frozen flags and a non-working default 409 | `listConfigStatuses`, `upsertConfigStatus` |
| `PUT /api/v1/configuration/statuses/{key}/default`, `…/retired` | the default (an active working status, else 409); retire (the default or the last working status 409) | `setDefaultStatus`, `retireStatus` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/app-links`, `PUT`, `PUT …/{key}/retired`, `GET /api/v1/configuration/permissions` | the links, active or retired; `PUT { key, label, address, permissionKey, sortOrder }` (https only 400, unknown permission 404); the permission catalog for the choice | `listConfigAppLinks`, `upsertAppLink`, `retireAppLink`, `listPermissions` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/absence-types`, `PUT`, `PUT …/{key}/retired` | the absence types, active or retired; `PUT { key, name, isPaid, sortOrder }` | `listAbsenceTypes`, `upsertAbsenceType`, `retireAbsenceType` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/coverage-targets`, `PUT` | every target across teams; `PUT { teamKey, skillKey, weekday 1..7, minCount 0..99 }`, 0 clears; needs `roster.manage` as well | `listCoverageTargets`, `setCoverageTarget` |
| `PUT /api/v1/configuration/teams`, `PUT …/{key}/dissolved`, `PUT /api/v1/configuration/skills`, `PUT …/{dimension}/levels` | `{ key, name, markets, sortOrder }` adds, changes or revives a team; dissolve ends it and its memberships; `{ dimension, key, name, sortOrder, active }` adds, changes, retires or reactivates a skill; `{ levels: [ { level, name } ] }` replaces a dimension's scale (empty is binary; levels in use 409) | `upsertTeam`, `dissolveTeam`, `upsertSkill`, `setSkillLevels` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/form-categories`, `PUT …` | the tenant's form categories with how many forms carry each; `PUT { code, name, sortOrder, isActive }` adds, renames or switches one on or off, never deletes; answers the list | read: `tenant.configure`, `reports.view` or `performance.team`; write: `tenant.configure` | `getFormCategories`, `setFormCategory` → `cma.set_form_category()`, `cma.set_form_category_status()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/configuration/settings` | `{ key, value }` writes one tenant setting, `value` null resets it to the catalog default (unknown key 404, a value outside the catalog 400); reading stays at `GET /api/v1/settings` | `setTenantSetting` → `cma.set_tenant_setting()` |

`GET /api/v1/roster/absence-types` and the planner leave retired absence types out since 0005a (nothing could retire one before).

**Intake report routes (B11, 12 October 2026, migration 0007d)**, read only, named after the data. Each route answers 403 itself when the caller holds none of its function's permissions, and the function checks again in the same transaction (`CMA06` → 403); every range is at most 92 days, refused with 400 by the route and again by the database (`CMA04`). Ids, times, codes and counts only: no customer name, email or phone number, and an owner id is never returned as an agent:

| Route | Does | Permission | Data |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/reports/speed-to-lead?from=&to=&group=` | per group (`day`, `market`, `agent` or `pipeline`; `day` when left out, anything else 400): deals, called, median and 80th percentile business minutes over the called deals (`p80BusinessMinutes` is returned, not shown: the screen dropped the column on 15 October 2026, Decision log), % within target and % before the next noon over the deals whose outcome is known, open not called; an agent whose owner is not mapped is the key `unknown` with a null label | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getSpeedToLeadSummary` → `cma.speed_to_lead_summary()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/speed-to-lead/rows?from=&to=` | one row per lead deal: the deal's and the first call's ids in the CRM, market and its source, pipeline, status (`called`, `not_called_open`, `not_called_closed`), the call times, the agent's user id (null when not mapped), the measures, and `recordUrl` from the connection's `record_url_deal` template (null without one) | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getSpeedToLeadRows` → `cma.speed_to_lead_rows()`, `cma.record_url()` (0008) |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/follow-up/leads`, `…/tickets`, `…/tasks` with `?market=&owner=&state=attention` | what is waiting right now (addition 0008o, 16 October 2026): the open lead deals not called, longest waiting first (owner as a person or `ownerUserId` null with the source's `ownerRef`, shown as Not mapped; market; created; business seconds waited; the age in business days and its bucket; `pastTarget`; the last call; `recordUrl`); the open tickets of counted pipelines against their response time, the least time left first and no business hours last (`slaMinutes` in force, `slaState` `ok`, `at_risk`, `breached` or `no_hours`, `minutesToBreach`); the open tasks, due first (type, `isCallback`, priority, status, `dueState` `overdue`, `today`, `later` or `none`, the deal or ticket by id; never a task's text). The filters narrow a list (`lib/follow-up`: a market code or `unknown`, a person's id or `none`, `attention` for leads past the target, tickets at risk or breached, tasks overdue) and are read back as `filter`. Since addition 0008s the lists come from the follow-up snapshot, every row (no cap: the page shows the first `follow_up.list_rows`), and the answer carries `computedAt`, when the snapshot was computed | `reports.view`, `performance.team` or `monitoring.live` | `getOpenLeads`, `getOpenTickets`, `getOpenTasks` → `cma.open_leads()`, `cma.open_tickets()`, `cma.open_tasks()`, each joined with the owner's name and `cma.record_url()`; since 0008s on `cma.follow_up_item`, `computedAt` from `cma.follow_up_computed_at()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/follow-up/summary` | the eight counts: leads not called and past the target, tickets at risk, breached and without owner, tasks overdue, due today and with an owner nobody maps to; since addition 0008s from the snapshot, with `computedAt` | `monitoring.live`, `performance.team` or `workday.team` | `getFollowUpSummary` → `cma.follow_up_summary()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/follow-up/by-owner` | per person (`displayName`), by name, then one row with `ownerUserId` null for everything whose owner maps to nobody: open leads, past the target, open tickets, at risk or breached, tasks overdue and due today; since addition 0008s from the snapshot, each row with `computedAt` (the answer stays an array) | `performance.team` only (the employee-monitoring point) | `getFollowUpByOwner` → `cma.follow_up_by_owner()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/forms?from=&to=&group=&market=&category=&form=` | the forms report (addition 0008p): every polled form's submissions per day or ISO week (`day` when left out, anything else 400), form and market, with the contact resolution split, `totals` and `previous` (the previous equal period, like for like), `historyStart`, the categories, forms and markets for the filters, `forms` false when no source has a form; at most 92 days; names, codes and counts only | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getFormsReport` → `cma.forms_report()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/forms/answers?…&field=&value=`, `GET /api/v1/reports/forms/export?…` | the kept choice fields' answers per form, market, field and option (never a free-text answer), `period` current or previous; the report as a CSV download in the tenant's export format and the caller's language | the same | `getFormsAnswers` → `cma.forms_answers()`; `getFormsReport`, `listSettings` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/intake?from=&to=` | per business date and market: form submissions, deals and tickets created and closed, calls in and out, tagged calls, new customers, first and repeat orders, renewals; with `connections` (`telephony`, `commerce`) and the tenant's `startDate` (`intake.start_date`). A telephony figure is null when the tenant has no telephony connected, a commerce figure null without a shop: never 0 for a source that is not there | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getIntake` → `cma.intake_per_day()`, the connection state, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/data-quality` | every check of `cma.data_quality_checks()` in its order with key, description and total (null for a check whose source is not installed) | `reports.view` or `tenant.configure` | `getDataQuality` → `cma.data_quality()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/inbound?from=&to=&group=` | inbound calls per group (`day`, `market`, `line` or `hour`; `day` when left out, anything else 400; since addition 0008i): offered, answered, unanswered, waiting, answered within the service level and its denominator, the service level, the average and 80th percentile time to answer (`p80AnswerSeconds` is returned, not shown since 15 October 2026), unanswered calls per reason code; `total` over every call (the database's own, not a sum); the tenant's missed-reason catalog for the labels, `serviceLevelSeconds` and `waitingMaxMinutes`; `telephony` false and no rows when no telephony is connected, never 0 | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getInboundService` → `cma.inbound_service()`, `cma.missed_reasons()`, the connection state, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/calls?from=&to=&group=&market=&line=` | the calls overview (since addition 0008j): per group (`day`, `week`, `market` or `line`; `day` when left out, anything else 400) the calls, inbound, outbound, answered inbound, connected outbound and its share, conversations with their total and average talk time, the connected outbound calls' talk time, untagged conversations; `total` (the database's own, not a sum), `previous` (the same over the previous period, like for like: `previousFrom` and `previousTo`, and `previousPartial` true when the period reaches today, so the previous one holds the same elapsed dates, the last up to the current time of day), `historyStart` (since addition 0008k: the business date of the tenant's earliest stored call, tenant-wide whatever the filters, null without one; a previous span from before it has nothing to compare with, and the page shows no change), `connectedMinSeconds`, and the `markets` and `lines` with calls in the period for the filters (the lines within the chosen market); `market` a code or `unknown` and `line` a connection id ':' line ref, else 400; `telephony` false and no rows when no telephony is connected, never 0 | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getCallsOverview` → `cma.calls_overview()`, `cma.telephony_history_start()`, the connection state, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/calls/tag-use?…` | the same query: per group and current tag the conversations carrying it (name, `isCounted`), the untagged ones (`tagKey` null), then the whole period (`groupKey` null) | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getCallsTagUse` → `cma.calls_tag_use()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/calls-per-agent?from=&to=&market=&line=` | per person by name: answered inbound with talk time, outbound, connected with talk time, conversations, untagged, the first and last call's time of day; the not-linked row with `unlinkedUsers`; `total`; never a telephony user id | `performance.team` | `getCallsPerAgent` → `cma.calls_per_person()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/calls-per-agent/tags?…` | the same query: per person (and the not-linked calls) and counted tag the conversations carrying it, then their untagged ones | `performance.team` | `getCallsPerAgentTags` → `cma.calls_per_person_tags()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/email?from=&to=&group=&market=&inbox=&account=` | the email report (addition 0008t): `rows` per day or ISO week (`day` when left out, anything else 400), `total` and `previous` (like for like) with received, logged replies, agent sends, automated, bounced and failed, conversations opened and closed, first replies (medians elapsed and in business time, within the target, without business hours) and time to close; the same per inbox, mailbox, category and market; received per date and inbox or mailbox; `waiting` and `notResponding`, each capped by `follow_up.list_rows` with its total; `historyStart`; the settings the hints name; the filter lists; `email` false (no inbox and no email: no rows, never 0). Filters: a market code or `unknown`, an inbox or mailbox as connection id `:` source id | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getEmailReport` → `cma.email_report_page()` (the report and, with a filter, the filter lists in one pass, since 0008x), `email_waiting()`, `email_not_responding()`, `email_history_start()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/email-per-agent?from=&to=&market=&inbox=&account=` | per person by name, never ranked: sent, first replies with the medians and the share within the target, closed, and the sends per hour of day; the not-linked row with `unlinkedOwners`; `total`. Not there (404) while the tenant's `reports.per_person` does not hold `email` | `performance.team` and `reports.per_person` holding `email` | `getEmailPerAgent` → `cma.email_per_person_page()` (per person and the filter lists in one pass, since 0008x) |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/first-orders?from=&to=&group=&market=&level=` | first orders (addition 0008u): `rows` per day, ISO week, market or level (`day` when left out), `total` and `previous` (like for like) with via the call center, self-service, repeat, renewals, not first, without a contact and the median days from the first touch; per level and per market; `amounts` and `amountsByMarket` per currency (never summed across); `list` (newest first, capped by `follow_up.list_rows`, with the total and each deal's link); `historyStart`, `phoneStart`; the attribution `settings`; `connected` false without an order pipeline. Filters: a market code or `unknown`, a level (one of the six evidence levels or `self_service`) | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getFirstOrders` → `cma.first_orders_page()` (the report, the amounts and the list in one pass, since 0008x), `first_orders_history_start()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/first-orders/export?…` | the first orders as a CSV download in the tenant's export format and the caller's language: the deal id, when, market, level, attribution, each evidence, currency, amount, days from the first touch; up to 500 rows | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getFirstOrdersExport`, `listSettings` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/orders-per-person?from=&to=&market=&level=` | the first orders via the call center per credited person by name, never ranked, with the levels and the median days; the not-linked row; `total`. Not there (404) while `reports.per_person` does not hold `orders` (Pulse4all: off until Yordi confirms it) | `performance.team` and `reports.per_person` holding `orders` | `getOrdersPerPerson` → `cma.first_orders_per_person()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/callbacks-kept?from=&to=` | callbacks kept (addition 0008w): per ISO week and in total the callbacks (a contact and a due time), kept, not kept, still open, without a contact, without a due time; `previous` (like for like); `connected` false while no callback type counts; `windowMinutes` | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getCallbacksKept` → `cma.callbacks_kept_report()`, `callbacks_kept_connected()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/callbacks-kept/by-owner?from=&to=` | per task owner by name, never ranked, the owners without a person as one row with `unlinkedOwners`, the total last | `performance.team` | `getCallbacksPerOwner` → `cma.callbacks_kept_per_owner()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/attempts?from=&to=&group=&market=&pipeline=&scope=` | attempts per deal (addition 0008y): `current` and `previous` (like for like) with the deals, those attempted, the attempts, the average and median per deal, those reached on the first attempt, any connected, the open deals and those never attempted, won and lost; `buckets` 0 to `attempts.max_bucket` (the top `n+`) with the deals, the first connected attempts, won and lost; `groups` per day, ISO week, market or pipeline (`day` when left out, anything else 400); `sinceLast` (the open deals by business days since the last attempt, those of a market without business hours as `noHours`); `list` (open first, the longest waiting first, then closed by the newest close; capped by `follow_up.list_rows`, with the total and each deal's link); the filter lists, `historyStart` and the `settings` the hints name. Filters: a market code or `unknown`, a pipeline as connection id `:` pipeline id, the scope `lead` (the default) or `counted`; a value the database would refuse is a 400 here first | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getAttempts` → `cma.attempts_per_deal_page()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/attempts-per-agent?from=&to=&market=&pipeline=&scope=` | per deal owner by name, never ranked: the deals they own, those attempted, the attempts (by anyone), the average per deal, those reached on the first attempt, the open deals never attempted; the owners without a person (or deals without an owner) as one row with `unlinkedOwners`; `total`. Not there (404) while `reports.per_person` does not hold `attempts` (Pulse4all: off until Yordi agrees) | `performance.team` and `reports.per_person` holding `attempts` | `getAttemptsPerAgent` → `cma.attempts_per_person_page()` |

The connection state is read in the same transaction, after the report function has checked the permission, under row-level security: telephony counts as connected when an active connection has a line in the telephony catalog (`telephony_number`) or a telephony call (0007a), commerce when an active connection has its store (`commerce_store`, 0007b, one store per commerce connection). No function answers this for a report reader: `cma.connections_all()` is `tenant.configure` only and names adapters, which are vendor values. The Dashboard's Intake row also reads `getCrmRecordsPerDay` → `cma.crm_records_per_day()` (0006, `performance.team`) for the deals and tickets created; it has no route of its own.

**Sources, markets and alert rules routes (night of 13 October 2026, addition 0008e)**, for people holding `tenant.configure`; each route checks the permission itself and the function checks it again (`CMA06` → 403). Every write goes through the function that existed for it (Data model: sources configuration) and answers what the database now holds. No route accepts or returns a secret value: a connection shows the names of its secrets only, and a setting key containing secret, token, password or key is refused (400) before the database refuses it too. A connection is never created here, because a connection names its secrets (the runbook does that).

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/sources` | every connection with id, adapter, name, account id, kind, the kinds its rows imply, status, secret names, non-secret settings, events in 24 hours, waiting, parked, the last event and its catalog sizes | `listSources` → `cma.sources()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/sources/{id}`, `PUT` | one connection with its pipelines, forms, call outcomes, fields, write-back fields, lines, tags, store and record link samples; a malformed id 400, an unknown one 404. `PUT { name?, isActive?, kind? }`: the name, switching it off or on, the kind (one the rows contradict 409) | `getSource`, `setSourceConnection` → `cma.sources()`, `cma.connection_catalogs()`, `cma.connection_config()`; `upsert_connection` with the stored secret names, `set_connection_status`, `set_connection_kind` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/settings` | `{ settings }`, every non-secret setting as a flat object of text, numbers and switches, at most 4 kB (the screen previews a record link template `record_url_<record type>` with the newest record id and shows no link unless the result is a plain https address) | `setSourceSettings` → `cma.set_connection_settings()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/pipelines` | `{ recordType, pipelineId, isCounted, isLead, isOrder? }`; lead for deals only (400); since addition 0008u the optional order pipeline switch, deals only (400) | `setSourcePipeline` → `cma.set_connection_pipeline()` (current routing kept), `cma.set_pipeline_lead()`, `cma.set_pipeline_order()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/stages` | `{ pipelineId, stageId, outcome }`: a deal stage's outcome, `won`, `lost` or `none`, set by a person and kept over every later read (addition 0008u); `GET …/sources/{id}` gains each pipeline's `isOrder` and `stages` with `outcome` and `outcomeSource` | `setSourceStageOutcome` → `cma.set_stage_outcome()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/forms` | `{ forms: [ { formId, isCounted, leadSource, market } ] }`, one form or a rule's whole preview in one transaction (current kept fields kept) | `setSourceForms` → `cma.set_form()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/call-outcomes` | `{ outcomes: [ { outcomeRef, isConnected } ] }`, one or a rule's preview in one transaction | `setSourceCallOutcomes` → `cma.set_call_outcome()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/lines`, `…/tags`, `…/store` | `{ lineRef, market, isCounted }`; `{ tagRef, isCounted }`; `{ handle, name, market, currency, timeZone }` (a handle another store holds 409) | `setSourceLine`, `setSourceTag`, `setSourceStore` → `cma.set_telephony_number()`, `cma.set_telephony_tag()`, `cma.upsert_commerce_store()` |
| `GET …/sources/{id}/lines/stored-calls?lineRef=` | the stored calls of one line and the first one's start (since addition 0008y, backlog CMA-020): what switching it off removes, which its confirmation names; no `lineRef` 400, an unknown line or connection 404 | `getSourceLineStoredCalls` → `cma.telephony_number_stored_calls()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/missed-reasons` | `{ code, label, countsInServiceLevel, needsCallback }`: one missed-call reason of a telephony connection (since addition 0008i); a connection of another kind 400. `GET …/sources/{id}` gains `missedReasons` (the codes seen on the connection's unanswered inbound calls and its adapter's catalog rows, with the label and switches in force) and, for a telephony connection, `sampleIds.call` (its newest call id) for the preview of a `record_url_call` template | `setSourceMissedReason` → `cma.set_telephony_missed_reason()`; `cma.connection_missed_reasons()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/pipeline-sla` | `{ recordType: "ticket", pipelineId, slaMinutes }`: a ticket pipeline's own response time in business minutes, 1 to 100000, or null for the tenant's `tickets.sla_minutes` (addition 0008o); a deal pipeline or a value outside the bounds 400, an unknown pipeline 404. `GET …/sources/{id}` gains `slaMinutes` on every pipeline | `setSourcePipelineSla` → `cma.set_pipeline_sla()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/task-statuses`, `…/task-types` | `{ statusRef, label, isDone }`; `{ typeRef, label, isCallback, isCounted }`: one task status or type of a CRM connection (addition 0008n); another kind of connection 400. `GET …/sources/{id}` gains `taskStatuses` and `taskTypes` (each with its task counts and last seen) and the newest task id under `sampleIds.task` for the link preview of `record_url_task` | `setSourceTaskStatus`, `setSourceTaskType` → `cma.set_task_status()`, `cma.set_task_type()`; the read `cma.connection_task_catalogs()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/inboxes`, `…/inbox-channels`, `…/mailboxes` | `{ inboxId, isCounted, market }`; `{ channelId, isCounted }`; `{ accountId, label, isCounted, market }` (a label with an `@` 400: the CMA keeps no address): an inbox, a source channel or a mailbox of a CRM connection's conversations (0008m; mailboxes since addition 0008t); `GET …/sources/{id}` gains them with the connection's email settings | `setSourceInbox`, `setSourceInboxChannel`, `setSourceMailbox` → `cma.set_inbox()`, `cma.set_inbox_channel()`, `cma.set_inbox_account()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/markets`, `PUT` | every market (inactive ones too) with its aliases, the office hours per market and weekday (`*` the default) and the holidays; `PUT { code, name, timeZone, languageCode, currency, languageSkillKey, minLanguageLevel, sortOrder, active }` adds or changes one | `getMarkets`, `upsertMarket` → `cma.markets_all()`, `cma.business_hours_all()`, `cma.business_holidays_all()`; `cma.upsert_market()`, `cma.set_market_status()` |
| `PUT …/markets/aliases`, `…/hours`, `…/holidays` | `{ alias, code }` (`code` null removes); `{ market, days: [ { weekday, hours } ] }` (`hours` like `9:00-17:30`, empty for closed; `market` `*` the default); `{ market, day, name }` (`name` null removes) | `setMarketAlias`, `setOfficeHours`, `setHoliday` → `cma.set_market_alias()`, `cma.remove_market_alias()`, `cma.set_business_hours()`, `cma.set_business_holiday()`, `cma.remove_business_holiday()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/alert-rules`, `PUT` | every rule of both triggers with its alerts in 30 days, the pipelines a rule can name and the default age limit (`alerts.max_age_minutes`); `PUT { name, trigger, recordType, pipelineIds, urgency, maxAgeMinutes, audiencePermission?, matchLanguage?, onlyClockedIn?, fallbackPermission? }` (the audience for new-record rules only; a name another trigger's rule holds 409) | `getAlertRules`, `upsertAlertRule` → `cma.message_rules()`; `cma.upsert_message_rule()`, `cma.upsert_assignment_rule()` |
| `PUT …/alert-rules/{id}/enabled` | `{ enabled }` switches a rule off or on; rules are never deleted | `setAlertRuleEnabled` → `cma.set_message_rule_enabled()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/documents/{name}` | one document of the System health page as a download (Markdown or CSV; addition 0008r): the name must be on the allowlist in `src/lib/health.ts` and in the manifest the build wrote (`documents/manifest.json`); any other name, a separator or `..` is 404, and the directory is never listed | the image's `documents/` directory; no database |

**Campaign monitoring routes (addition 0008v, night run N19-A)**, read only, for `reports.view` or `performance.team` (the route and the database both check): `GET /api/v1/journeys` answers `{ journeys }`, every journey of the tenant but the archived ones in their order (code, name, summary, market, status, design link, prerequisites; `getJourneys` → `cma.journeys()`), and `GET /api/v1/journeys/{code}` one of them, an archived or unknown code 404. Placeholders as configuration: no figures and no customer data.

Status codes:
 401 no identity, 403 no access, `CMA01` 403, `CMA02` 404, `CMA03` 409 (an ended day, or since 0004 and 0005 a conflict: an existing person with other details, another roster's entry that day), `CMA04` and invalid input 400, `CMA05` 500, `CMA06` 403, database unreachable 503. Every POST and PUT requires the header `x-cma-request: 1` and refuses a cross-site `Sec-Fetch-Site`, so another website cannot end someone's day, change their status, correct a day, change a role or plan a shift through their IAP session; `/logout/end` keeps its same-origin check, and `/invite/accept` (addition 0008l) has the same one (`src/lib/origin.ts`: `Sec-Fetch-Site` `same-origin`, else an `Origin` whose host is the request's; a malformed `Origin` is refused, since 15 October 2026 also on `/logout/end`, where it threw).

**Status and clock (since 6 October 2026).** A workday carries `statusKey`, `statusSince` (start of the current status) and `clock`: `closedSeconds` worked in closed stretches plus `runningSince` while the current status has `is_working`, read from `cma.time_interval`. Nothing in it changes between two status changes, so the screen ticks locally and two reads of an unchanged day are equal. The clock counts the same time as My hours (`is_working`), so it pauses on Break and Lunch with the seeded flags. My day shows the tenant's statuses as buttons in the tenant's order; no key or name is in code or copy. Keyboard: digits switch pages, S moves into the status buttons, arrows and Enter choose. The buttons call `POST /api/v1/me/status`, the path the verifier proves.

**Team hours (since 6 October 2026, increment c2).** A Report screen at `/team/hours`, shown to people holding `workday.team` and checked again by the database on every read and correction. Page keys follow the visible order of the pages a person may see (agents 1 and 2, supervisors 1 to 3). The period bar (today, week, month, custom range up to 92 days; T, W, M, C) is shared with My hours; a person filter (P) lists `team_people`. One row per person per day: date, person, employer, clocked in, clocked out, worked (`is_working`) and paid (`is_paid`) minutes, note chips (Still clocked in, Not clocked out, Needs a correction, Corrected afterwards) and Correct; totals below. Times are shown in each day's own zone, with the zone's short name when the person's clock differs from the viewer's. Correct is disabled on the viewer's own rows. **Correct a day** opens the day's effective events as rows in the day's zone: a 24-hour `hh:mm` time (905, 9:05 and 0905 all become 09:05), a status from the tenant's list or Clocked out, Remove and Add row; the earliest row is the clock-in. On the repeated autumn hour the editor asks which of the two times is meant, a time in the skipped spring hour is refused, and a time in the future, a clock-out first, two clock-outs or anything after the clock-out are explained before saving. Saving sends the smallest diff to the corrections route (a changed row replaces its event, a removed row voids it, a new row adds one, the start first) with one reason of 3 to 500 characters; the history of the day shows every event with source, approver, reason and replaced rows struck through. **Add day** (A) asks for a person (not oneself) and a date up to today and opens the same editor. Keyboard: Down or Up enters the table, the arrows move between days, Enter opens the editor, Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc closes (twice with unsaved changes) and returns focus to the row (c2.1). The logic lives in `web/src/lib/corrections.ts`, pure and without runtime imports. Mock mode has a fictional team of five over two employers and three zones, with a day still open, a forgotten clock-out, a missing day and a corrected day; the dev seed's `supervisor` and `manager` identities hold `workday.team` there.

**Exports (since 7 October 2026, increment c3).** Team hours shows Download hours (H) and Download status changes (X) to people holding `workday.export`; the files cover the period and person on screen. The export function checks the permission and the tenant's settings are read in the same transaction, so a file never mixes two configurations. The format follows the settings (separator, decimal mark, date format, duration format, byte order mark), with CRLF line ends and RFC 4180 quoting; a decimal comma with a comma separator stays one quoted cell. Times are 24-hour `hh:mm` in each day's own zone, the zone named per row; durations are whole minutes first (as on screen), then written in the chosen format. Column names follow the viewer's language from `copy.ts`. Every text cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or carriage return gets a leading apostrophe, so a name typed by a person never runs as a formula. Answers carry `content-disposition: attachment`, `no-store` and `nosniff`; the file name holds only the dates. Hours file (provisional until compared with Steam's figures, see Open decisions): date, person, employer, time zone, clocked in, clocked out, worked, paid, billable, not clocked out, corrected afterwards. Status-changes file: date, person, employer, time zone, status, from, to (empty while open), duration, working, paid, billable, entered by (person, system, correction, import since addition 0008d), not clocked out. Known limit: on the autumn clock-change night a time between 02:00 and 02:59 reads twice; durations stay right. Mock mode uses Dutch conventions as fixture settings; the mock `manager` holds `workday.export`, the mock `supervisor` does not, as in the default ladder.

**Welcome and Clock in (since 7 October 2026, increment e1).** The landing page at `/` for everyone with a role, built as blocks per permission: the greeting by time of day in the person's zone with the display name as stored (there is no first-name field, and splitting a name is a guess), the date (the shift line comes with the roster); Updates for everyone (an empty state until Messaging, migration 0008, then the demo's list with the urgent chip, title, body, time and sender); Before today's shift for `workday.team`, Steam's to-do line: the days not clocked out in the last 31 days from Team hours (`needsCorrection`, today excluded) with a link to Team hours; Reports for `performance.team`, a link to the Dashboard that grows into the one view of all KPIs and metrics for analytics (Roadmap step 9, each KPI as its source connects); the Clock in card for `workday.own` when there is no day today (the tenant's default status name from `/me/statuses`, the button focused so Enter clocks in, `POST /api/v1/me/day/start`), the open day's state otherwise (status with its colour, clocked in at, time worked so far, a link to My day), or the ended day; and the tenant's app links, each in a new tab. A visit opens nothing: the hand check in prod no longer opens the checker's day, IAP's silent re-login and an evening look at hours open nothing, and someone being added gets no day until they clock in. My day moved to `/day` and shows the same Clock in card when there is no day, so page 2 never dead-ends on an empty status grid; Clock out stays on My day and Log out. The end-of-day texts say "clock in tomorrow" instead of "log in". Mock mode has fictional links, one of them for `workday.team` only.

**Messages (since 11 October 2026, migration 0008, brief B12).** `GET /api/v1/me/messages?since=<ISO instant>&limit=` lists the caller's own messages newest first with `unreadCount` (the whole unread count, not only the listed ones); the first return of a message sets its delivered time; `since` lists only messages created after it, which is what the open Workspace polls. `POST /api/v1/me/messages/read` with `{ ids }` marks own deliveries read and answers how many changed (other people's ids change nothing); `POST /api/v1/me/messages/<id>/ack` acknowledges one own message (404 when it is not the caller's). `POST /api/v1/messages` with `{ title, body?, urgency?, target }` sends a message for `messages.send`: the target names exactly one of `{ "everyone": true }`, `{ "teams": [keys] }`, `{ "language": { "skill", "minLevel" } }` or `{ "users": [ids] }`, optionally with `"onlyClockedIn": true`; the body is checked by the pure `src/lib/messages.ts` before the database (a 400 with a code), the database resolves the recipients and refuses a target that reaches nobody (CMA04, 400) or an unknown team, skill or person (CMA02, 404); the answer is the id and the recipient count (201). `GET /api/v1/messages/stats?from&to` (at most 92 days) gives per message recipients, delivered, read, acknowledged and the median seconds to read, for `messages.send` or `performance.team`. The screens: `MessageCenter.tsx` (client, in the Shell's top bar for everyone: the poll loop, the badge and title, toasts, desktop notifications, the urgent dialog), `Inbox.tsx` with `src/app/messages/page.tsx`, `MessageComposer.tsx` with `src/app/messages/send/page.tsx` (the sent list is server-rendered from the stats); `verify/messages.mjs` (24 checks) proves the validation and the poller's decisions. In mock mode three fixture messages exist per tenant and a synthetic "New deal" alert arrives every three minutes, so the toast and the inbox can be seen without a source.

**Live board (since 7 October 2026, increment b).** A Live screen at `/live/board`, the first page of a new Live group, shown to people holding `monitoring.live` (supervisor, manager and analytics in the default ladder) and checked again by the database on every read. One read per page (`team_now`); everything else is computed in `src/lib/live.ts`, pure, with `web/verify/live.mjs`. Tiles with counts: the flag groups through the Dashboard's rule (`groupOf` in `lib/dashboard`, imported with its `.ts` extension so Node's type stripping can run the verifier; one definition for both screens), shown only for a group that at least one active status belongs to (Pulse4all has no Paid pause tile), plus Clocked out (today's day ended) and Not clocked in (no day today); a tile is also a filter. Then one row per person: name, employer, status with its colour dot (an inactive status marked), since, clocked in and clocked out in the person's own zone with the zone's short name only when the person's clock differs from the viewer's (Madrid next to Amsterdam shows none, London shows BST), worked so far; rows by group then name, no ranking (the employee-monitoring decision). Since and worked so far tick each second on the client from `statusSince` and the clock's inputs, as My day does; the page re-reads itself with `router.refresh()` every 10 seconds while the tab is visible, pauses while hidden and reads once more on return (the fallback until the realtime service). Filters: Group (G) and Employer (E), client-side, kept across refreshes; team later (0004). Reserved, not shown: today's shift (roster, 0005) and the on-call state from telephony (Sync, step 3). In mock mode today's fictional team is laid out relative to now, so every state shows at any hour. Compliance: live per-person status to supervisors, managers and analytics is monitoring in the Spanish and Dutch sense; the board goes to Yordi together with the Dashboard point before real agent hours (Open decisions). Checked in prod on 7 October 2026 (`a2b805e`): two people, Martin in Available - Operations with the clock's inputs equal to his own-day read, Finn without a day, seven status flags, no Paid pause tile. **Inbound today and the missed calls (since addition 0008i, brief B15).** The board opens with a tile row from `GET /api/v1/telephony/inbound-now` (inbound, answered, unanswered as a share of the calls with an outcome, service level, average time to answer, waiting now, longest waiting in the board's clock format) and the card **Missed, not called back**: one row per caller, oldest first, with line, market, reason, missed at, waiting in office time of the line's market ("Closed", muted, with the time since the call in its title, while no office time has passed since the call, since the IT audit of 17 October 2026; the time since the call, muted, for a line without a market), times missed (since the caller's latest callback, within the lookback) and a link icon only when the telephony connection has a `record_url_call` template; then the count of anonymous missed calls. A tenant without telephony sees "No telephony connected", never 0. Every tile and header explains itself in a hint, with the service level's seconds, the lookback and the waiting limit filled in from the tenant's settings. One poll for the whole page (`LiveRefresh.tsx`, 10 seconds while visible) reads the people, the tiles and the missed calls again together; the people table scrolls inside its own box at 1280 px. Never a number, a name or the caller's hash.

**People (since the night build of 8 October 2026, migration 0004).** A Team screen at `/team/people`, the first page of a new **Team** group (people icon) after Live and before Time, shown to people holding `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all` and checked again by the database on every read and write. One row per person: name and email, employer, role (with "manages people" under a managing one), teams and skills as chips (`Dutch · Fluent`, `Sales`), Active or Inactive and whether the clock runs, and Edit, disabled on yourself and, for a manager, on managing roles, as `may_edit` says. Filters: team (T) and Show inactive (I). **Edit person** is one native dialog: the role from the assignable part of the ladder (the held role shown but not choosable when the caller may not assign it), the active flag, the teams as checkboxes, the skills per dimension (a level per language from the tenant's scale, yes or no per work type), read-only without `skills.manage`; Save sends the smallest set of writes (`src/lib/team.ts` `editChanges`: the role, the active flag, the full team list, the full skill list, each only when it changed, the active flag last), Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc closes (twice with unsaved changes). **Add a person** (A) takes name, email, employer (from `cma.organisations()`), role (assignable only), the sign-in provider (prefilled with the one the app runs with, `google` behind IAP, `mock` in dev, as a data value) and the account id, written once and never shown again, and an optional zone; the database writes user, login id and role in one statement. Access requests are out: the manager adds people. Keyboard: Down or Up enters the table, the arrows move, Enter opens the row. Pure logic in `src/lib/team.ts` with `web/verify/team.mjs` (18 checks, provoked). Mock mode has a fictional team with teams and skills, a `manager` (`users.manage_agents`, `skills.manage`, `roster.manage`) and an `admin` identity. **Linked accounts (night of 13 October 2026, addition 0008e):** Edit person shows one field per id the tenant's customer-records and telephony connections use (`Owner id in <connection>`, `User id in <connection>`, and an id still held for a system whose connection is gone as `Id in <system>`), saved with the dialog through `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/linked-accounts` (one call per changed id; an emptied field unlinks). Whoever may edit the person may link them: the data layer reads `may_edit` from `cma.directory()` and the database applies the People rule again; nobody links their own ids on this screen, while the database lets an admin link their own (a seed or Studio path). An id another person holds answers 409 and the dialog says so without changing anything. The list gains a **Linked** column, `n of total` per person with a header hint. Ids are typed as the system shows them; there is no owner sync (Open decisions). `team.mjs` has 26 checks since. **Invitations (night of 15 October 2026, addition 0008l):** **Invite** (V) next to Add a person opens one dialog: work email, name, role (only the roles the caller may give, from `cma.roles()`; the system role is never listed), employer, the invitation's language (the copy's languages, each named in itself), the teams as checkboxes and, for a holder of `skills.manage`, the skills (both optional, as on Edit person; Add a person sets neither). Create shows the link (the page's own origin, `/invite/` and the token), **Copy link** and **Open in mail**, a `mailto` with the subject and body from `copy.ts` in the invitee's language (who invites, to which Workspace, which role, the link, its expiry in the inviter's zone, "sign in with" the address), and says the Workspace sends no email itself. Below the people an **Invitations** section lists the invitations the caller may see: open and expired first with **New link** (the link dialog again, the old link no longer works) and **Revoke** (a second tap on the same button; Esc or leaving it disarms), then accepted (who, when) and revoked (who, when); header hints on State and Expires. Pure logic in `src/lib/invitations.ts` with `web/verify/invitations.mjs` (27 checks, provoked). Mock mode has a fixture per tenant (an open, an expired, an accepted, a revoked and an admin's managing invitation in the main one; an open one in the second) with fixed fictional tokens, and mock identities whose subject starts with `invitee` have no person until they accept.

**The invitation page (night of 15 October 2026, addition 0008l).** `/invite/<token>`, behind IAP like every page, for a signed-in account that need not be a person yet (like No access yet), without the shell, in the invitation's language, `no-referrer` and not indexed. Reading it changes nothing (`cma.invitation_preview`, stable): **Join <workspace>** with "You are invited to the Workspace of <workspace> as <role>", the signed-in address and **Accept** (focused, Enter); or why it cannot be accepted: the link does not work (used, withdrawn or never existed), it has expired, or it is for <invited address> while you are signed in as <address>, with **Use another account** (the signed-out page) when the flag is on and otherwise how to switch (a private window, or signing out of that account first), or the account already works in another business line (no tenant picker yet, so it cannot join a second one; nothing changes). A person of that business line goes to Welcome. Accept is a same-origin form post to `/invite/accept` with the token in the body, not the address: the identity proxy.ts verified (system, id, address) goes to `cma.accept_invitation`, then 303 to `/` (accepted, or already a person there) or back to the invitation, which shows why; Locations are relative and built from a token of the one valid shape only.

**Log out and the signed-out page (night of 15 October 2026).** `/logout/end` answers its 303 by `CMA_LOGOUT_TARGET` (`src/lib/logout.ts`, set by the build's `_LOGOUT_TARGET`): `legacy` (the default) `/logout/done?gcp-iap-mode=CLEAR_LOGIN_COOKIE` as since 5 October 2026, `signed-out` `/signed-out?gcp-iap-mode=CLEAR_LOGIN_COOKIE`, where IAP clears its cookie and redirects to the static page `/signed-out` outside IAP (`web/signed-out/index.html`, Authentication; `docs/signed-out/`). With the flag on, No access yet and an invitation for another address offer "Use another account" to the same address. `cma-web` never serves `/signed-out` (no route, no file; `proxy.ts` gates it like any page). `web/verify/signed-out.mjs` (16 checks, provoked) checks the page (since 15 October 2026 also that Add another account is plain `AddSession` in a new tab, with no `continue` in the page or its script), the flag and the gate, and since the eighth Gemini audit the bucket's public role in `deploy.sh` and `urlmap.py`'s plan on a fixture map (with `python3`), and that `deploy.sh` passes no value to a boolean `gcloud` flag for public access prevention; since the second prod apply also that an unchanged plan is validated but not imported, and that the import is locked on the map's fingerprint while the restore command writes the live one.

**Roster planner, print view and My schedule (since the night build of 8 October 2026, migration 0005).** The **planner** at `/roster/planner` is the second page of the Team group (`roster.manage`): a week bar (previous, this, next week as links, P, W, N, the ISO week number and its dates), the team (T; the tenant's first team by default, or Everyone for a tenant without teams), a browser of the saved weeks with their states, and the grid: people by seven days, one text field per cell, today underlined. Typing `9-17:30`, `9.30-18`, `0830-1700`, `16 to 24` or an absence by key, name or unambiguous start (`si` is Sick) and pressing Enter, or leaving the cell, parses it (`src/lib/roster.ts` `parseCell`) and saves it at once through the cells route; the database keeps every version. Each cell shows its own state (saving, saved, a problem with its reason: the format, an end before the start, no such absence, the past, a conflict with another team's roster, a person off the grid), so nothing is lost silently and there is no unsaved state to guard; a cell in the past is read-only. Enter and the arrows move down and up a column, Tab across, Esc reverts. Per person the planned hours of the week, per day the headcount. **Publish** (U, with a confirmation) makes the week visible to agents as it stands; the header shows Draft or Published as version n on date by person, and "changed since the last publish" after a later edit until Publish changes. **Copy previous week** (C) fills this week from the one before. **Coverage per work type** below the grid: per work type per day the people on shift who hold it, as Nobody, One person (a single-person dependency), n of target (short) or Covered, with the names on hover. The **print view** (X, a new tab at `/roster/planner/print?week=&team=`) is the same grid without the shell on an A4 landscape sheet (the 1280px gate never applies on paper: `DesktopOnly` carries `print:` variants since the first print in prod on 8 October 2026) per the style guide's document rules (Deep Blue title with a thin divider, Background Blue table header, the logo, a footer with the date), with the version or a draft note and the note that the agents see an earlier version when the week changed; the Print button opens the browser's print dialog, whose Save as file gives the PDF. **My schedule** at `/schedule` (Time group, after My hours, `roster.view`): the person's own week from the published rosters in their own zone, one row per day with the shift, the absence, No shift or Not published yet, the note and the team, today marked, the planned total; week bar P, W, N. The **Live board** gained a Team column and filter (T) from the memberships and the shift line: today's published entry per person (`09:00–17:30`, the absence, No shift, Not published) with a flag derived in the web from the plan and the clock (`src/lib/roster.ts` `adherence`): Late (clocked in after the start plus the tolerance), Left early, Expected (the start has passed, nobody clocked in), Absent (an absence planned, on the clock anyway shows Not planned), with `roster.adherence_tolerance_minutes` from the tenant's settings. **Welcome** gained the shift sentence the greeting waited for: Your shift today: 09:00–17:30, Today: Leave, No shift planned for today, or Today's roster has not been published yet, for people holding `roster.view`. Pure logic in `src/lib/roster.ts` with `web/verify/roster.mjs` (31 checks, provoked); `web/verify/live.mjs` grew to 26 with the team filter. Mock mode mirrors the dev fixture: this week published for Team NL and Team Nordics with one cell changed afterwards, next week a draft.

**Configuration screens (since the night build of 9 October 2026, migration 0005a).** A **Configuration** group (gear icon) after Reports, shown to people holding `tenant.configure` (the admin in the default ladder; agents, supervisors and managers never see it) and checked again by the database on every read and write. Its pages carry no digit key (the admin's rail already has nine numbered pages): the group opens from the rail and with `C`, which focuses its first page so Enter opens it (the `C` keycap shows only while the group is open, Martin 9 October 2026; the key works either way and the button carries `aria-keyshortcuts`); a page's own control with the same letter wins (the planner's Copy previous week, the period bar's Custom range), a one-line precedence rule in `useKeyboardShortcuts` (controls inside `main` before the rail). Five pages, each one card with native dialogs (Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc closes, A adds, R shows or hides retired rows): **Statuses** (`/configuration/statuses`: the list with its four flags in one line each and their meaning, the default, usage, add, edit, reorder, Make default, Retire and Reactivate; the dialog freezes the flags of a status with time behind it and says why; the rules the database applies are explained before sending, from `src/lib/configuration.ts`), **Teams and skills** (`/configuration/teams`: the current teams with markets, members and order, add, edit, dissolve with a confirmation; the skills per dimension with the level scale as one comma-separated line of names, add, edit, retire, reactivate), **Absences and coverage** (`/configuration/absences`: the absence types with the paid flag; the coverage targets as a grid per team (T): one row per work type, one cell per weekday, saved when the cell is left or Enter is pressed, 0 or empty clears), **Exports** (`/configuration/exports`: the five CSV settings of 0003b as choices saved at once, each with Use default, and a preview of the hours file's header and one fictional row in the chosen format, byte order mark named; plus the two minute settings, the adherence tolerance and the forgotten clock-out grace), **App links** (`/configuration/app-links`: label, address, who sees it from the permission catalog, order; add, edit, retire, reactivate). Mock mode keeps a configuration copy of the fixture catalogs on `globalThis` with the same refusals as the database. Pure logic in `src/lib/configuration.ts` with `web/verify/configuration.mjs` (30 checks, provoked).

**Sources, Markets and Alert rules (night of 13 October 2026, addition 0008e).** Three more pages in the Configuration group (`tenant.configure`, checked by the page, the route and the database), so the intake configuration Martin set by SQL on 12 October 2026 is a screen for any tenant and any connection kind the data model knows (`crm`, `telephony`, `commerce`, `automation`); nothing on them names a vendor, pipeline, form, market, owner or outcome, and they show no customer data, only ids, counts and names from the source's own catalogs. **Sources** (`/configuration/sources`): every connection, one row each, with its vendor as stored, kind, account id, status, events in 24 hours, waiting and parked events and the last event; a row opens the connection's page (`/configuration/sources/{id}`; another tenant's or an unknown id gives a calm "does not exist" card). There: the connection (name with N, vendor, account id read-only, kind chosen from those its rows allow with Set kind, Switch off with a confirmation, the address key and the names of its signing and access secrets, read-only and never a value), the non-secret settings (S adds one; a key that names a secret is refused before sending; a record link template `record_url_<record type>` shows its live preview with the newest record id), then the sections its kind has: **Pipelines** (record type, label, Counted, Lead for deal pipelines only, each switch's effect in its header hint), **Forms** (name, Counted, lead source, market picker; F applies a rule by name pattern), **Call outcomes** (label, Connected; O applies a rule), **Fields** read-only with the hint that a change goes through the runbook, the write-back fields read-only, **Lines** (market, Counted; unticking Counted asks first, because a line that does not count keeps no calls and switching it off removes its stored calls, addition 0008f: the confirmation opens on Cancel, so Enter or Esc cancels, its confirm button is the outlined error style without a keycap, and the box stays ticked until the switch-off is confirmed and saved; ticking it again does not ask) and **Tags** (Counted) for telephony, the **Store** (A: handle, name, market, currency, time zone) for a shop; an automation connection has its settings only. A rule dialog takes the words a name contains and does not contain and what the rule sets (forms: counted, the lead source from the first matching word in the name, the market from the name's two-letter prefix read through the tenant's aliases, a name without a prefix keeping its market, and optionally the non-matching forms set to not counted; outcomes: connected or not, and optionally the opposite for the rest), shows every change row by row before anything is saved, and Save sends exactly that preview, written in one transaction (`src/lib/sources.ts` holds the plans the preview and the verifier share). **Markets** (`/configuration/markets`): the markets with zone, language, currency, language skill and order (A adds, an inactive market stays listed), their aliases as chips (I adds, × removes), the office hours per weekday as text (`9:00-17:30`, empty for closed) with the tenant's default row and a market's own week in a dialog that starts from the default ("Use the default" clears a market's own hours: a market with hours of its own uses only those), and the holidays (H). **Alert rules** (`/configuration/alert-rules`, brief B14): the rules of both triggers, each with its effect in plain words under it ("When a deal in <pipeline> gets an owner who is linked to a person on People, that person gets one alert per record and owner.", followed by the urgency and the sound), its alerts in 30 days and the last one; A adds, Edit changes record type, pipelines, urgency (the sound follows: urgent chimes and pops up), the age limit and for a new-record rule the audience, with the effect previewed in the dialog; Switch off and on; never deleted, and name and trigger fixed once the rule exists. **Linked accounts** on People: Edit person shows a Linked accounts section with one field per id the tenant's connections use (`Owner id in <CRM connection>`, `User id in <telephony connection>`), saved through `set_user_external_id` and editable by whoever may edit that person (a manager the agents, an admin everyone; nobody their own ids on screen, where the own row's Edit stays disabled as before); an id another person holds is refused with a clear message and nothing changes; the People list gains a **Linked** column (`n of total`, with a header hint). Data quality's "Owners without a person" and "Telephony users without a person" link to People for those who may link. There is no owner sync yet: ids are typed as the system shows them (Open decisions).

**System health (17 October 2026, addition 0008r; the audit's brief, 7.5).** A ninth page in the Configuration group, `/configuration/health` (`tenant.configure`, checked by the page, the documents route and the database), read-only: six cards, each read in parallel and streamed under one Suspense boundary, a Refresh button (`router.refresh()`) rather than a poll. **This application:** version, sign-in and data mode, and a timed storage round trip (one trivial read under the tenant context; fast under 200 ms). **Ingest heartbeat:** the age of the latest `link_calls` run for the tenant, fresh, late or silent against the two tenant settings `health.heartbeat_fresh_seconds` (120) and `health.heartbeat_late_seconds` (900), edited on the Exports page with the other settings. **Sources:** per connection its kind, status, last event, events in 24 hours, waiting and parked counts, the last successful read-back and the last failure per job, and a derived state (working, needs attention, failing, switched off) with a hint that nothing pings a vendor. **Jobs:** the last run per job and connection from `sync_run` in 30 days (started, duration, outcome, counts, runs in 24 hours; a run going for more than 15 minutes reads as stuck); the scheduler's forgotten-day close now writes its own run row (`close_forgotten_workdays`, `connection_id` null) through `cma.scheduler_run_start()` and `cma.scheduler_run_finish()`, so every scheduled job is on one list. **Outbox:** `cma.outbox_status()` per connection and state. **Documents:** the files `docs/system-health/documents.txt` names (the audit set, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, Plan B), copied by `cloudbuild.yaml`'s `copy-documents` step into `web/documents/`, listed with size and SHA-256 by `web/scripts/documents-manifest.mjs` at prebuild and served as downloads by `GET /api/v1/configuration/documents/{name}` (allowlist and manifest, never `public/`, never a directory listing), plus two external links from the settings `health.link_gemini_doc` and `health.link_datamap` (https only, else the card says the link is not set). The UI shows counts, ages and codes only: no personal data, no secret name, no connection key. Verifiers: `verify:health` (pure; the state derivation, the thresholds, the run grouping, the allowlist and the manifest's name and path checks; 39 checks, the provoke switch fails them), and the theme and layout verifiers cover the page and the ninth link. Out of scope for v1 (Open decisions): alert pushing (CMA-031), cross-tenant health, Cloud Monitoring figures.

**The scheduler (since the night build of 9 October 2026, migration 0005a).** A Cloud Run **job** `cma-close-forgotten-workdays` on the web image, command `node jobs/close-forgotten-workdays.mjs`, started by Cloud Scheduler `cma-close-forgotten-workdays-hourly` at every full hour (UTC) through the job's run endpoint with an OAuth token of the service account `cma-web@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, which runs the job too (its IAM database user is a member of `cma_app`, as for the service; it needs `roles/run.invoker` on the job); created or updated with `docs/scheduler/deploy.sh <dev|prod> <short sha>`, dev first. The job lists the tenants it serves with `cma.find_tenants_for_identity('scheduler', 'scheduler')`, then per tenant, in one transaction with `app.tenant_id`, `app.user_id` set to that tenant's Scheduler user and `app.actor_label = 'scheduler'`, calls `cma.close_forgotten_workdays()`: every day whose workday window ended more than the tenant's grace ago is ended by a system event at the person's published planned shift end, else at the tenant's default time (`workday.auto_close_at`), never before the day's last event and never after the window end (addition 0008b, 12 October 2026; before it, at the business day's end). The person gets a message, the holders of `workday.auto_close_notify_permission` one digest per run, and the day stays flagged and waits for a manager's correction on Team hours (the review flag and Welcome's to-do line keep showing it). The rule is in the database, the schedule is only a sweep: hourly, so a day is closed within the hour after its grace in every zone, and a second run in the same hour finds nothing; a tenant changes when its days close with the grace on the Exports page. The log holds tenant slugs, counts and dates, never a person. `pg_cron` on Cloud SQL was looked at and not chosen: the `cloudsql.enable_pg_cron` flag restarts the instance (prod runs with high availability and real time), the extension lives in one database per instance, and a job scheduled inside the database would run as a database login, so the audit would not name the tenant's scheduler user. Proof: `web/verify/scheduler.mjs` (10 checks, provoked; `PGCONN` for the forgotten day as the owner on a free date 400 days back, the convention of the API verifier, then the job as it runs on Cloud Run): the forgotten day ended by a system event at the tenant's close time (its business day's end for a tenant without settings) and flagged, today's open day untouched, a second run closes nothing, the log names nobody. The default time and the notice to the agent came with addition 0008b; the job's log line is unchanged.

**The follow-up refresh (since addition 0008s, 18 October 2026).** A second Cloud Run job on the web image, `cma-refresh-follow-up`, command `node jobs/refresh-follow-up.mjs`, started by Cloud Scheduler `cma-refresh-follow-up-minutely` every minute (UTC) the same way and with the same account and database variables as the scheduler (pool 2, statement timeout 55 s, task timeout 5 minutes, no retries: the next minute is the retry); created or updated with `docs/scheduler/deploy-follow-up.sh <dev|prod> <short sha>`, dev first. The job's script ships in the web image, so it is deployed with the image of the merge that brings it, right after that merge; `80` runs before the merge (in prod just before it), and the web on `main` runs unchanged on it, but between the migration and the job's first run nothing refreshes the snapshot (the fallback computes it once), so the gap is kept to the build's few minutes (`docs/RUNBOOK.md`, 7a). It lists the tenants with `cma.find_tenants_for_identity('scheduler', 'scheduler')` and serves each in turn as that tenant's Scheduler user, in transactions of their own: `cma.job_run_start('refresh_follow_up')`, `cma.refresh_follow_up()`, then `cma.job_run_finish()` with the rows, leads, tickets, tasks, `duration_ms` and `skipped` (1 when another refresh of the tenant held its lock), or `failed` with the error code. One tenant's failure never stops the others; the exit code is 1 when any tenant failed. One JSON log line per tenant (`severity`, `message` `refresh-follow-up`, `tenant_id`, `rows`, `duration_ms`, `refreshed`, and `reason` or `error`), never a name or a record id. Configuration → System health lists the job as Refresh follow-up. On the local volume fixture (3,000 open items) a refresh took 1.5 to 1.6 s. Proof: `web/verify/refresh-follow-up.mjs` (`npm run verify:refresh-follow-up`, 10 checks, provoked; dev only, `PGCONN` as for `verify:scheduler`): every tenant served with one line each and no `@` in the output, every snapshot computed just now with a succeeded run row matching its line, a second session holding the dev tenant's lock makes the job skip that tenant (WARNING, `running`, its row `skipped` 1) while it refreshes the others, and once that session ends the tenant is refreshed again.

**Shell and navigation (since 7 October 2026, increments c4 and c4.1; Welcome since e1).** The top bar is white, the left rail Background Blue `#EAF4FC` (the current page white, hover white at 60 %), the content area Sand `#FFFDF6`, cards white with a `#D9E2EC` border; all palette tokens, so the theme verifier stays green. The rail holds Welcome as a page of its own at the top (home icon, key 1, no permission: everyone with a role), then navigation groups. The model lives in `src/lib/nav.ts` (since 7 October 2026, d1; entries since e1): `navEntries()` lists the rail in order, a bare page or a group (id, label, icon, items), every page with the permission it needs or null; `visibleNav()` filters by permission, never by role, and numbers the visible pages across the rail; `visiblePages()` flattens them and `firstPage()` gives a person's first visible page, Welcome for everyone. **Live** (since 7 October 2026, increment b; a signal icon) holds the Live board (`/live/board`, `monitoring.live`) and sits before Time, the screen a supervisor keeps open (Martin, 7 October 2026); **Team** (since the night build of 8 October 2026; a people icon) holds People (`/team/people`, `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all`, the first page with an any-of permission, `holdsAny()`) and Roster (`/roster/planner`, `roster.manage`); **Time** (clock icon) holds My day (`/day`) and My hours (`workday.own`), My schedule (`/schedule`, `roster.view`) and Team hours (`workday.team`); **Reports** (chart icon) holds the Dashboard (`performance.team`) and, since B11 (12 October 2026), Speed to lead and Intake (`reports.view` or `performance.team`) and Data quality (`reports.view` or `tenant.configure`), numbered after the Dashboard (since addition 0008i, 14 October 2026, Inbound follows Intake with the same permissions, so each of them gains one more page; since addition 0008j Calls follows Inbound with the same permissions and Calls per agent follows Calls for `performance.team` only, so each of them gains two more and someone with `reports.view` alone one), so the supervisor gains two pages and the manager, admin and analytics three; since addition 0008o (16 October 2026) Follow-up follows Speed to lead for `reports.view`, `performance.team` or `monitoring.live`, so everyone who sees the Reports group gains one page and the keys of the pages after it move by one; since addition 0008y (19 October 2026) Attempts per deal (`reports.view` or `performance.team`) and Attempts per agent (`performance.team`, while `reports.per_person` holds `attempts`) follow Speed to lead, so analytics' and a reports reader's digit keys 4 to 9 move, accepted during the soft launch (Decision log, 19 October 2026)); **Configuration** (gear icon, since 9 October 2026, 0005a) holds the five configuration pages (`tenant.configure`), unnumbered, with the group's own `C`. An agent sees Welcome, My day, My hours and My schedule (1 to 4), unchanged by the Live and Team groups; a supervisor seven pages (Welcome, Live board, My day, My hours, My schedule, Team hours, Dashboard); a manager or admin eighteen pages, the first nine with a digit key (Welcome to Team hours; People and Roster between the Live board and My day) and the report pages without one, the admin also the Configuration group without numbers; analytics twelve pages, nine with a key. A person without `workday.own` who opens My day or My hours is sent to Welcome. A new module adds a group to `navEntries()`. Digit keys number the first nine visible pages across all groups in order (a key is one keypress, so the tenth page and later carry no key and no keycap; the IT audit of 17 October 2026) and keep working when a group is closed. `NavGroup.tsx` (client) is a button with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`. Since 19 October 2026 the rail is an accordion: at most one group is open, opening one closes the other, a click on the open group closes it (none open is allowed), and a group's letter (it also focuses the group's first page) and a click in the folded rail (it unfolds the rail) follow the same rule. `NavAccordion` (client, around the rail's list; the links stay server-rendered children) holds the open group's id and writes it to the one cookie `cma-nav-open` (the id, or empty for none; path `/`, one year, `samesite=lax`, `secure`), read on the server by `Shell` through `cookies()`, so the page renders with that group open and never flickers. The per-group cookies `cma-nav-<id>` of 7 October 2026 are ignored, and expired (`max-age=0`) on the first toggle (Decision log, 19 October 2026). A closed group that holds the current page gets a white background. The cookie prefix lives in `src/lib/nav.ts`, because a constant exported from a `"use client"` module becomes a client reference on the server. `Shell` is therefore an `async` server component. Copy keys `nav.time` (`Time`, `Tijd`), `nav.reports` (`Reports`, `Rapportages`) and `nav.dashboard`. The Workspace has no separate footer; the bottom of the rail (shortcut hint, version) plays that role. The rail is 256 px (`--spacing-rail`, since 9 October 2026, PR #55: at 216 px Configuration with its keycap overflowed and flexbox squeezed the icons to dots); icons, chevrons and keycaps never shrink and a label too long truncates. **My account** (`/account`, since 9 October 2026, addition 0005b) opens from the person's name in the top bar, for everyone with access, and is never a rail item: Your details (name, email, employer, role, time zone), Teams and skills (teams as chips, skills per dimension with level), Your time (whether the clock runs for them, the forgotten clock-out rule as the scheduler applies it, links to My day and My hours), all read only; since 16 October 2026 (backlog CMA-019) also What your role allows: the person's permission keys in plain words from the copy map `allow.<key>` (English and Dutch), an unknown key shown as the key, the narrowed set while previewing a role. **The folding rail, breadcrumbs, hidden hints and the preview select (16 October 2026, N16-A).** The rail folds to icons: the cookie `cma-rail` (`expanded`, the default, or `collapsed`; path `/`, one year, no personal data, as the group cookies) is read by `Shell`, which sets `data-rail` on the frame; `globals.css` gives the frame's first column `--spacing-rail` (256 px) or `--spacing-rail-collapsed` (64 px) and hides labels, keycaps, chevrons, a group's pages and the foot's text under `data-rail="collapsed"`, so a page renders folded without a flicker and a click changes it at once (`ShellPrefs.tsx`, client). Folded, each bare page and each group shows its icon in a 40 px pill, named by `title` and `aria-label` (the label with its key, "Live board (3)"); the current page keeps its white pill, a folded group that holds it too; a group's button unfolds the rail and opens the group, writing both cookies. The toggle at the rail's foot (chevron left "Collapse navigation", chevron right "Expand navigation", `aria-expanded`) takes W, free in the shell; a page's own W (This week on the roster and My schedule) wins over it by the hook's rule, so there the toggle is a click away. The digit keys, C and L work unchanged in both states: the hook reads `data-shortcut`, the keycaps are only hidden. Above the content a breadcrumb trail (`nav` named "You are here", an `ol` in caption size Footnote Grey, 16 px chevrons, the last crumb Inkt semi-bold with `aria-current="page"` and never a link) is derived by `breadcrumbs()` in `src/lib/nav.ts` from the active page and the model: Welcome › the group's label (a link to the group's first page the person may see) › the page; a bare page is Welcome › Messages; My account and Send a message have their own trails; Welcome shows none; a page's own trail is appended through `Shell`'s `trail` prop (the connection's name on a Sources page). The keyboard hints hide with the cookie `cma-hints` (`shown`, the default, or `hidden`): `data-hints` on the frame hides every `kbd` and the rail's shortcuts line; since 19 October 2026 nothing in the top bar or the rail moves on the toggle: Log out keeps the width it has with its keycap (the label with the keycap stays in the layout, invisible, and the bare label shows centred in the same grid cell) and the shortcuts line keeps its space, while controls on a page may narrow (Decision log, 19 October 2026); the keyboard icon left of the inbox (`aria-pressed`, "Hide keyboard hints (?)" or "Show keyboard hints (?)") and the key `?` toggle it; the rail's links and the top bar's controls carry `aria-keyshortcuts`, so hidden keycaps lose nothing for assistive tech (backlog CMA-004; hiding them by default after a person's first week needs a per-person preference, Open decisions). For an admin (`Principal.canPreview`) a labelled select "Preview as" sits between the hints toggle and the signed-in name, with "Your own view (<role>)" and the tenant's other roles; a change posts to `/api/v1/me/preview` and refreshes; while previewing an info Notice spans the content area above the breadcrumbs ("Previewing as <Role>: you see what this role sees. Your own data and permissions are unchanged.") with Exit preview (Users and roles, Preview as role). The layout verifier covers both rail states, the accordion with its cookie, the retired cookies and the letter (19 October 2026), the hints in both states, `aria-keyshortcuts`, the breadcrumbs on eleven pages (Attempts per deal and Attempts per agent among them since 19 October 2026), the preview for the admin and the ignored cookie of a manager and an agent, and the roster's week bar; the theme verifier the folded rail and the previewing admin's Welcome and My account; the hints verifier the top bar's and the rail's boxes within 0.5 px on the toggle, both ways, at 1440 by 900 and 1280 by 800 with the rail open and folded.

**Dashboard (since 7 October 2026, increment d1).** A Report screen at `/reports/dashboard`, the first page of the Reports group, shown to people holding `performance.team` (supervisor, manager and analytics in the default ladder) and checked again by the database on every read. The period bar is shared with My hours and Team hours (T, W, M, C, up to 92 days); the person filter (P) lists the people with time in the period, so it needs no team list. One read per page; everything else is computed in `src/lib/dashboard.ts`.
