Tiles: Worked and Paid (whole minutes per person per day, added up exactly as Team hours does, so both screens show the same figure), Productive as a share of worked time, and People with the number of person-days. A legend gives the time per group; Time per status shows one bar per status in the tenant's order with its time and share of clocked time (an inactive status is marked); Hours per day stacks the groups per day up to today (shown for periods longer than a day); Per person shows one stacked bar per person by name with worked and paid, no ranking until the employee-monitoring decision. A note says how many days in the period were not clocked out. Charts are plain SVG and CSS, no chart library: gridlines Border Grey, axis labels 12 px Footnote Grey, every mark with its label or a title. My day shows the status colour as a dot on the badge and the buttons. Mock mode has an `analyst` identity (analytics permissions) and meetings and training in the fixture days, so every group has something to show. **Intake row (since 12 October 2026, B11).** Between the period bar and the person filter, for the selected period and the whole tenant (the person filter does not apply to it): Leads (counted form submissions, `intake_per_day`), Deals created and Tickets created (`crm_records_per_day`, counted pipelines), Speed to lead (the median business minutes to the first outbound call over the period's lead deals, % within target and open not called, computed from `speed_to_lead_rows` exactly as `speed_to_lead_summary` computes a group: `percentile_cont`, rounded to one decimal, shares over the deals whose outcome is known), Calls (in and out) and First orders (with new customers, repeat orders and renewals). Calls show "No telephony connected" and First orders "No shop connected" when the tenant has no such source, never 0; a period that starts before `intake.start_date` says so under the row. Each tile links to its report. The time tiles and charts below are unchanged. **Header hints (since 12 October 2026).** Every column header of Speed to lead, Intake and Data quality and every Dashboard tile (the Intake row and the time tiles) explains itself in a hint: an info mark after the label (`src/components/HeaderHint.tsx`, its own tab stop) opens a short text on hover, keyboard focus or a tap and closes on Esc, with the texts in `src/lib/copy.ts` (`hints`, both languages), the speed-to-lead target filled in from the tenant's settings (`speed_to_lead.target_minutes`, carried by the rows) and the office hours named as the market's own (tenant configuration per market), so neither value is written into the copy.

The verifier clocks the test agent in through the start route and leaves that day open; on its next run it closes every open past day of the test agent itself through the corrections route, one minute after the day's last effective event (since 7 October 2026, c3.1: an end one minute after the start was refused once the status checks had changed the status later that day), and adds one day per run on the first free date more than 400 days back. Fixture 08, which did this by hand in Cloud SQL Studio, was retired on 6 October 2026.

---

## Source of truth

Every data type has exactly one system that owns it. Postgres mirrors and combines; it does not overrule the owner. The rule of thumb since 6 October 2026: **customer facts are owned by the CRM, Postgres holds a mirror of the fields the Workspace needs; work facts live only in Postgres.** A result code and a call engagement go to the CRM because they are about the customer; a lease, a status interval, a roster shift, a message delivery or a productivity figure never does. The owners below are Pulse4all's; another customer fills in its own systems, the rule stays the same.

| Data | Owner (source of truth) |
|------|-------------------------|
| Contacts, companies, leads, deals, owner assignment | CRM (HubSpot); mirrored in Postgres for the fields the Workspace serves. A contact is held (its thin record: country with the slot it came from, language, currency, store, refs) while a deal or ticket, and since 0008m an email or an inbox thread, of the connection points to it; the CRM still owns it, and its privacy deletion forgets it |
| Call facts: attempts placed, durations, talk time, recordings | Telephony (Aircall), through webhooks into Postgres, for a connection's counted lines only (addition 0008f); client-side events are a live hint, never the record |
| Work queue: campaigns, tasks, leases, attempts, dispositions (result codes), callbacks, counters, ceilings | CMA (Postgres); the CRM receives each outcome as an engagement with the result code in a custom property, and optionally a callback as a CRM task (tenant setting) |
| Result-code catalog, scoped by business line and call type | CMA (tenant configuration) |
| Support tickets | CRM (HubSpot) |
| Form submissions | CRM (HubSpot), polled into Postgres, contact id only |
| Call engagement on the contact | CRM (HubSpot, logged by the telephony integration); the CMA reads it for the contact link |
| Email engagements (sent from the CRM, logged by BCC, replies to logged emails) | CRM (HubSpot); their metadata is mirrored into Postgres by polling (addition 0008m; the email poll of brief B17b, every minute): direction, status, time, actor kind, the agent's owner id, category, contact id; never subject, body, addresses or names |
| Inbox conversations (threads, their messages, assignments and closes) | CRM (HubSpot's conversations inbox); their metadata is mirrored into Postgres by polling (addition 0008m, B17b), kept apart from the email engagements; no content, and no actor id of anyone who is not an agent |
| Customer contact details (name, address) | To decide: Shopify or customer portal |
| Invoices, payment status, billing address | NetSuite |
| Orders (id, time, amounts, first, repeat or renewal) | Shopify; read directly per store since brief B10 (ids, times, amounts, kind, the keyed hash of the phone, never the email); HubSpot holds the copy the HubSpot–Shopify app writes (the Shopify contact fields, a Self Serve deal per new order), not the CMA (Decision log, 13 October 2026) |
| A Shopify customer's CRM contact | The CMA (addition 0008h): the contact id the ingest service matched the customer to, kept in `cma.commerce_customer`, never written to HubSpot; HubSpot stays the source of the contact itself |
| Complaints, vulnerability, SLAs | CRM (HubSpot ticket category, contact field) |
| Call transcripts, sentiment, playbook results | Telephony AI (Aircall AI) |
| Customer App interactions: self-service orders and changes, service requests, in-app messages | Customer App, synced as activity events and tickets into the CRM where they need follow-up |
| Devices, installations, service status, battery and pad expiry, replacements | Asset Management Database (system and access to confirm) |
| Payments, days to pay, dunning stages | NetSuite |
| Subscriptions, terms, cancellations | Shopify and Juo, synced into HubSpot |
| Reviews | To decide: review platform |
| Agents, roles, teams, skills, rosters, statuses, time entries, lead assignments, quality scorecards, messages, change history | CMA (Postgres) |

**What is written back to the CRM:** field diffs the agent made on a served record, the engagement with the result code, do-not-call, owner assignment, through the outbox (0007c, its worker `ingest-outbox` since brief B10), and optionally callbacks as CRM tasks. Nothing for Shopify: the HubSpot–Shopify app writes the Shopify contact fields and creates a Self Serve deal per new order, and the CMA keeps each customer's matched contact in its own database (addition 0008h; Decision log, 13 October 2026; one writer per property). Everything else stays in Postgres by default. Write-back goes through the outbox; the mirror is updated from the CRM's answer.

---

## Users and roles

| Role | Example | Sees and does |
|------|---------|---------------|
| Agent | Call center agents (mostly in Barcelona) | Own day: clock, status, roster, next task from the queue, messages, own hours and scores |
| Supervisor | Team lead | Their own group of agents, live status and performance, corrections for their team (the whole tenant until teams exist; never their own day) |
| Call center manager | Arno | Full operational environment: rosters, skill coverage, live team view, productivity, messaging, hours and export (`workday.export`, only this role in the default ladder), team management |
| Admin | Builders (Martin, Joshua), later a business line's own administrator | Everything the manager has, plus configuration (statuses, teams, skills, campaigns, result codes), connections and API keys, and user management for all roles (`users.manage_all`); `tenant.configure` left the manager row. Decided 7 October 2026 (Martin) from the demo's Admin versus Management split; a person holds one role, so `admin` is a superset of `manager`. Landed with migration 0004 in the night build of 8 October 2026, where the People screen first assigns roles; Martin and Joshua become `admin` with one Studio statement per person in the morning runbook (a migration cannot know who they are), Arno `manager` |
| Scheduler (system) | the scheduler's jobs | One system user per tenant since migration 0005a (`app_user.kind = 'system'`, login system `scheduler`), holding the non-assignable system role `scheduler` with the one permission `workday.close_forgotten`, and since addition 0008s also `follow_up.refresh`; never on People, never in the ladder a person can get, maintained by migrations only. It ends forgotten workdays at the end of their business day plus the grace, and refreshes the tenant's follow-up snapshot every minute, as the audit's named actor |
| Analytics | Kira, Peter, Bas | Dashboards, KPIs and reporting; in the Workspace Welcome, where they land like everyone, and the Dashboard (`performance.team`); Welcome grows into their one view of all KPIs and metrics (Martin, 7 October 2026; Roadmap step 9). Kira (Head of Call Center) also uses live and quality monitoring, see KPIs section. No clock (`workday.own`), no corrections and no file downloads in the default ladder: hours reach them through reporting (`cma_read`, BigQuery) |

Roles and permissions are tenant-aware: every business line gets the same ladder with its own people. Reporting across business lines (Kira over Subs and Invest, finance over all three) is a customer-level reader role on `cma_read` and BigQuery, not a tenant role; how the Workspace shows a cross-tenant Report surface to such a person is an open decision (tenant picker with an "all business lines" view on reporting views only). Roles are rows per tenant, seeded from one default ladder and adjustable; the permissions they bundle come from a catalog defined by the application code. A grant can be scoped (a supervisor for one team, a manager for one market). Users are not only Pulse4all's own employees: each user belongs to an organisation (the company itself, the call center partner, …), so hours and results can be reported per employer. The same person in two business lines is two users. A manager may edit agents and the other non-managing roles; an admin may edit anyone (`users.manage_agents` versus `users.manage_all`, enforced in the database since migration 0004). `app_user` gains a worker kind (person, AI agent) when the first AI agent works a queue; everything that attributes work and time to a user applies to both. A pulse4all.com account that reaches "no access yet" is added by a manager or admin on People (the demo's approval queue stays an open idea; access requests are not stored), or invited by email (since addition 0008l, 15 October 2026): an invitation carries one of the tenant's assignable roles (role rows, never a name in code; the system role `scheduler` never), within the inviter's ceiling, the same rule as assigning the role (`users.manage_agents` gives the non-managing roles, `users.manage_all` every one), checked again when the invitation is accepted. `app_user.kind` (`person`, `system`) and `app_role.is_assignable` exist since 0005a for the scheduler's system user; the worker kind for AI agents joins `kind` later.

**Role and surface (17 October 2026).** Which of the three surfaces of the Workspace a person gets, the Dashboard, the Cockpit or the Desk (What we are building), follows from their permissions, never from the role key: read permissions alone (`reports.view`, `performance.team`, `monitoring.live`) give the Dashboard, the figures and signals without handles; a write permission next to them (`roster.manage`, `workday.team`, `users.manage_agents`, `messages.send`, `leads.manage`, `tenant.configure`) turns the same screens into the Cockpit, each handle shown to the person who holds its permission and checked by the database like every write; `workday.own` with the agent's permissions (`leads.accept`, later the queue's) gives the Desk. In the default ladder analytics is a dashboard, the supervisor and the manager a cockpit, the agent a desk; a tenant moves a role from one surface to another by a grant, as with every permission here, and Preview as role below shows an admin what each one sees.

**Preview as role (16 October 2026, N16-A).** An admin, a person whose real permissions hold `users.manage_all`, can look at the Workspace as any of the tenant's assignable roles from the select in the top bar: the Welcome blocks, the rail, the pages and the JSON API follow the intersection of the admin's real permissions and the role's (`src/lib/auth/preview.ts`, applied in `getAccess()` after `findPrincipal` for a granted access), a banner says so and Exit preview ends it; the cookie `cma-preview-role` (httpOnly, secure, samesite=lax, path `/`, 12 hours: a preview must not outlive the day) is set and cleared by `POST /api/v1/me/preview` and cleared by Log out. A preview narrows, never widens: an unknown role, an ill-formed key or a non-admin's cookie is ignored, and the database runs every call with the real permissions (`cma.current_user_id()` is the real user), so nothing can be granted through a preview and nothing the database does changes; a previewing admin gets the role's screens and the app's 403 on the routes the role may not call. The data shown is always the person's own (own day, own hours): the preview changes visibility, not identity, and the audit's actor stays the real user. It is not impersonation; acting as someone else (backlog CMA-017) stays a separate, later question. Checked by `web/verify/preview.mjs` (pure: the intersection as a property over random sets, unknown role and non-admin ignored, the empty intersection allowed and still landing on Welcome, `canPreview` for the admin with or without a preview, the cookie's name and 12 hours), the layout and theme verifiers (the banner and the select for the admin, nothing for a manager or an agent) and `verify/api.mjs --preview`.

**Per-person reports (18 October 2026, night run N19-A).** A report's team figures are for `reports.view` or `performance.team`; its figures per person need `performance.team` and the report's key in the tenant setting `reports.per_person` (comma-separated keys from the fixed list `email`, `orders` and, since addition 0008y, `attempts`; empty by default, so a new tenant shows none). While a key is off, the per-person page (Email per agent, Orders per person, Attempts per agent), its route (404) and its rail item are absent, and the database refuses the read (`CMA06`). Pulse4all's value is `email` for Pulse4all Subscriptions (Yordi agreed, Decision log, 18 October 2026; seed `02`) and empty for Pulse4all Invest; Orders per person and Attempts per agent stay off until Yordi confirms them. The dev seed `03` holds `email,orders,attempts` once `92` has run (`email,orders` before it). Calls per agent (14 October 2026) and the per-person tables of Follow-up and of the callbacks kept are not under the setting: `performance.team` alone, as decided for each.

**Teams and skills (decided 6 October 2026).** Three dimensions, all tenant configuration:

| Dimension | What it is | Shape | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Teams | Membership, not proficiency. Pulse4all: Team EN, Team NL, Team DE, Team FR, Team Nordics | `team` with one or more markets and validity; `team_member` with validity; an agent can be in several teams | Queue hard filter, Live board and roster grouping, message targeting, scope of a supervisor or manager grant |
| 1 Languages | Proficiency with a four-step scale: Basic, Good, Fluent, Native | `skill` with dimension `language`; `user_skill` with level and validity; the scale is tenant configuration | Queue filter with a minimum level per task, message targeting, coverage |
| 2 Work types | Sales, Operations, Debt, and whatever a tenant adds | `skill` with dimension `work_type`; binary for Pulse4all, levels allowed by configuration | Queue hard filter, roster coverage, productivity per work type |

A third skill dimension, channel (phone, email, chat), stays in the model as a dimension a tenant may leave empty. Nordics shows why team and language are separate: one team, several markets, and per agent Swedish Native, Danish Good. A task declares team, required language with minimum level and work type; eligibility is derived at serve time, no precomputed table (the AgentUI trial measured 2.6 ms for 5,000 queued tasks).

---

## Features

### 1. Workday and time tracking
- The workday starts with an explicit Clock in on the Welcome page (decided 7 October 2026, increment e1; until then logging in was the clock-in): a visit opens nothing, the Clock in button opens today's day with the tenant's default status, and My day then shows the clock, "Started at 09:12", one Clock out button and under it a grid of status buttons with a timer since the last change (taken from the demo, 6 October 2026). The database creates a day only for a person whose time is kept (`workday.own`), whatever path asks.
- Agents switch status during the day within the general clock-in and clock-out: available, break, lunch, training, meeting, and work on other projects or business lines (configurable list, each status flagged as working, productive, paid, billable). Non-working statuses stop the clock. A status can carry an activity code (Organisation module), so time on another project is reportable and billable separately from V1 without a new table. A non-working status chosen during a call takes effect at wrap-up (pause after this call).
- A status change also sets the agent's availability in the telephony tool through the adapter, with the mapping per status as tenant configuration (Lunch → Out for lunch, Available → Available, a work status → Back office or Available depending on whether calls should still ring). One action instead of two, and the phone stops ringing during a pause. Reading availability is confirmed in Aircall's API; whether it can be set per user, and on which plan, is verified before this is marked done. If it cannot, the Live board shows the mismatch (CMA says Lunch, phone says Available).
- A running status chip in the header on every screen; the browser title carries the unread count.
- A working day runs within the tenant's workday window (addition 0008b, 12 October 2026; Pulse4all 07:00 to 22:00 in the day's zone): a clock-in before the window start is refused with the time the day starts; status changes and clock-outs after the window end are accepted, and time after it is not counted.
- If an agent forgets to clock out, the day stays open, is flagged and capped at the window end. Since addition 0008b the hourly sweep ends it once the tenant's grace after the window end has passed (`workday.auto_close_grace_minutes`, on the Exports page; Pulse4all 60 minutes): at the person's published planned shift end for that date, else at the tenant's default time (`workday.auto_close_at`, Pulse4all 17:00), never before the day's last event and never after the window end. It is a system event under the tenant's Scheduler user. The agent gets a message that the day was closed and when; the holders of `workday.team` get one digest per run without names. The day stays flagged until a manager corrects or confirms it with a correction. A rule that uses the last call once Sync runs stays an option. Whether the agent may propose the end time from a dialog, stored as a correction awaiting approval, is an open decision.
- Full history of every clock-in, clock-out and status change, append-only; corrections are new rows with reason and approver. Time data is the heart of the system and must be reliable. A corrected or afterwards-entered end time is visible as such to the agent and the manager.
- The shift belongs to the business day it started on.
- Keyboard: digits switch pages, S moves into the status buttons, arrows and Enter choose a status, so a tenant can have any number of statuses without clashing shortcuts (6 October 2026). Since the IT audit of 17 October 2026 the current status is pressed, not disabled; a change disables only the chosen button and gives it the focus back when the change has landed, and a status live region names the new status; `app/error.tsx` and `app/not-found.tsx` render calm cards in both languages (the person's language on the 404 when known, the tenant default on the error boundary).
- The CMA stays open all day (second screen) and lands on Welcome: the agent's day line (date; today's shift from the roster later), updates and messages (Messaging), the Clock in or the day's state, the buttons to the other applications and, once the desk exists, the next task.

### 2. Rostering (manager)
*First version built in the night build of 8 October 2026 (migration 0005, the planner, the print view, My schedule, the shift line and sentence); see API, Roster planner. Still open: coverage targets on a configuration screen, shift templates, swaps and requests, forecasting.*
- Arno publishes rosters per team and market.
- Planner: agents by seven days, one structured shift per cell (start and end, or an absence type such as Off or Sick), quick typing (`9-17:30` normalises to `09:00–17:30`), Copy previous week, a dirty-state guard; agents see only their own row and their shift on My day and on the Live board (taken from the demo).
- Roster shows **skill coverage** per team per day: for example France on Tuesday, is sales covered, courtesy calls, outstanding payments, exchanges?
- One agent can hold many skills. An omniskilled agent covers multiple slots alone.
- Coverage shows not only *covered* but *how deeply covered*, so single-person dependencies are visible.

### 3. Teams and skills
*Built in the night build of 8 October 2026 (migration 0004 and the People screen); the catalogs are seed data until the configuration screens.*
- See Users and roles: teams (membership, markets), languages with a four-step level scale, work types, and channel as a dimension a tenant may leave empty. All lists and scales are configurable per tenant.
- Skills and teams drive rostering coverage, lead assignment, the work queue's eligibility and message targeting.
- Team shows each person's teams and skills; the Skills dialog sets a level per language and a yes or no per work type (from the demo).

### 4. Speed to lead

The first increment is measurement and alerts (intake track, Roadmap step 6); assignment and accept follow with migration 0009.

Since the night of 16 October 2026 (addition 0008o) the open leads not called are also a live list: Reports → Follow-up and the person's own Waiting for you show them with their age in business days (the groups from `leads.age_buckets_business_days`), whether the target has passed and the last call (Feature 5).

1. A lead comes in (through the sync, from the CRM or an ingest call).
2. The system finds agents who are **available** and have the **matching skills** in the right team.
3. The lead or deal is assigned to that agent as owner in the CRM, through the tenant's CRM adapter (HubSpot for Pulse4all).
4. The agent gets an immediate push message in the CMA.
5. The agent **must click to accept** the lead.
6. If not accepted within **5 minutes** (configurable per tenant), the lead moves to the next available agent.

Since 11 October 2026 (addition 0008a) step 4 exists for the CRM's own assignment: a deal whose owner in the CRM maps to a person raises an alert for that person (an assignment rule, urgent if the tenant says so), once per record and owner, with a sound and a desktop notification while the tab is in the background; the Acknowledge of an urgent alert is the first form of "seen". Every step is logged as an append-only event with timestamps: offered, delivered to the agent's screen, seen, accepted or declined, expired, reassigned. Speed-to-lead reporting and the accept rule both run on this log. Once the agent desk exists, a new lead is also a task at the top of the queue, so the accepting agent's next served task is the lead.

**The first speed-to-lead report is live (12 October 2026, B11)**, on migration 0007d and the HubSpot deals and calls that reach prod: **Reports → Speed to lead** (`/reports/speed-to-lead`, `reports.view` or `performance.team`) has the shared period bar, a grouping switch (day, market, agent, pipeline; G cycles), the summary table (deals, called, median business minutes, % within target, % before the next noon, open not called, with a total row of counts only; the 80th percentile left the table on 15 October 2026, Martin: the API still returns it, Decision log), then the open deals not called, longest waiting first: the deal's id, created, market, pipeline, the business minutes waited ("No business hours" for a deal whose market is unknown) and a link icon that opens the record in a new window from the connection's record link template. An agent whose owner is not mapped shows as Not mapped, never as an owner id; no customer name, email or phone number appears. The Dashboard's Intake row carries the period's speed-to-lead tile.

### 5. Reporting and steering for every management layer
What the reporting gives Kira, and Peter for Invest: the Workspace combines the CRM, telephony, Shopify and Juo, NetSuite, email, SMS, chat, the Customer App and the Asset Management Database with the staff data only the CMA has (clock, statuses, teams, skills, employer, roster). Every outcome is attributed to the agent who produced it and placed in the time interval they were on the clock. The human factor and the time factor from all channels, in one place, for every layer:

- **Supervisor:** the team today, who is on what, outcomes per agent per hour so far.
- **Manager:** productivity per agent per worked hour and per productive hour, benchmarking against the team median and the agent's own previous period, by channel, work type and campaign; sales and support work shown side by side so support-heavy agents are not misjudged; Prepare and Finish time per agent and campaign (the AgentUI trial's analysis found they consume most paid agent hours).
- **Head of call center:** Kira's KPIs (next section) with the staff dimension added: conversion per agent and team, speed to lead per agent, reasons for not buying per agent.
- **Business line owner and finance:** outcomes and cost per outcome per employer, team and work type (rates from the Billing module), approved hours, for Subs and for Invest.
- **Cohorts:** customers grouped by entry month, country, source or campaign, proposition, and the agent or team that converted them; per cohort how long they stay customer (retention curve, churn before and after the minimum term), how they pay (days to pay, late payments, dunning stages, collections outcomes), reviews, upsell and service events from the asset database. Agents form cohorts too: hired per month, ramp-up time to target productivity, retention. See KPIs section 12.

Rules: per worked or productive hour, never per calendar day; the first view is Total Calls Today per agent with talk time (from the Subs agent UI), fed by the Aircall sync; the join is designed now and filled source by source along the roadmap. What agents see of each other, and what the partner's HR sees, follows the employee-monitoring decision with Yordi.

**The first intake reports are live (12 October 2026, B11)**, ahead of B14 and the intake configuration screens, so Kira can review the lead and contact definitions against real numbers: the Dashboard's **Intake row** (`performance.team`; see API, Dashboard), **Reports → Speed to lead** (Feature 4), **Reports → Intake** (`/reports/intake`, `reports.view` or `performance.team`: per business date and market the counts of `intake_per_day` as a table with a total row and a small inline bar per row (leads, deals and tickets created against the busiest row), a market filter (F), and the telephony columns (calls in, calls out, tagged) and shop columns (new customers, first and repeat orders, renewals); where the table is wider than its card (1280 px) it scrolls sideways inside the card, never the page (since 12 October 2026)) and **Reports → Data quality** (`/reports/data-quality`, `reports.view` or `tenant.configure`: one row per check with its count and one line on what fixing it means, CRM owners without a person first, checks with nothing to fix muted; since 13 October 2026 also "Contact updates not sent" (`outbox_pending`), the outbox actions still waiting ten minutes after they fell due, shown only while there are any). Sources: speed to lead reads HubSpot deals and calls, live in prod; in Intake, calls and tagged calls come from telephony lines and customers and orders from commerce (in prod since 13 October 2026 for Aircall and 14 October 2026 for the 14 Shopify stores, NL first), and a tenant without one sees "No telephony connected" or "No shop connected" in those places, never 0 (how the state is decided: API, Intake report routes). Counts start at `intake.start_date`; a period that starts earlier says so. Numbers follow the viewer's language; dates are business dates in the tenant's zone. Mock mode has a fixture with invented ids: every status, NL, GB, DE and deals of an unknown market (no business hours, empty measures), one agent in five not mapped, every data-quality check non-zero, and a second tenant without telephony or a shop (the `invest-analyst` identity), so both states can be seen. Pure logic in `src/lib/reports.ts` with `web/verify/reports.mjs`.

**Reports → Inbound is live (14 October 2026, brief B15, addition 0008i; in prod since 14:51 UTC that day, `cma-web` `516b9e5`).** `/reports/inbound`, for `reports.view` or `performance.team` like Intake: the inbound calls on counted lines per day, market, line or hour (G cycles; each call's day and hour in its line's market zone), with offered, answered, unanswered as a share of the calls with an outcome, the service level (answered within `telephony.service_level_seconds`, 20 for Pulse4all, over the answered calls and the unanswered calls whose reason counts), the average and 80th percentile time to answer (the menu and ringing included, as Aircall), and one column per missed reason that occurs, labelled from the tenant's catalog (Configuration → Sources, the telephony connection), with a total row the database computes over every call and a small stacked bar per row (answered in Leaf Green; the reasons in the style's series order without Leaf Green, which reads as Available on the Live board, then the neutral grey, with Denim last because it is hard to tell from Deep Blue in the small bars (B15.1): Deep Blue, Light Blue, Soft Rose, Inkt, Neutral, Denim, each reason's colour fixed by its code (the catalog's codes sorted by code take the series in order, a code without a catalog row follows, cycling), so a reason keeps its colour across periods, groupings and markets while the columns stay sorted by total; never a red; no text on a Light Blue or Soft Rose segment, `docs/style/Pulse4all-Style.md` 7.7 and 7.8). Every header explains itself in a hint, the seconds filled in from the settings; the table scrolls inside its card at 1280 px; a tenant without telephony sees "No telephony connected", never 0. Source: Aircall's calls and their `missed_call_reason`, in prod since 13 October 2026. Mock mode has a fixture with every reason of Pulse4all's seed, a code nobody saved, a caller missed twice and then called back, an anonymous caller, a call older than the lookback, and the `invest-analyst` tenant without telephony.

**Reports → Calls and Calls per agent (14 October 2026, brief B16, backlog CMA-055 and CMA-044, addition 0008j; live in prod at `70354b1`).** Aircall's General Performance, Outbound and User Activity views in the CMA. `/reports/calls`, for `reports.view` or `performance.team` like Inbound: every call on counted lines for a period, by day, ISO week, market or line (G cycles), filtered by market (K) and line (N) next to the period bar, the active filters named in the page's date line. Four tiles: calls, inbound, outbound and average talk time, each with its change against the previous period as Muted text with an arrow (a change in volume is neither good nor bad; Decision log, 14 October 2026), like for like: a period that is still running against the same point in the previous one, the label saying which ("vs the previous period", "vs the same point in the previous period"), the dates in its title; against a previous span that starts before the tenant's telephony history (the business date of its earliest stored call, addition 0008k) no change at all but "No history to compare yet" in the same Muted text, the history's first date in its title (Decision log, 14 October 2026, brief B16.1); the calls per group as stacked bars, inbound Deep Blue and outbound Leaf Green (the series order of `docs/style/Pulse4all-Style.md` 7.7), columns over days and weeks, bars for markets and lines; an outbound section with the connected calls (at least `telephony.connected_min_seconds` of talk time, 10 for Pulse4all), their share and their talk time; and a tag section following the grouping: the untagged share as a tile, the whole period's tags as horizontal bars (largest first, the top twelve and Other, a tag that does not count in Neutral and marked), and the groups by tags as a table with counts and row shares (twelve tag columns, Other and Untagged) that scrolls inside its card at 1280 px. No donut. `/reports/calls-per-agent`, for `performance.team` only and left out of the rail for everyone else: one row per person, sorted by name and never ranked (Decision log, 7 and 14 October 2026), with answered inbound calls and their average talk time, outbound calls, the connected ones with their average talk time (average talk time in and out, not the totals, since 19 October 2026: Decision log; the API keeps both totals), untagged conversations, and the first and last call's time of day, each figure with an inline bar against its column's largest, as Aircall's User Activity does (inbound Deep Blue, outbound Leaf Green, untagged Light Blue); a row opens with Enter on its name or a click to the person's conversations per counted tag (a small bar per tag with count and share, then the untagged ones), and Up and Down move between people; the calls of telephony users not linked to a person come last as one row with how many users that is, linking to Data quality for those who may open it; then the total. Talk time runs from the answer to the end, without ringing, so these figures differ from Aircall's on purpose. Every tile and header explains itself in a hint, the threshold filled in from the settings; a tenant without telephony sees "No telephony connected", never 0. The telephony connection's Sources page states the threshold in words, linked to its field on Configuration → Exports, Other settings. Data prerequisites (KPIs 11): every Aircall user linked to a person on People, and every conversation tagged. Mock mode has outbound calls connected, short and unanswered and one at exactly the threshold each day on the NL line, fifteen tags of which one does not count, conversations untagged and with only that tag, four linked telephony users and one linked to nobody, calls on the test line that does not count, the previous period, and the `reader` identity with `reports.view` alone; since brief B16.1 its calls start at the fixture's intake start date, six weeks back, as its inbound calls already did, so its telephony history starts there.

**Reports → Follow-up (16 October 2026, night run N16-B, addition 0008o; in prod since 16 October 2026, merged as `c7a3cab`).** `/reports/follow-up`, for `reports.view`, `performance.team` or `monitoring.live`: what is waiting right now, read again every 10 seconds while the tab is open, with no period: eight tiles (leads not called and past the target, tickets at risk, over their response time and without owner, tasks overdue, due today and with an unlinked owner), then the leads not called (longest waiting first: the deal's id, pipeline, market, the owner as a person, created, business minutes waited, the age group in business days from `leads.age_buckets_business_days`, past the target or within it, the last call, the link), the tickets and their response time (the least time left first, no business hours last: the ticket's id, pipeline, stage, market, owner, business minutes open, the response time in force, On time, At risk, Over time or No business hours, the time left or how far over, the link), the tasks (due first: the task's id, type with a Callback badge, priority, status, owner, due, Overdue, Today, Later or No due time, the deal or ticket by id, the link where the connection has `record_url_task` (none for HubSpot, which has no task page: Open decisions); never a task's text) and, for `performance.team` only, Per person (by name, the not-linked row last: open leads, past target, open tickets, at risk or over, overdue tasks, due today). The market (K), person (O) and state (S: everything waiting, or needs attention now) filters narrow the three lists and sit in the address; the tiles count everything. Every header and tile explains itself in a hint with the tenant's target and warning share filled in. **Waiting for you** (`/me/follow-up`, `leads.accept` or `workday.own`; Welcome › Waiting for you, no rail item) shows the person's own three lists from `cma.my_follow_up()`, and the Welcome card of the same name counts them. The Welcome block **Needs attention** (in place of Before today's shift, for `workday.team`, `monitoring.live` or `performance.team`) lists the days not clocked out (workday.team), the leads past the target, the tickets at risk or over their response time, the tasks overdue and the missed callers not called back (monitoring.live, a tenant with telephony), each a count with a link to where it is acted on (the days not clocked out open Team hours filtered to the days to correct, `/team/hours?range=month&needs=correction`, with a chip and Show all, since the IT audit of 17 October 2026); the Live board's **Follow-up now** strip under the inbound tiles shows six of the counts. Mock data, API and Postgres alike; the pure verifier `verify:follow-up` covers the filters, the counts as the database computes them, the settings' rules and the attention lines. **Since addition 0008s (18 October 2026)** the page makes one data call (`getFollowUpPage` → `cma.follow_up_page()`): the figures come from the follow-up snapshot, computed once a minute per tenant, so they are at most about a minute old, and "Updated …" under the title says how long ago (Muted once older than three minutes); the counts stay complete; the filters run in the database; each list shows its first `follow_up.list_rows` rows (100 by default, at most 500, on Configuration → Exports) with "Showing n of total" and, when the list holds more, a hint to narrow it with the filters. The 10-second refresh stays. The Live board's Follow-up now, Welcome's Needs attention and Waiting for you and the follow-up API read the snapshot too. On the local volume fixture: 112 ms server time and a 133 kB refresh instead of 2,859 ms and 5,748 kB. **Since 19 October 2026** (Decision log) the three tables, here and on Waiting for you, show created and due in the compact form (Mon 19 Oct, 14:05) with the full date on hover, end a pipeline, stage or task type in an ellipsis at 14rem with the full label on hover, and keep the link column pinned to the table's right edge in its row's colours, so the link stays in view when a table scrolls sideways inside its card. A lead without business hours (no market) shows a Muted dash as its waiting time and age, No business hours on hover, beside Unknown market and the No business hours badge; such a lead may make the leads table scroll at 1440 px with the rail open (Decision log, 19 October 2026).

**Reports → Forms (16 October 2026, brief B17d with its amendment, addition 0008p; in prod since 17 October 2026, merged as `964a347`).** `/reports/forms`, for `reports.view` or `performance.team` like Calls: every polled form's submissions for a period, counted in Intake or not, per day or ISO week (G), filtered by market (K), category (Y) and form (F) next to the period bar, the active filters named in the date line. Four tiles: the submissions and the forms with submissions, each with its change against the previous period like for like (as Calls; before the tenant's forms history, `cma.forms_history_start()`, "No history to compare yet" with the history's first date in the title), the share of submissions whose contact was found, and the submissions without a market. Then the submissions per day or week as Deep Blue columns; the table per form and market (each form on a row with its category and its total, then a row per market: submissions, the previous period's, the change in Muted text and the contacts found with their share); the totals per category (the catalog's order, forms without one last) and per market (unknown last); how the contacts were found (found, no contact, several contacts, no email address, not looked up yet) as bars in the series order with their labels beside them, no donut; and the answers to the kept choice questions per form, each option with the period's and the previous period's submissions, narrowed by question and answer. The table downloads as a file in the tenant's export format (X; one row per form and market with the resolution split). Every tile and header explains itself in a hint, in English and Dutch. The market of a submission: the form's, else its market question's answer through the aliases, else its contact's country, else unknown (Decision log, 16 October 2026). Configuration → Sources: a CRM connection's forms show polled, counted, category, lead source and market, and a form's dialog sets polled (a counted form is always polled), counted, category, lead source and market; the Sources page lists the form categories (add, rename, change the order, switch off; a switched-off category stays on the forms that have it). Kept fields, a form's market field and its lead condition are set by the runbook (`docs/b17d-forms/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`). Run in dev and prod on 17 October 2026 (`records/b17d-dev-2026-10-17`, `records/b17d-prod-2026-10-17`): in prod 50 forms polled, 28 of them counted, 478 submissions from 1 to 17 October; the four forms without a market have no market field, because they ask no country (Decision log, 17 October 2026).

**Reports → Email and Email per agent (night run N19-A, 18 to 19 October 2026, addition 0008t; merged 19 October 2026 as `997749c`).** `/reports/email`, for `reports.view` or `performance.team` like Calls: every counted email of a period per day or ISO week (G), filtered by market (K), inbox (I) and mailbox (B). Five tiles (received with the logged replies counted apart, sent by agents with automated, bounced and failed, conversations opened and closed, the first reply's median in business minutes with the share within `email.reply_target_minutes`, time to close), each volume with its change as Muted text with an arrow, like for like, and none before the email history; received per inbox and per mailbox as stacked columns (the style's series order, the rest as Other), sent by agents and automated; the tables per inbox (first reply), mailbox, category and market; Waiting now and Not responding with their links, capped by `follow_up.list_rows`. `/reports/email-per-agent`, for `performance.team` while the tenant's `reports.per_person` holds `email`: per person by name, never ranked, sent, first replies, closed, the median first reply and the share within the target, and with Enter or a click the person's sends per hour of day (Down moves to the next person); the owner ids without a person as one row. Configuration → Sources gains the CRM connection's email section: inboxes, mailboxes (label, counted, market) and channels. They replace Arno's HubSpot reports 15a and 15b, the first-reply half of 17, the library's Conversations reports and "Contacts with deals who aren't responding to emails". A tenant without email says "No email connected", never 0. Since addition 0008x (19 October 2026) each page reads its figures in one pass, filter lists included (in prod the report took 7.0 s, 14.6 s with a filter, and Email per agent 18 to 20 s; Data model: report speed).

**Reports → First orders and Orders per person (night run N19-A, addition 0008u; merged 19 October 2026 as `997749c`).** `/reports/first-orders`, for `reports.view` or `performance.team`: the new customers' first orders, by self-service or through the call center (KPI 2), per day, ISO week, market or level (G), filtered by market (K) and level (V). Four tiles (first orders with repeat, renewals, not first and without a contact counted apart; via the call center and self-service with their shares; the median days from the first touch), the change as Muted text with an arrow, like for like; call center (Deep Blue) and self-service (Leaf Green) stacked per day or week; a horizontal bar per level in the tenant's order with every level explained in a hint; the table per market with the amounts per currency; the amounts per currency, never summed across; the first orders of the period, newest first, with the deal id, market, when, level, the evidence as small badges, days from the first touch, the amount and the deal's link (no name), and the file (X) in the tenant's export format. "History from …" and "Phone matching from …" in Muted under the date line. `/reports/orders-per-person`, for `performance.team` while `reports.per_person` holds `orders` (off for Pulse4all): the first orders via the call center per credited person, by name. Configuration → Sources gains the Order pipeline column and each deal stage's outcome (won, lost, none, from the source or set here). A tenant without an order pipeline sees "No orders connected". Since addition 0008x (19 October 2026) the page reads the report, the amounts and the list in one pass, in under a second on a tenant at prod's volume (in prod 108 to 239 ms as `cma_app` on 19 October 2026, `records/perf-prod-2026-10-19`); before it each of the three reads took 20 to 27 s in prod and the page failed on the 15 s statement timeout (Data model: report speed).

**Callbacks kept (night run N19-A, addition 0008w; merged 19 October 2026 as `997749c`).** The Dashboard's Intake row gains a seventh tile, Callbacks kept (KPI 5): the share kept of the period's callbacks with "kept of decided" and the ones still open, linking to Follow-up; and Reports → Follow-up shows below its lists the callbacks kept of the last eight ISO weeks (the current week first, a week without callbacks as zeros, the total) for `reports.view` or `performance.team`, and per person for `performance.team`. A tenant that marks no task type as a callback sees "No callback type set".

**Reports → Attempts per deal and Attempts per agent (addition 0008y, 19 October 2026; to merge).** `/reports/attempts`, for `reports.view` or `performance.team`, wide: the outbound CRM calls each deal gets until it is reached (KPIs 1), for the deals created in a period, per day, ISO week, market or pipeline (G), filtered by market (K), pipeline (P) and which deals (S: the lead pipelines, the default, or every counted deal pipeline; never an order pipeline). Five tiles with the change against the previous period like for like: deals; deals attempted with their share; attempts per deal with the median and the total; reached on the first attempt as a share of those attempted; open deals never attempted. A change is Leaf Green when better and in the error colour (`#B8433C`) when worse, for the three figures with a direction (attempted share and first reach up, open never attempted down), and neutral for the others (Pulse4all-Style 7.7). Four cards of horizontal bars with their values: deals by number of attempts (0 to `attempts.max_bucket`, the top as `n+`), reach per attempt (the share of those attempted first connected on each), the won rate by number of attempts (closed deals with an outcome only) and the open deals by business days since the last attempt (one neutral hue, Light Blue; the deals of a market without business hours named apart). Then the table per group and the deals of the period: open first, the longest since the last attempt first, then closed by the newest close, capped by `follow_up.list_rows` with "Showing n of total"; each deal by id with its pipeline, market, created, outcome, attempts, connected, the first connected attempt, the last attempt, the business days since it and the link to the deal in the CRM; no name, address or phone number. A note says that a call to a contact with two open deals counts for both. Every tile, card and header explains itself in a hint (outbound CRM calls, the pre-window, the connected rule, the buckets, the top bucket). `/reports/attempts-per-agent`, for `performance.team` while the tenant's `reports.per_person` holds `attempts` (absent from the rail and not found otherwise; Pulse4all: off until Yordi agrees): per deal owner by name, never ranked, the deals they own, attempted, the attempts (by anyone) with an inline Deep Blue bar, per deal, reached on the first attempt and open never attempted; the owners without a person as one row with how many; the total. Both sit after Speed to lead in the Reports group; analytics' and a reports reader's digit keys 4 to 9 move, accepted during the soft launch (Decision log, 19 October 2026). Data prerequisites as Data model: attempts per deal names them. Since the same day the line switch-off confirmation on Configuration → Sources names the stored calls it removes and the first one's date (backlog CMA-020): its confirm button waits for the count, and a failed read says so in the error colour.

### 6. Gamification (agent)
- Daily goals, scores, streaks and comparison with yesterday or colleagues, built from the same metrics the manager uses.
- Metrics must reward the right behaviour (not just volume, not just easy conversions).
- Whether agents see colleagues' call counts is decided with Arno before Total Calls Today is shown to agents.

### 7. Messaging

The first increment is record alerts (new-deal alerts, migration 0008, polling every 10 s). Its database half was written on 10 October 2026 (see Data model: messaging core and record alerts): rules, messages with one delivery row per recipient resolved at send time, the ingest service's alert call, manual messages to everyone, teams, a language with a minimum level or named people (optionally only those clocked in), the caller's own reads, and stats. The Workspace half was built on 11 October 2026 (brief B12): the open Workspace polls `GET /api/v1/me/messages?since=` every 10 seconds while the tab is visible and every 30 seconds hidden, a toast for every new message, the unread count on the top-bar inbox and in the browser title, a desktop notification while the tab is in the background (the permission is asked from a button in the inbox, never on load), and an urgent message as a pop-up with a sound that Esc cannot close and only Acknowledge (A) ends; Messages at `/messages` is a rail page right after Welcome for everyone (key 2), with Mark all read (M) and the record link that opens in a new window; a sender (`messages.send`) composes at `/messages/send` to everyone, teams, a language at a minimum level or named people, optionally only those clocked in, and sees the sent list with recipients, read and confirmed counts and the median time to read; the Updates card on Welcome lists the latest messages. The ingest service raises the alerts: a deal or ticket that arrived by a `created` event is handed to `cma.ingest_record_alerts()` after its upsert (reconcile, backfill and the second read send `changed` events, so they never alert). No alert rule is seeded: a rule is tenant configuration, written with `cma.upsert_message_rule()` (the go-live runbook has the statement) until a configuration screen exists.

- Managers and supervisors push messages directly to agents in the open CMA; later 1:1 and team chat, announcements that must be acknowledged, and an assist request from agent to supervisor.
- Targets: everyone, a team, a language with a minimum level, a work type, or specific agents, optionally only those clocked in or Available right now (from the demo). Group targets are resolved into delivery rows at send time, so history stays exact when teams change and the read count always has a denominator.
- Every message is a row in Postgres with one delivery row per recipient (`delivered_at`, `read_at`, `acknowledged_at`). An urgent message opens a pop-up with a sound that the agent must confirm; confirming writes `acknowledged_at`, separate from reading.
- Pushing goes through an outbox: the application writes the message and the outbox row in one transaction, a relay forwards to the realtime service, acknowledgements flow back. Nothing is lost if the push service hiccups.
- Messages carry references (CRM id, case status, link), never customer data. Delivery respects quiet hours and working time; Spain has a statutory right to disconnect.
- The alert rules (new record, assignment) are configured on Configuration → Alert rules since the night of 13 October 2026 (brief B14, addition 0008e): record type, pipelines, urgency (the sound follows it), age limit and the audience of a new-record rule, each rule's effect in plain words and its alerts of the last 30 days; switched off, never deleted.
- Automations (Make, n8n, a CRM workflow) send messages through the ingest service, `POST /automation/<connection key>` with the connection's bearer secret (addition 0008a, 11 October 2026): targets by team, language, named people, CRM owner id or work email are resolved through `app_user_external_id` and the catalogs; the answer names the recipients per message.
- Later channels for reaching users (email, mobile push) are extra delivery rows behind the same message.
- Alerts on tasks and on tickets against their response time (a task assigned, a task overdue, a ticket at risk) are not built: the tasks (0008n) and the follow-up layer (0008o) land in the night of 16 October 2026 without alert rules, which are an open end (Open decisions).

### 8. Agent desk: the directive work screen (decided 6 October 2026)
The agent's working surface, Steam Connect-like, built so daily practice can show whether Newco needs it; the assumption is that it will.

- **Next task, served.** Clock in, press Next, the system serves the task that matters most for this agent: callback appointments first, then the priority matrix by call type and attempt, filtered by team, language level and work type. One screen, no list to browse, no choice of what to do next.
- **Queue models per campaign.** `owned` (agents own their records, no pool; Invest) and `shared` (team pool, callback appointments stay personal; Subs). Unassigned work on an owned campaign is refused at write time.
- **No task forgotten.** Every served task is leased; an expired lease returns to the pool. Callbacks return to the pool when the agent is absent on a shared campaign. A ticket closes automatically at its ceiling. Overdue work and unleased callbacks show on the Live board and in the queue counter (due, mine, pool, handled today, per period).
- **The record, read live.** The served record comes from the mirror and is refreshed from the CRM through the adapter; field blocks per campaign decide what is shown. Deep links open the record in the CRM and in other systems (Shopify, Bloqhouse). Edits become field diffs in the outbox.
- **One-press outcome.** A result code books in one press; a code that needs something (an objection, a memo, a deal amount, a callback moment) asks for exactly that. Booking is one transaction: attempt, memo, field diff, counters, next task or close, lease release, outbox rows, then the next task is already there. Never gated on call state.
- **Result codes** are a scoped catalog (business line, call type) with outcome, priority, ceiling, retry interval, requirement flags and side effects (do-not-call, email flow). Processors (product emails, request a quote) are actions, not codes, and still require a code afterwards.
- **Telephony embedded.** The Aircall softphone mounted in the desk, kept mounted all day; the agent's call phase (idle, dialing, ringing, on call, wrap-up) from SDK events as a live hint; call facts from webhooks. One-click dial through the telephony API (`POST /v1/users/:id/calls`) where the plan and the embedding allow it; staging the number in the dialpad otherwise. Incoming call: a screen pop with the number called and the CRM match.
- **Speed.** Keyboard for every action (1–9 and 0 for codes, letters for actions, F2 search), shortcuts suppressed while typing, prefilled memo, requirement-only dialogs, nothing in the render path waits on an integration. Search opens a record outside the queue and never steals the task.
- **Universal.** Campaigns, field blocks, button grids, result codes, queue model, ceilings and tools are tenant configuration; the Invest line's prospectus compliance is a campaign's compliance hook, not product code. Built for Subs first, Invest after.

### 9. Cockpit: the management screens (from the demo, 6 October 2026; the cockpit since 17 October 2026)
The manager's and the supervisor's surface, the Cockpit since 17 October 2026, one of the three surfaces of the Workspace beside the Dashboard and the Desk (What we are building; Users and roles, Role and surface). The screens below show the signals of the day, and the design rule is that every signal has a handle: next to a figure that asks for action sits the action, on the same screen, through a write path the CMA has or plans, audited and gated by its permission like every other write; a signal with no write path behind it is a dashboard view, and a person without the handle's permission sees the signal without the handle. The handles today: Correct a day and Add day on Team hours (`correct_workday`, `workday.team`), the planner's cells (`roster_set_entry`, `roster.manage`), the composer (`send_message`, `messages.send`), a person's teams and skills on People (`set_person_skills`, `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all`), the thresholds, alert rules and settings on Configuration (`tenant.configure`); the ones to come: an owner for a lead past the target, pushed to them (0009's offers and the owner write-back on the outbox), a missed caller handed to an agent and an idle agent served the next task (0011's queue engine), a ticket at risk reassigned (Roadmap step 9). Needs attention on Welcome and Follow-up now on the Live board already link each count to where it is acted on; the cockpit brings the action to the count. Live, Report and Data stay the groups: the cockpit is a posture, not a page, and the IT audit of 17 October 2026 checks every management screen against the rule (the manager's first week: which signals, which handles, who holds them).

- **Welcome:** the landing page for everyone with a role, blocks per permission: updates, Steam's to-do line before today's shift (days not clocked out) for supervisors and managers, the Reports block for analytics (the one view of all KPIs in step 9), the Clock in card, the app links (done 7 October 2026, increment e1, see API, Welcome and Clock in).
- **Live:** who is on which status right now, tiles per group, since, clocked in and out, worked so far, filters by group and employer (done 7 October 2026, increment b, see API, Live board); the team filter and column and today's shift with its adherence flag since the night build of 8 October 2026 (0004, 0005); the skill filter and the on-call state from telephony with Sync (step 3). Stale days are not a board matter: today's day cannot be past its end, and earlier open days sit on Welcome and Team hours. Since 14 October 2026 (brief B15, addition 0008i) the board opens with today's inbound tiles (inbound, answered, unanswered, service level, average time to answer, waiting now, longest waiting) and the missed calls not called back, one row per caller, oldest first (see API, Live board); live in prod since 14 October 2026 (`cma-web` `516b9e5`).
- **Follow-up (16 October 2026, night run N16-B, addition 0008o):** Welcome's to-do line became Needs attention (days not clocked out, leads past the target, tickets at risk or over their response time, tasks overdue, missed callers not called back; counts and links), Welcome gained Waiting for you with the person's own page `/me/follow-up`, the Live board the Follow-up now strip, Reports the Follow-up page (Feature 5), Sources the task statuses and types of a CRM connection and each ticket pipeline's own response time, Exports the Follow-up settings.
- **Campaign monitoring (night run N19-A, addition 0008v; merged 19 October 2026 as `997749c`):** a rail group Campaigns (after Reports, a flag) with one page, Campaign monitoring (`/campaigns`, `reports.view` or `performance.team`): a card per journey with its name, market, status badge, summary, how many prerequisites it lists and the design's link when there is one; a journey's page (`/campaigns/{code}`, breadcrumbs Campaigns › the journey) says in a Muted banner that the monitor is not built yet and nothing there is real data, shows the planned layout as dashed placeholders, one line each (Coverage, Steps and due rules, Workflow monitor, Needs attention, Customer drill-down locked until a dedicated permission, an access log and Yordi's answer, Life of the lead, Audit pack), then the prerequisites as a checklist, none ticked. No figures; the journeys are configuration (Data model: journeys).
- **Dashboard:** time per status, hours per day, per agent, for a period (done 7 October 2026, increment d1, see API, Dashboard); grows into the per-layer reports of Features 5 as sources connect.
- **Hours and export:** clock-in and clock-out per agent per day for payroll, pauses, note chips, two CSV exports (hours per day, status changes) with separator, decimal mark and date format as tenant settings; Correct a day and Add day on `correct_workday` with a required reason (database, API and the Team hours screen done 6 October 2026, increments c1 and c2; the two CSVs done 7 October 2026, increment c3; see API; the Pauses column on My hours since 11 October 2026).
- **Team:** roles, employer, teams and skills, disable and enable, add a person (done in the night build of 8 October 2026, see API, People); access requests are out, the manager adds people. Linked accounts since the night of 13 October 2026: each person's owner id in the CRM and user id in telephony, typed by whoever may edit that person, with a Linked column on the list (addition 0008e; see API, Sources, Markets and Alert rules). Invitations by email since the night of 15 October 2026 (addition 0008l; see API, People): the manager (or admin) invites someone by work email with a role within their own ceiling, employer, teams and skills, sends the single-use link from their own mail, and the person sets themselves up by accepting it; access requests stay out, nobody asks the CMA for access.
- **Roster:** the planner with quick typing, publish, copy week and coverage, the print view, My schedule (done in the night build of 8 October 2026, see API, Roster planner).
- **Configuration (admin):** status catalog with flags, teams, skill catalog and level scales, campaigns and result codes, connections and API keys for Make and n8n (create a connection, see the instructions once, rotate by creating a new one). Since the night of 13 October 2026 (addition 0008e): **Sources** (per connection its name, kind, status, secret names read-only, non-secret settings with a live record link preview, and the pipelines, forms, call outcomes and fields, lines and tags, or store its kind has, with rules by name pattern previewed before saving), **Markets** (aliases, office hours, holidays) and **Alert rules** (both triggers with their effect in plain words, switch off, never delete). A connection is still created by the runbook, because it names its secrets.

## Target scope beyond V1

The CMA covers everything needed to run the contact center besides the telephony itself (IVR, routing, recording stay in Aircall), the customer channels and the CRM. V1 builds the first slices (Roadmap steps 2 and 5 to 8); the foundation is designed so the rest can be added without reworking what exists.

| Module | Covers | Main users |
|---|---|---|
| Organisation | tenant settings, markets, office hours and holidays per market, sites and time zones, teams and hierarchy, employers, activity codes, skill and channel lists, roles, integrations and adapters (CRM, telephony), API keys, audit, retention | manager, admin |
| People and staffing | identity, employment history (employer, contract type, hours, start and end), skills with proficiency and validity, availability and preferences, onboarding and offboarding | manager, HR at the partner |
| Time and attendance | clock, statuses, breaks, adherence (planned vs actual), corrections with approval, timesheet approval and locked periods, leave and balances, overtime and rest rules | agent, supervisor, manager |
| Rostering | shift templates, roster versions and publication, coverage requirements per market, work type and channel per interval, swaps and requests, forecasting from historical volume, intraday reforecast | manager, supervisor, agent |
| Agent desk | the directive work screen: campaigns, queue models, next task, leases, result codes, callbacks, memo, embedded telephony, call context from the mirror, outbox write-back, scripts and templates per campaign, compliance hooks | agent, supervisor |
| Routing | the queue engine, assignment rules per tenant, market and channel, queues, accept and expiry events, fallback and escalation, full trail | system, agent, manager |
| Performance and quality | metric catalog with definitions, targets per role, team and period, scorecards as versioned forms, calibration, disputes, coaching and action plans, gamification | supervisor, manager, analytics, agent |
| Communication | push, broadcasts, must-acknowledge announcements, chat, assist requests, preferences and quiet hours, mobile push | everyone |
| Billing and cost | rates per employer, role and work type with validity, billable and paid flags, approved hours per period, outcome counts, exports; invoicing itself stays in NetSuite | manager, finance |
| Reporting | live views and wallboards, agent dashboards, reports for every management layer from the combined sources plus staff, agent benchmarking per worked hour, cost per outcome, cohort analysis (retention, payment behaviour, outcomes per entry cohort, agent cohorts), weekly and trend reports, as-of reporting (who was in which team, on which contract, on a given date) | all roles |
| Compliance | staff data under GDPR, retention per data type, employee monitoring rules in NL and Spain, right to disconnect, who-saw-what for sensitive alerts and for served records | Yordi, manager |
