## Open decisions

- `npm audit` reports 5 high-severity advisories, one chain under `eslint-config-next` (a devDependency; `npm ls braces --omit=dev` is empty, so nothing of it ships in the image); no fix exists and `npm audit fix --force` is never run; reviewed again with every Next.js update. The heads-up to Yordi was drafted on 7 October 2026 (four questions: Spain's daily registration and retention, Wet DBA, employee monitoring including the Live board and later benchmarking, retention of staff data and the change log and what Newco may see); whether it was sent is to be confirmed by Martin.
- Timing of the heads-up to Yordi and of prod high availability: both were set before real data lands, and since 5 October prod holds the team's own working time. High availability on a dedicated core is in place since 7 October 2026 (Decision log); the heads-up to Yordi is the open part: send it now, before the first Newco agent. A second heads-up, plus the processor view and who-saw-what audit, before the mirror (step 3) and the desk (step 8).
- Tenant picker: needed before anyone works for two tenants (in dev `agent-one` shows the refusal); a screen after login, or a default tenant per person; plus the "all business lines" reporting view for readers across business lines in the Workspace.
- Pulse4all US as the third tenant: region and residency (EU-only folder policy versus a US region, which would mean its own database and project), currency, time zones and languages in configuration; decide when US is set up.
- Matching the same customer across the Subs, Invest and US portals for cohort reporting: purpose limitation and legal basis across business lines, with Yordi, before it is built.
- Aircall accounts per tenant: one webhook endpoint per account with its own secret on the ingest API; per-number settings checklist per account.
- Roles in prod: Martin `manager` until the morning run of 8 October 2026 sets him `admin` with the runbook's Studio statement (migration 0004 brought the role), Finn `supervisor`; Joshua joins prod as `admin` through the People screen when V1 goes live, and Finn stays `supervisor` until then (Martin's decisions of 7 October 2026, see Decision log). The first Newco agent is added as `agent` (employer `newco`) on People once the conditions for real agent hours in Roadmap step 2 are met; before the gate opens to the domain, an agent passes IAP through an IAP-only grant on `cma-web-backend` (`roles/iap.httpsResourceAccessor`), never through `cma@pulse4all.com`.
- A PR check trigger (typecheck, lint, build and the pure verifiers, no deploy) when a second person commits; `web/verify/**` is out of the deploy trigger since 7 October 2026. A build-only trigger for branch images, so dev verifies a web change before prod has it, belongs to the same step.
- Aggregation of agent data older than 1 year (Decision log, 12 October 2026): an addition that keeps per-team, per-market and cohort figures and removes the link to a person from workdays, status intervals, corrections and deliveries older than 12 months; needed before January 2027, when the first imported days (batch `steam-2026`, from 2 January 2026) pass 12 months.
- Copy language of the Workspace for multilingual Newco agents: English and Dutch exist in `web/src/lib/copy.ts`, default English via `CMA_DEFAULT_LOCALE`; per-campaign copy and theme become tenant configuration (from the Subs agent UI); whether locale becomes a per-user setting (with Kira).
- Forgotten clock-outs: since addition 0008b (12 October 2026) the scheduler ends the day at the published planned shift end, else at the tenant's default time (Pulse4all 17:00), within the workday window, after the grace from the window end (Pulse4all 60 minutes), with a notice to the person and a digest to management, and the day stays flagged for a manager (Decision log, 12 October 2026). Still open: a richer rule once Sync runs (last Aircall or HubSpot activity, or the trial's rule using the last disposition), whether a clock-in after the window end (accepted today, it counts nothing) should be refused (the night of 13 October 2026 proposes refusing it at the window end exactly, as the start is refused before the window start, with the message "The workday ended at {time}"; the grace stays the scheduler's alone; design and question in `docs/night-2026-10-13/NIGHT_NOTES.md`, Part 4), and whether the agent may propose an end time from a dialog, stored as a correction awaiting a supervisor's approval (the demo lets the agent enter it, flagged). The four-eyes step for corrections belongs here: in V1 the person correcting is the approver.
- The employee-monitoring point with Yordi now covers the Live board too: live per-person status to supervisors, managers and analytics, besides the Dashboard's per-person time. Whether analytics (Kira, Peter, Bas) should hold `monitoring.live` is part of the ladder review with Arno and Kira; the board checks the permission, so changing that later is a grant, not code. Raised again by the seventh Gemini audit (13 October 2026): agents are live since 12 October, and Yordi's answers of that day cover processing, informing the agents and retention, not explicitly live per-person status; to confirm with Yordi. Settled for the per-person call figures (14 October 2026, brief B16): Reports → Calls per agent shows each person's calls, talk time and split by counted tag to `performance.team`, sorted by name and never ranked, as Yordi agreed (reported by Martin; Decision log); the Live board's live per-person status stays to confirm. Settled for the per-person email figures (18 October 2026, night run N19-A): Reports → Email per agent (sends, replies, closes and first reply times per person and by hour of day, through the linked accounts) for `performance.team`, as Yordi agreed (reported by Martin; Decision log), switched on for Pulse4all Subscriptions by `reports.per_person` = `email`. Open with Yordi: Orders per agent (first orders credited to a person, Reports → Orders per person), off by the same setting until he confirms it; and the callbacks kept per person on Reports → Follow-up (addition 0008w), shown to `performance.team` like Follow-up's per person. The Live board's live per-person status stays as it is. Since 17 October 2026 this is also the dashboard-versus-cockpit line (What we are building; Users and roles, Role and surface): the Live board is a cockpit screen for the manager and the supervisor, and the open question for analytics is whether Kira, Peter and Bas see it as a dashboard view, the same tiles, strip and rows without the handles (`monitoring.live` granted, no write permission beside it), or not at all (`monitoring.live` not granted, the same signals reaching them through Reports → Follow-up and the Dashboard). Kira, as Head of Call Center, uses live monitoring (Users and roles; KPIs 9), so the answer may differ per person; either way it is a grant in the ladder review with Arno and Kira, not code, and the point with Yordi above stands.
- Attempts per agent for Pulse4all (employee monitoring, with Yordi; addition 0008y): built behind `reports.per_person` holding `attempts`; Pulse4all Subscriptions keeps `email` until Yordi agrees, the dev seed holds `email,orders,attempts`.
- Attempts per deal counts a call to a contact with two open deals as an attempt on both (the page says so): accepted for now, with Kira.
- Invest-line CRM calls counted as attempts in Subscriptions: part of the item on Invest calls in the Subscriptions HubSpot portal (addition 0008f), below.
- An order pipeline left marked as a lead pipeline (every pipeline is a lead pipeline until marked otherwise) still counts in Speed to lead, Follow-up and the other readers of `cma.lead_deals_of` (0007d); Attempts per deal leaves order pipelines out in both scopes. Whether `cma.lead_deals_of` should leave them out too, or the order pipelines are unmarked on Configuration → Sources (Martin, with Kira). How many there are: `docs/attempts-2026-10/RUNBOOK.md` step 6 lists them in prod before `92` (read only); the count found goes here from Martin's report. Locally, on the dev seed with `reports-fixture.sql`: none.
- Which hours the agent's screen shows: decided 7 October 2026, working time plus a Pauses column on My hours, own data only (Decision log); the Pauses column is a small web follow-up. The status flags: the flags and the Subscriptions list were decided on 7 October 2026 (Decision log), a change to them is an update on `work_status`, not a migration. Related: which money column the management screens show. Team hours and the Dashboard show paid; with Pulse4all's flags paid equals billable, and Billable may be the word Arno needs. Proposal: a tenant setting (paid or billable, default paid), settled together with the hours columns when the Steam sample is compared.
- Flag changes with history behind them: the clock, the reports and the exports read a status's flags as stored, so changing `is_paid` or `is_billable` on a status that already has stretches would rewrite past pay and billing figures. The configuration screen of 0005a therefore freezes the flags of a status with time behind it (Decision log, confirmed 9 October 2026): retire the old status and add a new one; flags with a validity stay the alternative if daily use shows the need. Pulse4all decided its flags while prod held only the team's own time.
- Pulse4all Invest's own status list, with Peter, at the copy to Invest (step 10); until then Invest has the universal ladder.
- Spanish (ES) leads: a Spanish language skill and who takes them (Arno).
- Newco agents' HubSpot owner mapping: their Google accounts' local part equal to their Newco address or not (decides the `--also-domain` run or one line per agent).
- App links: the exact HubSpot portal and Aircall addresses per tenant replace the generic `app.hubspot.com` and `dashboard.aircall.io` when Martin provides them, now on the App links page (0005a) rather than in `02`; links for Shopify, NetSuite and the shared mailbox follow as those teams use them.
- Shifts that end after midnight: decided 7 October 2026, none at Pulse4all, the business day ends at midnight as built (Decision log). A `business_day_end` tenant setting stays the option for a tenant with night shifts, with the roster (0005).
- Hours CSV columns and Pulse4all's duration format: provisional (see API, Exports). The Steam sample arrived on 12 October 2026 and holds login and logout sessions only (no statuses, no breaks), so it is loaded as working time (addition 0008d) rather than compared column by column. With the sessions in the CMA the hours comparison is now possible: the hours export over the imported period against Steam's own logged-in time per account; then the parallel run below.
- Roster cells: structured start and end plus absence types with the demo's quick-typing parser, built in the night build of 8 October 2026 (Decision log); confirm the entry method and the absence types with Arno on the first real week. Coverage targets per team, work type and weekday are set on Absences and coverage (0005a); shift templates, swaps and requests, and a server-generated PDF file of the week are later.
- The repository is public until launch (Martin, 8 October 2026, Decision log), so a build chat can read `main` without a zip. While it is public, the README and `records/` name staff (roles, emails, Finn's first clock-in): tell Yordi, and strip or keep as he says. New for Yordi since 0005a: the scheduler ends forgotten days as a system actor and the audit records it; the rosters are staff data (8 October). The fourteenth README pass never reached the repository: PR #39 carries the eleventh pass (943 lines) under the fourteenth's title, so the fifteenth pass replaces the file whole.
- Supervisors and the planner: a supervisor holds `roster.view` (own schedule) and `monitoring.live` (the shift line), not `roster.manage`; whether a supervisor should see the team's planner read-only (a `roster.view_team` permission, the night's unbuilt slice 4), or plan their own team, is a grant question for Arno and Kira, not code.
- Slice 4 of the night build of 9 October 2026: My account is done (9 October, addition 0005b, PR #57); next are shift templates per team (`cma.shift_template`, presets `1` to `5` in the planner, migration 0005c) and a supervisor's read-only planner (`roster.view_team`, granted to `manager` and `admin` by the migration; the supervisor grant is one line in the tenant seed once Arno and Kira say yes, see the line above), each its own pull request. An agent's own teams and skills on Welcome or My day (the demo's third line) is now a link to My account if wanted.
- My account and ids in other systems: the page leaves `app_user_external_id` out, because the sign-in id is stored once and never shown again (the People screen's rule). Since the night of 13 October 2026 the CRM and telephony ids are set on People → Linked accounts by whoever may edit the person (Decision log, 13 October 2026); still open: whether My account shows a person's own linked ids read-only, so an agent can see why an alert did or did not reach them, and the owner and user picker that replaces typing (Decision log, 13 October 2026, names what it needs).
- Urgent messages: read and acknowledged are two facts and the urgent pop-up writes acknowledged (built 11 October 2026); the alert rules have their configuration screen since the night of 13 October 2026 (B14). Still open: quiet hours and Spain's right to disconnect (delivery outside working time). Design and question in `docs/night-2026-10-13/NIGHT_NOTES.md` (Part 4): the proposal is that a person who is not clocked in gets no sound and no pop-up, the message waits in the inbox, and an urgent pop-up shows at their next clock-in; people whose time is not kept (managers, admins) are not quieted; a per-person quiet-hours preference and the work-mobile channel come later. Question for Martin, with Yordi on the Spanish rule: is "not clocked in means quiet" the rule, and does a must-acknowledge broadcast wait for the clock-in too?
- Total Calls Today and other productivity figures: whether agents see colleagues' numbers (gamification), with Arno.
- One-click dial through `POST /v1/users/:id/calls`: verify against a live Aircall account that it works with the embedded Workspace rather than only the desktop Phone app, and the plan coverage; otherwise `dial_number` stays two clicks.
- Setting an agent's Aircall availability per user through the API (the status-to-availability mapping of Features 1): endpoint, plan coverage and the custom statuses Aircall allows (Out for lunch, On a break, In training, Back office, Other); if not possible, mismatch display only.
- Cohort definitions for Pulse4all (entry, country, source, proposition, converting agent or team) and the payment-behaviour measures (days to pay, dunning stages, collections) with Kira and Peter; which system holds the Asset Management Database and how it is reached; the Customer App's event feed (webhooks or API) and how its interactions are attributed to an agent when one is involved.
- Benchmarking agents across employers and countries: what agents see of each other, what the partner's HR sees, retention of per-agent performance history (employee-monitoring rules in NL and Spain, with Yordi).
- HubSpot API rate limits for the desk's write-back and live reads at Newco's volume; static versus OAuth auth of the project app per tenant; search-by-phone limits make the mirror's phone index necessary.
- Result-code seeds for Subs (semantics, priorities, retry intervals, do-not-call flags) with Kira; do-not-call scope across business lines (one boolean on the contact today) with Peter and Kira.
- Callback appointments written back to the CRM as tasks: tenant setting, default on or off.
- Dev identities as system `mock` with a subject switch per request for two-user tests: decided for dev; to revisit if Cloud Run's direct IAP integration lets dev run with real Google identities without a load balancer (check when the first real agent is in dev).
- Parallel run against Steam Connect before Steam is switched off for hours: with the import (0008d) both sides can be compared for the same weeks.
- Legacy time import, staged rows of `steam-2026`: `import_session` holds Steam's account names (`external_person_name`, no reader view) for traceability; clear the staged names after Arno's review of the 108 flagged days (the days and events do not depend on the names).
- Legacy time import and the 1-year rule: the identifiable retention of agent data (Decision log, 12 October 2026) reaches the first imported days (2 January 2026) in January 2027, not October 2027; the aggregation must cover imported days like any other, so it is needed before January 2027.
- Legacy time import, the 108 days flagged for review in batch `steam-2026` (closed by the 17:00 rule or clipped to the window): Arno reviews them on Team hours (needs correction).
- The route for the next legacy import instead of Cloud Shell: a Cloud Storage bucket in europe-west4, or the Cloud SQL Auth Proxy from a trusted machine. Martin's own Chromebook, his terminal since 16 October 2026, is a personal device and not the trusted machine meant here; the bucket route stays preferred.
- Prod admin access from a personal device (16 October 2026): whether Pulse4all's device policy allows prod admin access (gcloud as `martin@pulse4all.com` with `roles/owner`, the Auth Proxy to `cma-prod-pg`, `gh` as Pulse4all-DEV) from Martin's own Chromebook; and whether the Workspace's Google Cloud session control (reauthentication, for example every 16 hours) is set for gcloud and the Console.
- Context-Aware Access and session length on IAP, and 2-Step Verification for the Newco unit (both depend on the Google Workspace edition). 2SV enforcement for the agents' unit to be checked in the Admin console now, the gate is open to the domain since 12 October (fifth audit FIND-06).
- Container vulnerability scanning in Artifact Registry (fifth audit FIND-08): billed per scanned image, one per merge; automatic scanning or an on-demand scan before a release.
- Cloud SQL Data Access logs and pgaudit: scope and cost, decided before more direct readers are added.
- The final name instead of the working title Contactcenter-Management-App, with Mark.
- Build versus buy check: compare the CMA with workforce-management tools that integrate with Aircall (Assembled, Playvox, injixo, Surfboard) and with agent desks on CRM plus telephony, so the choice to build stays checked; HubSpot and Aircall themselves don't cover this layer (their focus is CRM and telephony).
- Business case: confirm the ten targets with Arno, Kira and Peter; the hourly rate per employer and the margin per order for the ROI line come from finance and stay out of the README; who takes and records the baselines, and when the first quarterly review is.
- Assignment tie-breaker and no matching agent: decided 9 October 2026 (Decision log). Still open: a fallback skill or a manager notice when a record waits too long, and the queue engine's "campaign has queued work and no eligible agent" check at campaign creation.
- Routing's language source (S2): the record's market through a market-to-language mapping (Decision log, 9 October 2026) or the contact's own language, now that it is on the contact record; a per-tenant mapping either way.
- Heads-up to Yordi for the intake track: sent 10 October 2026 (`docs/night-2026-10-10/YORDI_HEADS_UP.md`), including Claude querying the reporting views through BigQuery; prod for that waits for his answer. The thin contact record (contact id, country, language, Shopify id) is pseudonymous personal data, and the HubSpot app's contacts scopes can read every contact field even though the ingest requests only those properties.
- Shopify's Email and Phone fields: closed on 14 October 2026 (Decision log): a custom-distribution app gets every customer field automatically, so nothing is selected and no fallback for Email is needed. Shopify withholds the order's own phone, so an order's hash is its customer's phone's (Indirect sales, below).
- Moving the remaining Make scenarios (contact creation from Shopify, quotes) into the CMA, one at a time.
- Aircall's token-in-body authentication (Aircall signs nothing: each webhook request carries the webhook's own token in the JSON body, no HMAC): accepted, with the mitigations built in B9 (13 October 2026; `docs/night-2026-10-10/NIGHT_NOTES.md` §4, DESIGN §6.1 and §6.2): the token compared in constant time (`crypto.timingSafeEqual` on equal-length buffers, another length a failure) with the connection's signing secret before anything but the key lookup touches the database; the opaque connection key in the path as the second factor (an identifier in the URL, never a secret: Decision log, 12 October 2026); HTTPS only (Cloud Run and the load balancer terminate TLS, and `aircall-webhook.mjs` refuses an address that is not `https://…/aircall/<key>`); one webhook per connection, so its token pins a request to one connection (the script refuses a second webhook for the same address); the nightly reconcile watching the webhook (`webhook_id_not_set`, `webhook_missing`, `webhook_token_mismatch`, `webhook_inactive`, `webhook_reactivated`); and the read-back from Aircall's API, so a forged event can only make the CMA read a real call of its own company by id. Still open: asking Aircall for signed webhooks; rotating a token means deleting and creating the webhook (a new token), never by a job.
- Shopify organisations and store plans: the client credentials grant covers only stores in the app's organisation, and some plans withhold protected customer data; checked per store.
- Aircall and Shopify in prod (`docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` phases 8 and 9, 13 October 2026): Aircall's phase needs B9 (`Pulse4all-com/cma#111`) merged and deployed, Shopify's B10 and B10.1 merged, 0008g and 0008h run and the service and jobs deployed; until then only the vendor-side steps run. B9, B10 and B10.1 were merged on 13 October 2026 (`Pulse4all-com/cma#111`, `Pulse4all-com/cma#114`, `Pulse4all-com/cma#115`), and 0008g and 0008h ran in dev and prod the same day (`records/0008g-prod-2026-10-13`, `records/0008h-prod-2026-10-13`). B9 answers the three points that were open for it (Decision log, 13 October 2026): the account id is the company's name (`Pulse4all`), `aircall-webhook.mjs` refuses a second webhook for the same address, and the backfill lists a day at a time at `aircall_max_per_minute`. B10 answered the one open for it (`ingest-outbox` every minute); since B10.1 the job has no schedule, as nothing enqueues a write-back. Not walked through in Pulse4all's accounts: Aircall's API Keys page and the user id in a user's address. Shopify's phase 9 was walked through for the NL store on 14 October 2026 and corrected where it differed (`records/intake-prod-2026-10-14`); the other 13 stores followed the same day, all at once (`PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.6, Several stores at once; same record).
- Indirect sales: an order (Shopify first order, later repeat if Kira decides) is indirect when the person or company was in contact with an agent before it: rules in order (1) the same CRM contact had a lead deal or a call, (2) the same CRM company, (3) the order's phone hash equals an earlier call's; window, counted touches and credit rule as tenant settings (Kira); domain and name matching later. The Self Serve deals are web orders closed as won on creation; once Shopify is connected, orders come from Shopify and the intake tiles count lead pipelines only (Kira). Touches are levelled, in an order Kira sets: conversation (a connected outcome, or answered with at least n seconds of talk, n a tenant setting), quote email, product email (backlog CMA-002), attempt, lead deal only. The credit rule is a tenant setting. First orders are attributed, renewals never, repeat orders if Kira decides. Lifetime value per CRM contact or company sums the linked Shopify customers' amount spent; to decide: currency and Shopify versus NetSuite as the source of value. Sales cycle (contact created to first order) uses a HubSpot property for the original created date of contacts from the old CRM. Retention from renewals is a later KPI. Data quality and lead to order still join through the Shopify id properties and move to the 0008h link with the attribution brief. Built in the night run N19-A (addition 0008u, Data model: first orders) with proposed defaults as tenant settings; for Kira: the window (90 days), the level order and the assisted set, the known-contact minutes (60), the order match (1,440 minutes), whether repeat orders are ever attributed, and the old CRM's created date as a contact property (a `created_at` slot); the company rule and quote or product emails as levels are not built.
- Indirect sales after the NL store's first read (14 October 2026, `records/intake-prod-2026-10-14`): (a) about a third of the NL orders carry no phone hash (339 of 501 orders with one; 911 of 1,874 customers), because the phone sits only in the shipping address, which the adapter never reads; whether to read the shipping address's phone as a fallback when the customer has none, hashed like the others and the address itself dropped, is to decide with Yordi (it widens what the adapter reads, not what it stores). (b) Whether the adapter should stop asking for the fields Shopify withholds (the order's own `phone`), so that Shopify's Dev Dashboard no longer counts those answers as errors (about 51%); the adapter itself already reads a withheld field as empty and fails nothing (Decision log, 14 October 2026).
- Retention of the keyed phone hashes: the commerce hashes of 0008g follow the tenant's retention for call data (1 year), but no retention job exists for call data either (`crm_call` and `telephony_call.counterpart_hash`, `commerce_customer` and `commerce_order.phone_hash`, and the audit log's images of those rows keep them indefinitely today). To build with the aggregation of agent data older than a year (above): clear the hashes older than a year, the audit images included, with Yordi.
- Shopify's `read_all_orders`: not offered when the app was created on 14 October 2026, and the Dev Dashboard has no API access requests page to ask for it (`PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.2); how to get it is open. Without it the API serves the last 60 days of orders, enough for the backfill from 1 October 2026 until about 30 November 2026, after which an older backfill needs the scope. The prior order count follows Shopify's own `numberOfOrders` (whether that counts test orders is not documented).
- Shopify backfill window: customers all (lifetime totals live on the customer); orders the last 13 months; the phone hash only on orders within the 1-year retention; `read_all_orders` if Shopify offers it; Bulk Operations if a store's volume needs it (Kira, Martin).
- Shopify subscriptions are checked once a night: a subscription Shopify deleted after failed deliveries is recreated at the next reconcile, and the 26-hour look-back reads what was missed; a shorter check (the subscription check on its own schedule) if the alerts or intake need the gap closed sooner.
- Lines by hand on the Sources page: a telephony line appears when the adapter reads the line catalog; `set_telephony_number` can add one by its id, the screen does not (no line to type before the catalog exists). Revisit if a line must be configured before its first call.
- Invest's Aircall connection, a later step: its own connection in the Invest tenant on the same Aircall company, with its own webhook (`aircall-webhook.mjs`, its own key and token) and its own lines counted; Aircall sends every call of the company to each webhook, so the Invest connection marks the Subscriptions lines not counted, as Subscriptions does the Invest lines (Decision log, 13 October 2026). A line added later in Aircall arrives counted in every connection until someone switches it off (which then removes what it stored); whether a connection should take new lines as not counted is open.
- A line switched back on gets its earlier calls back only through a read that takes them as new (addition 0008f): the reconcile and the backfill skip a call they listed before with an unchanged fingerprint, also one skipped while its line was off, so the next backfill does not restore them today. A re-read option for the backfill (a run whose events are keyed apart) would; it is an ingest change, left out of 0008f, which changes no adapter.
- The audit images of removed calls (addition 0008f): the deletes of an uncounted line keep ids only, but the earlier insert and update rows of those calls in `cma.audit_log` keep their full images (times, line, user id, keyed hash; the raw payload is filtered); removing those too means rewriting the append-only audit log, so it is left for the audit-retention decision with Yordi. FIND-05 of the fifth audit asks the same; decide with Yordi together with Invest calls in the Subscriptions HubSpot portal, including that stores and business lines are separate legal entities (e.g. Defibswap UK LTD for the UK store).
- Invest calls in the Subscriptions HubSpot portal (addition 0008f): the Aircall–HubSpot integration logs calls on every line of the Aircall company into the Subscriptions portal, so calls on lines that do not count still arrive as CRM calls (`crm_call`) in the Subscriptions tenant; 0008f removes their telephony calls and links but keeps the CRM calls, as HubSpot owns them. In prod on 13 October 2026, all 12 calls on the five Invest lines were linked to such CRM calls. Metrics on CRM calls (attempts, connected rate) still count them, since addition 0008y Attempts per deal and Attempts per agent too, and the call link rate drops by those calls. To decide (Martin, Kira): leave CRM calls as they are and exclude them in reporting by the line of their linked or matched telephony call, or stop the Aircall–HubSpot integration from logging the Invest lines into the Subscriptions portal (an Aircall integration setting per number).
- Aircall tags as a refinement of "connected": a HubSpot call linked to an Aircall call already counts as connected when Aircall reports it answered, but an answered outbound call can still be a voicemail box or a gatekeeper. Option: a per-connection mapping of Aircall tags to meanings (the same shape as `connection_call_outcome`), tenant configuration, never hardcoded. Data prerequisite: agents apply the tags consistently. B9 records tags as tags (no meanings): still to decide, before tags feed "connected".
- An index on `cma.telephony_call (tenant_id, connection_id, number_ref)` with the next telephony migration (fifth audit FIND-01).
- Cloud Armor's rate limits (600 per minute per IP, and 2,400 on `/shopify/` since 13 October 2026) reviewed after the first month of real webhook volume in the prod records and the `cma-ingest 429` alerts.
- HubSpot's call timestamp for calls logged by Aircall: assumed to be the start of the call, verified on the first prod call; the call link supplies the start otherwise.
- Office hours and holidays per market, with Kira (provisional default Mon to Fri 09:00 to 17:00 local).
- Belgium's language (`nl` by default).
- Which forms count as intake, and their markets.
- Renewal source names for Juo: per store at `PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.6, read with `shopify-subscribe.mjs --source-names` before the store's backfill. Every store uses Juo; since 14 October 2026 `commerce.renewal_source_names` = `9548333057,subscription_contract_checkout_one`, `commerce.renewal_app_ids` empty (Decision log; the NL pilot's `9548333057` alone corrected the same day). IE, NO, ES, FI, CH and IT had no orders in the lookup's 60 days (no sales yet): their first renewals are checked with 9.8's source-name query once they sell.
- The commerce match outcome: since 0008h (B10.1) the matched contact is stored per customer (the link on `commerce_customer`); no contact and several contacts clear it and are counted in the adapter's log lines (`match_none`, `match_several`, no ids), not stored. The bridge check of `PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.8 reads the link. Whether to store the reason a customer has no link stays open.
- Data quality (0007d: commerce customers whose id no contact holds, or several hold) and lead to order still join commerce customers to contacts through the contact refs (`crm_contact_ref`, the HubSpot–Shopify app's Contact-Shopify ID properties as the HubSpot connection's `shopify` refs), not through the link of 0008h. Because the CMA holds a contact only while a record points to it, customers without a deal count as unmatched there. Moving those views to the link (or to either) is a later migration; B10.1 changed nothing in 0007b, 0007c or 0007d. In prod on 14 October 2026 (NL store) Data quality counted all 1,874 customers without a contact and lead to order found 0 orders for 189 deals, while the link held 1,830 (`records/intake-prod-2026-10-14`); both stay there until the HubSpot–Shopify app fills Contact-Shopify ID-1/2 or the views move to the link.
- The reporting view `cma_read.commerce_customer` leaves the link of 0008h out (B10.1 changed no 0007b view); adding `crm_connection_id`, `crm_contact_source_id` and `matched_at` to it, for reporting and indirect sales, is a later migration.
- Data prerequisite: the match by id (for a customer without an email, since Shopify no longer withholds Email) and the refs above assume the HubSpot–Shopify app writes the Shopify customer id into the properties the HubSpot connection names as its `shopify` contact refs (Contact-Shopify ID-1 and ID-2 today, `PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 7.3). Which properties the app fills is to be checked on a real contact; if they differ, the refs move to them. Not in place on 14 October 2026: Data quality and lead to order found no NL customer through these properties (the line above).
- The outbox job `ingest-outbox` is deployed without a schedule since B10.1 (`deploy.sh` pauses a schedule an earlier deploy made): nothing enqueues a write-back. Scheduling it again is a `deploy.sh` change that comes with the next action that needs it (0009's owner write-back). Until then the sweep's outbox guard (13 October 2026) warns, and Data quality shows the count, when an action waits ten minutes past its due time.
- The HubSpot app's contacts write scope (`crm.objects.contacts.write`, Decision log, 10 and 11 October 2026) is unused since 0008h; remove it (reinstall in both portals). Dropping it (least privilege) is a change to the `hubspot/` project template, its verifier and a new app version; 0009's owner write-back decides which write scope the app needs next.
- NocoDB visibility of the new intake views (ids only).
- Showing customer data from HubSpot inside the CMA (the offer carries the contact id and opens HubSpot in a new window): belongs to the agent desk (Roadmap step 8) and its heads-up to Yordi, not to the speed-to-lead track.
- Whether an agent who lets a lead expire is automatically set to an "away" status.
- Can one person work for two business lines on the same day, and if so where does the time clock live (today: one user and one clock per tenant).
- Default role ladder and permissions: confirm the seed with Arno and Kira.
- Owner of customer contact details: Shopify or customer portal.
- Shared mailbox: agreement that customer mail goes through HubSpot, which depends on making attachments as easy in HubSpot as in Gmail. Also how to attribute replies to individual agents. Brief B17 reads HubSpot's email engagements and conversations inboxes only: mail that stays in Gmail is not counted, and a reply is attributed to the replying agent's CRM owner id, which the ingest resolves.
- Choice of realtime push service: managed (Pusher Channels, Ably; check EU data residency and pricing) vs self-hosted (Centrifugo on Cloud Run) vs custom WebSocket service. Decide in Roadmap step 6; polling is the fallback.
- HubSpot subscription check for UI extensions and OAuth.
- NetSuite connectivity (Make module vs. direct API).
- Plan B for agents if the CMA is down (today: HubSpot and Aircall as they work now).
- Source of review data per country and how it links to deals.
- Office hours per country, used for speed-to-lead and SLA calculations.
- Handling of GB vulnerability data in Postgres, in CMA alerts and on the desk's GB records (with Yordi).
- Retention of staff data, the audit log and the customer-field mirror; employee monitoring rules in NL and Spain; quiet hours for notifications (with Yordi).
- Cloud SQL's Knowledge Catalog integration (on by default on both instances, shares schema metadata only, not data): keep or switch off (with Yordi).
- Which Aircall AI features (transcripts, sentiment, playbooks) are in the current Aircall plan.
- Tool for the short satisfaction question after a call or ticket.
- An alert on missed calls waiting too long for a callback (addition 0008i built the list, not the alert): a rule type for `cma.message_rule` with a waiting limit per tenant, in a follow-up brief.
- The Aircall call link template: the telephony connection's `record_url_call` setting (for example a template with `{id}`) gives the link icon on the Live board's missed calls; the exact address of a call in Aircall's dashboard is to be verified there before Martin fills it in on Configuration → Sources. Not set at the go-live of 14 October 2026, so the missed calls in prod have no link icon yet.
- Pulse4all's inbound settings, with Kira: confirm the service level's 20 seconds (`telephony.service_level_seconds`), the callback lookback of 3 days (`telephony.callback_lookback_days`) and the 30 minutes a call counts as waiting (`telephony.waiting_max_minutes`), and whether "Hung up within 10 s" (`short_abandoned`) needs a callback: 5 of the first 13 callers listed in prod (14 October 2026) were missed for that reason. The values are tenant settings and the switch is on Configuration → Sources, so the answer needs no code.
- Primary buttons at 40 px: `docs/style/Pulse4all-Style.md` 7.2 asks 48 px for a primary button; the Workspace's 21 primary buttons are 40 px: 20 through the `Button` and `LinkButton` primitives at their default size `md` (7.2's height for secondary and in-table actions), and the period bar's Show button. Either keep 40 px as a deviation for dense operational screens, logged and flagged to Mark per section 14, or move all 21 to 48 px.
- Waiting calls are derived, because Aircall has no queue object: an inbound call not answered and not ended, younger than `telephony.waiting_max_minutes` (30), counts as waiting; a lost end event makes a call stop waiting after that limit. A queue or ringing-state source, if Aircall offers one, would replace the rule.
- Automatic sending of invitations: a sender mailbox (for example `team@pulse4all.com`), Gmail API with domain-wide delegation, or a mail provider; until then the inviter sends the link from their own mail client (Decision log, 15 October 2026).
- The invitation token in request logs (`docs/night-2026-10-15/NIGHT_NOTES.md` question 4; eighth Gemini audit, 3.2): `/invite/<token>` is in the load balancer's and Cloud Run's request logs, which the `_Default` bucket keeps 30 days, while a link works 7 days by default (`auth.invitation_valid_days`, 1 to 30): a logged link works until it expires, and the logs keep it weeks longer; accepted for now because a token works once, expires within days and only for the invited address's Google account; a token in the address's fragment (`#`), which no server log sees, needs the page to post it and IAP to keep a fragment through its sign-in, which is unverified.
- Retention of invitations (`docs/night-2026-10-15/NIGHT_NOTES.md` question 5; eighth Gemini audit, 3.1): rows are never deleted (their audit and who accepted what stay readable), so the address and name of someone who never accepted stay as well, in `cma.invitation` and in the images `cma.audit_log` keeps of its rows; a retention rule (for example, blank the address and name of an expired or revoked invitation after a number of days, keeping the row) must cover those audit images too, and belongs with the staff-data retention rule (with Yordi).
- The HubSpot scope `marketing.email.read` for brief B17e's marketing email aggregates: Yordi's answer of 15 October 2026 covered B17b's `sales-email-read` and `conversations.read`, email metadata and the held contacts (Decision log), not this scope; ask with B17e, before its prod.
- Automated emails from HubSpot workflows (backlog CMA-072): in October 2026 every outbound email the CMA read had `actor_kind` agent (inbox messages 923, their `CONVERSATIONS` copies 922, engagements without an origin 186, `BCC_TO_CRM` 64, `EMAIL_INTEGRATION` 9; `records/n19a-prod-2026-10-19`). HubSpot sends workflow emails as marketing emails, which the CMA does not read, so Reports → Email counts none of them. Whether and how to read them: backlog CMA-072 (compare the scope `marketing.email.read` above).
- Conversation webhooks: the inbox conversations are polled every minute (Decision log, 15 October 2026). Whether a 2026.09 project app can subscribe to conversation events is unproven; if it can, a webhook route could replace the poll's thread listing (the open check and the reconcile would stay). Not before the poll has run a while in prod.
- The overlap of the two email sources: measured in dev on 16 October 2026 (thread 22376276167; `records/b17b-dev-2026-10-16`, step 6). An inbox reply and the incoming email are also logged as `crm_email` engagements, whose thread property (`hs_email_thread_id`) differs from the inbox thread's id; both copies carry `hs_engagement_source` `CONVERSATIONS` (incoming: `hs_object_source` `EMAIL`; outgoing: `CRM_UI`). Decided the same day (Decision log): inbox messages are the source for connected inboxes, and a `crm_email` with `hs_engagement_source` `CONVERSATIONS` is an inbox copy, flagged and left out of reports by brief B17c (the property and its values configurable). Until B17c, no report adds the two sources (they stay apart anyway, Decision log). Built in the night run N19-A (addition 0008t): `email_message.origin` from the connection's `email_origin_property`, the copies named by `email_inbox_copy_origins` (Pulse4all: `CONVERSATIONS`, set by `docs/night-2026-10-19/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`).
- Email engagements deleted in HubSpot are not seen: the poll lists live engagements, and the app subscribes to no email webhook, so a deleted engagement stays counted (a contact's privacy deletion still clears its contact from emails and threads). To decide after a month in prod whether a deletion subscription for emails (unproven in a 2026.09 project, like the conversation webhooks) or a nightly comparison is worth it.
- B17b's data prerequisites, read in dev and prod (`records/b17b-dev-2026-10-16`, step 3a; `records/b17b-prod-2026-10-16`, 10.2): the thread property is `hs_email_thread_id`; `hs_email_status` holds `BOUNCED`, `FAILED`, `SCHEDULED`, `SENDING`, `SENT` and `DRAFT` (`SENDING` and `DRAFT` map to `other`); `hs_email_direction` adds `DRAFT_EMAIL`, which the ingest skips; the actor id prefixes `A` `AGENT`, `V` `VISITOR` and `S` `SYSTEM` are confirmed in prod (11, 66 and 1 actors in 200 threads; `docs/ingest/README.md`, section Email). Still open: the origin property (`email_origin_property` and `email_automated_origins`), which `hs_engagement_source`'s values in prod decide.
- Contacts' country fallbacks after B17b (addition 0008m): done in prod on 16 October 2026 (`records/b17b-prod-2026-10-16`, 10.6): 4,569 contacts take their country from `country_dropdown` (slot 2), 1 from `contact_country`, 176 have none; Kira was told before the re-read. Still open: the HubSpot workflow that fills `contact_country` (KPIs 11). "All set" means removing slot 2 by configuration (`cma.set_connection_field` with an empty property; a slot goes only from the end); the data-quality check `contact_country_fallback` shows how many contacts still depend on it (4,569 for `pulse4all-subscriptions` on 16 October 2026).
- Reopens at a thread's creation: HubSpot records a status change to open by the actor `system` when a thread is created, which the ingest stores as `reopened` (dev, thread 22376276167: `reopened` (`system`) at its creation; prod on 16 October 2026: 1,455 reopens for 1,209 threads). Brief B17c counts a reopen only after a close; built so in addition 0008t (a reopen whose latest status event before it is no close is no opening).
- Which inboxes, channels and, since addition 0008t, mailboxes count, with Arno and Kira (`PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 10.4 left every inbox at the default, counted): in prod on 16 October 2026 `Inbox` (948000000) and `Support` (948001000) hold no threads, `Help Desk` (948000001) holds 1,206 and `Social Workspace` (948000002) 3; and whether the forms channel (1003) counts as workload.
- Whether the Mail mailbox counts (Martin, 19 October 2026): `3796125277`, labelled Mail on Sources, is mail@, Newco's address, and holds internal traffic between Pulse4all and Newco (about 516 received in October 2026). It is still counted, so Reports → Email includes it; not counting it is its Counted switch on Sources, configuration only (`records/n19a-prod-2026-10-19`).
- Pulse4all's email settings, with Arno and Kira: the first reply target of 240 business minutes (`email.reply_target_minutes`), not responding after 5 days (`email.no_response_days`), the market order contact, form, record, inbox (`email.market_order`) and the email history from `intake.start_date` (`email.start_date` empty); `02` sets none of them until confirmed.
- Email's period deltas before the history (19 October 2026, the hand checks on `de12f43`, `records/perf-prod-2026-10-19`): Reports → Email compares a period with a previous one that lies before `intake.start_date`, where the CMA holds almost no email, so its deltas show four-digit growth; First orders says "No history to compare yet" in that case. The fix is to decide.
- Retention of email metadata (0008m): 1 year like call data, inside backlog CMA-003's job, which does not exist yet; the audit log keeps the rows' images (metadata and the filtered raw payload) until it does.
- Dev's connection budget: the ceilings sum to 63 with NocoDB's pools at 3 each (15 October 2026), the email poll of B17b (counted since the IT audit of 17 October 2026) and the follow-up refresh's pool of 2 (18 October 2026) against `db-g1-small`'s 50 (Infrastructure, Database connection budget); dev's real load is far below, so dev's other instances and pools stay as they are.
- `verify:email-flow` against dev and the scheduled email poll (brief B17b.1): while a run lasts, its throwaway connections are active and its `conn1` has `email_poll` on, so dev's scheduled poll (`ingest-poll-email`, every minute) may serve that test connection mid-run. The fixture token is not in dev's Secret Manager, so such a poll writes a failed `email_poll` run on it, which can make A1 (one `email_poll` run) or B3 (the run of the 429) fail on a rare run. It never touches another tenant: the scheduled poll writes only into the run's own throwaway tenant, and guard I1 proves the run's own logins wrote nothing outside its two tenants. When A1 or B3 fails and every other check passes, rerun the verifier.
- Keyboard hints hidden by default after a person's first week (16 October 2026): today the hints follow the browser's cookie `cma-hints` and are shown by default; hiding them once a person knows the keys needs a per-person preference row (with the locale preference above), not a cookie.
- A shortcut letter for the Preview as select (16 October 2026): none tonight; the select pauses the shortcuts while focused like any select, and the free letters are few.
- Dropping the 80th percentile from the SQL functions (`cma.speed_to_lead_summary`, `cma.inbound_service`) and the JSON API (`p80BusinessMinutes`, `p80AnswerSeconds`) in a later migration, now that the screens no longer show it (Decision log, 15 October 2026); until then the API returns it.
- Alert rules on tasks and on tickets against their response time (16 October 2026, N16-B): a task assigned to a person, a task overdue, a ticket at risk or over its response time could raise a message as the record and assignment rules do (Feature 7); not built with 0008n and 0008o. To decide with Arno which of them agents want pushed, and whether a task's owner change is an assignment.
- Callbacks kept (KPI 5; 16 October 2026): the task types marked `is_callback` and `tasks.callback_window_minutes` are in place (0008n, 0008o); since the night run N19-A (addition 0008w) the figure is computed with the definition proposed here (a call with the task's contact, in either direction, starting within the window around the due time; a task completed without a call not kept), still for Kira to confirm: changing it is a new migration of `cma.callbacks_kept_of`, the screens stay.
- Hiding the keyboard hints by default after a person's first week (16 October 2026, with the preference row above) and a shortcut letter for the Preview as select: still open after N16-B; the free letters are fewer still, now that Follow-up takes K, O and S on its own page.
- HubSpot's SLA properties feeding `connection_pipeline.sla_minutes` (16 October 2026): the response time is the Workspace's own setting per tenant and per ticket pipeline; whether HubSpot's own SLA configuration (where a portal has it) should fill the pipeline's value through the pipeline catalog read is to decide once the live portal's SLAs are known (Data first).
- A task's link (16 October 2026, N16-B): HubSpot has no record page for a task. `https://app-eu1.hubspot.com/contacts/{account}/record/0-27/{id}` answers 404 and a task opens only as a preview on the task list (Martin's check, N16-B morning runbook step 9), so `record_url_task` cannot be set for a HubSpot connection and the task rows of Follow-up and Waiting for you have no link (Decision log, 17 October 2026). Option: link a task row to its deal, ticket or contact, which the row holds by id (0008n): the deal and the ticket through `record_url_deal` and `record_url_ticket`, a contact through a template that does not exist yet; to decide with Arno and Kira which record an agent should land on, and in which order when a task has more than one. The Sources hint for `record_url_task` stays, for a CRM whose tasks have a page of their own.
- Task types as a catalog scoped across connections (16 October 2026): `connection_task_type` is per connection, like the call outcomes; a tenant with two CRM connections configures a type twice. A scoped catalog (tenant, type) would need the same decision as the result codes (KPIs 5).
- Data prerequisite: the task webhooks (16 October 2026; closes with CMA-067). Until the new app versions with the nine task subscriptions are uploaded and installed in both portals, tasks reach the CMA only through the backfill (`--object task`) and the nightly reconcile: a change to a task appears the next morning (03:30, the reconcile's 26-hour look-back), and a task deleted in HubSpot is never seen (a search lists live objects only), so it stays in the CMA, and an open one keeps counting. Until then Reports → Follow-up's tasks, Waiting for you and Welcome's tasks overdue are incomplete (Decision log, 16 October 2026: accepted). Closes with CMA-067: the app version, its install and a test task in each portal (`docs/night-2026-10-16/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` step 6, `docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 11.2).
- A dev-only web build path (16 October 2026, backlog CMA-068): `cloudbuild.yaml` always ends in its deploy step, which rolls `cma-web` in the project it runs in, and has no switch to skip it, so a branch's web image cannot be built without deploying it; dev runs the image prod's build made (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` section 4), so a branch is seen in dev only after its merge (N16-B's dev hand check moved after the merge for that reason). A build that only builds and pushes an image, where it is tagged and who may start it, is to decide; a change to `cloudbuild.yaml`, its substitutions or the trigger is named in its pull request.
- HubSpot hygiene of the forms (brief B17d's data, 16 October 2026): the NL Info form's label for VVE, Switzerland missing from 13 of the forms' country dropdowns, and the cancellation reason sets that differ between forms (DK and the general form add Other). The CMA reports the option values as HubSpot holds them; fixing them is HubSpot configuration, with the forms' owner. Since B17d's prod run (17 October 2026, Decision log): a country question (`country_dropdown`) on the four forms without a market (Cancellation Form Helpdesk, Request a Replacement Device Form, General support form Helpdesk, Quote Form), so their submissions get their market from the form, not from the contact (a data prerequisite; then `cma.set_form_market_field()`); and whether the generic Quote Form, counted without a market and never submitted, is still in use (Arno).
- HubSpot hygiene of the mail@ mailbox (19 October 2026): internal mail between Pulse4all and Newco sent to mail@ becomes tickets in the Support Pipeline; fixing that is HubSpot configuration, with Arno and Kira.
- A lookback bound on Follow-up's overdue tasks (18 October 2026, addition 0008s): not set, because it changes the KPI's meaning (Decision log); an option for Kira, as a tenant setting if chosen.
- Campaign monitoring (night run N19-A, addition 0008v): the FCA journey's step list (Gemma and Kira, checked by Yordi); OPC's and Exchange's steps and population (Kira); the customer drill-down's dedicated permission and access log, and whether per-customer data may be shown at all (Yordi); the design links (Martin sets them with `cma.set_journey`). No journey step, population or evaluator is built.
- The forms probe (`forms_poll_probe`, 18 October 2026, backlog CMA-069): built and off. Switched on, one contacts search a minute (`recent_conversion_date` since the last full read) decides whether the counted forms are read that minute; without a conversion they wait for their own cadence slot. A submission without an email address creates no contact and no conversion, so the search never sees it: it arrives at its form's slot, at the latest one interval later, and the nightly reconcile is the net under that. Whether to switch it on after measuring the calls without it (`docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md`, API budget (CMA-069)).
- HubSpot's daily-limit headers (18 October 2026, backlog CMA-069): settled on 19 October 2026, they arrive. In dev and in prod the run lines' `daily` holds the limit, 1,000,000 (prod 967,340 remaining at the reading after N19-B's first hour; `docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 12.2, `records/n19b-prod-2026-10-19`), so the floor guard on backfill and reconcile reads them and can pause.
